One gazette row (Grasim Century Rayon, TXTOE127MH) prints baseline GEI as '.94244' against a 9.3345 target, which is probably a typo. Validated, lineage-linked data is a real differentiator here. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/CCTS-745/blob/main/CCTS_Manuscript_V2.pdf), Repo last commit 2026-08-07)
- **[high]** CCTS offset mechanism (CEEW issue brief, Aug 2025): - Non-obligated entities can register projects under government-set sectoral methodologies and be issued CCCs. - The first set has 8 methodologies: two each for energy, industry, and waste handling & disposal, and one each for agriculture and afforestation/reforestation. Transport methodologies were still awaited. - CEEW recommends quarterly briefings to NSCICM, including annual performance reviews of Accredited Carbon Verification Agencies (ACVAs) and VVBs. - India issued 278 million VCM credits between 2010 and 2022, 17% of global supply. - Indian AFOLU projects average 1,689 days to register on Verra, against 623 days elsewhere in Asia. ([source](https://ceew.in/sites/default/files/voluntary-carbon-offset-mechanism-and-challenges-in-carbon-credit-trading-scheme-market-for-india.pdf), 2025-08 (read from the copy at https://github.com/herrrickshaw/eu-ets-compliance-platform/blob/main/docs/references/ceew_unlocking_indias_voluntary_carbon_market_2025.pdf))
- **[medium]** Indian Carbon Market portal. The earlier brand research (PIB) says the portal launched at Prakriti 2026 on 21 Mar 2026, and that 208 entities were added on 13 Jan 2026, bringing the total to 490. The 13 Jan date and the 282 + 208 = 490 count match the gazette. I could not reopen the PIB page because the proxy blocked it. ([source](https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2243377&reg=3&lang=1), 2026-03 (via the earlier brand-context research))
- **[low]** Trading start date and price band: NOT VERIFIED. A secondary article said the first CCC trade was expected around Oct 2026. Public modelling tools assume a 'CERC (Terms and Conditions for Purchase and Sale of CCCs) Regulations, 2026' but use conflicting price assumptions: one uses a ₹500 floor and ₹1,500 forbearance price, another uses ₹150/₹250/₹600 'collar' scenarios. I found no primary confirmation of any official floor or ceiling. Buyers clearly want a reliable answer on CCC price, timing and exchanges. ([source](https://github.com/SaChIn5419/INDIA-CCTS-CARBON-RISK-ENGINE/blob/main/config/ccts.py), Repo updated 2026-08-28; see also https://www.saurenergy.com/solar-energy-news/indias-carbon-market-edges-toward-its-first-trade-12482615 and https://github.com/sneha-tatiwala/carbon-intelligence-india/blob/main/pitch-note.md)
- **[low]** Article 6, PAT and the voluntary market (secondary only). A Sept 2026 research log says: - India signed a Joint Crediting Mechanism (JCM) Memorandum of Cooperation with Japan in Aug 2025. It calls this India's first live Article 6.2 bilateral framework, with no published volume. - India's Article 6.2 eligible list has 13 activities (Feb 2023), including RE with storage, green hydrogen, green ammonia, offshore wind, CCUS and SAF. - Fertiliser status was contested across sources, and the steel draft was criticised as weak by Climate Risk Horizons. - BEE's PAT page shows 25.78 Mtoe of cumulative savings. - PIB reports 97.01 MtCO2 avoided in PAT cycles I-II only. I did not open any of the primary sources. ([source](https://github.com/herrrickshaw/eu-ets-compliance-platform/blob/main/docs/INDIA_CCTS_SOURCES.md), Log dated Sept 2026; repo last commit 2026-09-19)
- **[medium]** CBAM overlap. Three CCTS sectors are also CBAM goods: aluminium (16 entities), cement (186) and iron & steel (255, draft). That makes up to 457 of the 745 CCTS entities potentially subject to both regimes if they export to the EU. CBAM's definitive period began on 1 Jan 2026. Certificate sales start on 1 Feb 2027, and the first declaration for 2026 imports is due by 30 Sep 2027 (EC dates via brand research). NOT VERIFIED: whether or how CCC costs could count as a 'carbon price effectively paid' and be deducted under CBAM. ([source](https://taxation-customs.ec.europa.eu/news/reminder-cbam-goes-live-1-january-2026-2025-12-23_en), 2025-12-23 (EC); entity counts computed 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Demand proxy (estimate, no search volumes measured). GitHub repository search for 'CCTS india' returns 13 public repos, most updated in 2026. They include CCTS compliance calculators, risk engines, an MRV command centre, a JSW Steel optimisation case and pitch notes to Big-4 advisory teams. Questions these tools surface for practitioners include: 'How does BEE set compliance targets under CCTS?', 'What is India's MRV capacity gap?', 'How does CCTS differ from PAT?', 'What is the ESCert overhang risk for CCTS prices?' and 'What happens under Article 6 ITMO framework for India?'. That points to strong advisor-side interest. ([source](https://github.com/search?q=CCTS+india&type=repositories), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Competitive SERP context (from the brand research, not re-run in this session): - Oren, Climate Decode (TerraNova), CarbonNeeti and Green Curve rank for 'CCTS compliance software India'. - Climes, Bharat Carbon and Sentra rank for India carbon-accounting queries. - ESG Astraa's only non-brand ranking was about position 8, for a timely 'Indian Carbon Market portal launched 2026 CCTS' news query. Timely, fact-dense regulatory explainers are ESG Astraa's only proven organic entry point, and answer engines extracted its CCTS post accurately. ([source](https://esgastraa.com/insights/blogs/2), Brand research as of 2026-10-01)

### cbam-exports (44 searches run)

- **[high]** ACCESS LIMITS. WebSearch had already used up its 200-call session budget before this run, Firecrawl returned 'insufficient credits', and the egress proxy blocked every regulator and news domain I tried (taxation-customs.ec.europa.eu, eur-lex, consilium, gov.uk, pib.gov.in, beeindia, commerce.gov.in, policy.trade.ec.europa.eu, carbonbrief, business-standard, ieefa, gtri, competitor sites, Google Trends/autocomplete). Only GitHub was reachable. So every fact below comes from: (a) GitHub mirrors of EU legal texts (legalize-dev/legalize-eu), (b) dated GitHub knowledge bases and news archives that quote Economic Times (ET) and PIB URLs, or (c) ESG Astraa's own product brief. Confidence ratings reflect this. None of the primary pages were opened directly, and no search volumes were measured. ([source](https://github.com/legalize-dev/legalize-eu), 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** Regulation (EU) 2025/2083 (the CBAM Omnibus simplification), dated 8 Oct 2025, creates a single cumulative de minimis threshold of 50 tonnes net mass per importer per year for iron/steel, aluminium, fertilisers and cement. Electricity and hydrogen are excluded from it. An importer that crosses the threshold must be an authorised CBAM declarant and reports all of that year's imports. The threshold is designed to keep about 99% of embedded emissions in scope. It entered into force on the third day after publication (OJ 17 Oct 2025, per the transcription; ELI http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2025/2083/oj). ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/legalize-dev/legalize-eu/main/eu/32025R2083.md), Oct 2025, in force)
- **[high]** Authorised declarant transition: importers and indirect customs representatives that applied for authorisation by 31 March 2026 could keep importing in 2026 above the 50 t threshold while their application was pending. Importing above the threshold without authorisation triggers sanctions (new Art. 26(2a)). Splitting shipments to stay under the threshold is defined as evasion (Art. 27). Implementing Reg. (EU) 2025/2549 of 10 Dec 2025 simplified the declarant application procedures. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/morellid/skill-per-ingegneri/main/skills/cbam-declarant-trimestrale/references/fonti/reg-ue-2025-2083-cbam-omnibus.md), Oct-Dec 2025)
- **[high]** Money timeline: Member States start selling CBAM certificates on 1 Feb 2027 for 2026 imports. The annual declaration and surrender are due by 30 Sep of the year after import, so the first deadline is 30 Sep 2027. From 2027, declarants must hold certificates covering at least 50% of embedded emissions at each quarter-end (cut from 80%). ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/legalize-dev/legalize-eu/main/eu/32025R2083.md), Oct 2025)
- **[medium]** Certificate price (Implementing Reg. (EU) 2025/2548 of 10 Dec 2025): for 2026 it is the quarterly average of EU ETS auction clearing prices, published the week after the quarter is calculated. From 2027 it becomes a weekly average. A tracker reports Q1 2026 at EUR 75.36 (published 7 Apr 2026) and Q2 2026 at EUR 75.28 (published 6 Jul 2026), so the Q3 2026 price is due in early October 2026. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ets-overview/free-allocation-benchmarks/main/cbam.html), Jul 2026)
- **[high]** Verification rules: after 2025/2083, only actual values are verified; default values need no verification. Implementing Reg. (EU) 2025/2546 of 10 Dec 2025, applicable from 1 Jan 2026, requires a physical site visit to the installation in the first verified year and at least every two years after that. Virtual visits are allowed from year two under low-risk criteria. Materiality is 5% of total specific embedded emissions per good (CN code), and the report uses a Commission template in the CBAM Registry. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/legalize-dev/legalize-eu/main/eu/32025R2546.md), Dec 2025)
- **[high]** Verifier accreditation (Delegated Reg. (EU) 2025/2551 of 20 Nov 2025): EU national accreditation bodies accredit CBAM verifiers. A third-country applicant, such as an Indian verifier, may apply to any EU national accreditation body. Accredited verifiers are registered in the CBAM registry. Separately, Implementing Reg. (EU) 2025/2550 gives registry access to 'independent persons' for certification reports no earlier than 1 Dec 2026. I infer these relate to certifying carbon price paid. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/legalize-dev/legalize-eu/main/eu/32025R2551.md), Nov-Dec 2025)
- **[medium]** Default values: Implementing Reg. (EU) 2025/2621 of 16 Dec 2025 applies from 1 Jan 2026 and must be revised by Dec 2027 at the latest. Implementing Reg. (EU) 2026/1740, adopted 20 Jul 2026 (OJ 31 Jul 2026), replaced its Annex I, and DG TAXUD published Excel v2 on 6 Aug 2026. Mark-ups on default values: 10% (2026), 20% (2027) and 30% (2028 onward) for cement, iron and steel, aluminium and hydrogen; 1% for fertilisers. Default values cannot be combined with a carbon-price deduction except through Commission-set default carbon prices. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Keremozdemirra/cbam-mcp/main/README.md), Aug 2026)
- **[medium]** India-specific default values, as transcribed from the 2026/1740 workbook by a third-party India CBAM calculator (base tCO2e/t, then 2026 marked-up value): hot-rolled flat steel CN 7208: 4.28, marked up 4.708; semi-finished 7207: 4.27; pig iron 7201: 2.53; unwrought aluminium 7601: 1.87 (direct only); grey Portland cement 2523 29: 1.48; urea: 2.22; ammonia: 0.99; nitric acid: 2.01; hydrogen 2804 10: 14.03, marked up 15.433. Another repo says 2026/1740 has 256 India CN-code rows. Not checked against the EU primary file. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Meetv9/ecosetu.cbam/main/data/india_cbam_defaults.csv), Aug-Sep 2026)
- **[medium]** Free allocation phase-out (CBAM factor): 97.5% in 2026, 95% in 2027, 90% in 2028, 77.5% in 2029, 51.5% in 2030, 39% in 2031, 26.5% in 2032, 14% in 2033, 0 in 2034. Only about 2.5% of the benchmark-adjusted obligation bites in 2026, and it rises steeply after 2028. The free-allocation adjustment methodology is IR (EU) 2025/2620. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ets-overview/free-allocation-benchmarks/main/cbam.html), 2026)
- **[medium]** Downstream extension: proposal COM(2025) 989 was tabled 17 Dec 2025 (procedure 2025/0419(COD)). It would cover about 180 steel- and aluminium-intensive downstream products (fasteners, wire, springs, household articles) from 1 Jan 2028, plus anti-avoidance rules including on the pre-consumer 'scrap loophole'. A companion Temporary Decarbonisation Fund was proposed as COM(2025) 990. The European Parliament adopted its first-reading position on 15 Sep 2026 (464-50-159). Trilogues are pending, so it is not yet law. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/victorsole/brubru/main/backend/knowledge_base/guides/cbam_downstream_goods_extension.md), Sep 2026)
- **[medium]** UK CBAM starts 1 Jan 2027. The Oct 2024 consultation response confirmed aluminium, cement, fertilisers, hydrogen and iron & steel, and dropped glass and ceramics. Secondary notes (Aug 2026) mention a GBP 50,000 12-month de minimis, no transitional phase, a first return due 31 May 2028, and electricity excluded. These details were not verified on gov.uk, which was blocked. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/EUClimateAdvisoryBoard/ESABCCMethodHub/main/beta/modules/cbam-leakage-watch/data.generated.ts), Oct 2024 to Aug 2026)
- **[medium]** The UK recognises India's CCTS: Economic Times reports (8 Sep 2026 and 10 Sep 2026, quoting the Commerce Secretary) that India's carbon price will be accepted for carbon price relief under the UK CBAM. ET URLs: https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/economy/foreign-trade/uk-recognises-indias-carbon-credit-trading-scheme-relief-for-indian-exporters/articleshow/133890963.cms and .../uk-recognises-indias-ccts-carbon-price-to-be-accepted-under-cbam-regime-comm-secy/articleshow/133966978.cms ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/abhishekfolder-spec/my-news/main/docs/archive/2026-09-09.html), Sep 2026)
- **[medium]** India-EU FTA: concluded 27 Jan 2026. ET reported on 29-31 Jul 2026 that it includes a separate CBAM annexure and a 'comprehensive work plan', with engagement on carbon prices paid in India and support for SMEs (ET articleshow 132748746, 132736801, 132735330). An EU trade-policy knowledge base says the Commission proposed signature to the Council on 11 Sep 2026 and that the deal was still 'concluded but not signed' in mid-Sep 2026. Sources conflict on whether it has been signed. Either way, CBAM continues to apply alongside the FTA. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/abhishekfolder-spec/my-news/main/docs/archive/2026-08-01.html), Jul-Sep 2026)
- **[medium]** Indian government activity: the Department of Commerce held an EU CBAM awareness session on 18 Aug 2026 for about 100 exporters, covering obligations, embedded-emissions calculation, data collection, verification, and steel and aluminium case studies (PIB PRID 2301032: https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2301032&reg=48&lang=1). On 27-29 Sep 2026, India reserved third-party rights in Russia's WTO dispute against EU CBAM (ET articleshow 134488568, 134548994). ET on 28 Sep 2026 reported 'India's bid to keep carbon cash home' via CCTS (articleshow 134531752). ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Ankitbuilder/upsc-current-affairs-pipeline/main/data/2026-08-19.json), Aug-Sep 2026)
- **[medium]** MSME supply-chain pressure: ET (6-8 Sep 2026) reports that engineering goods exporters want the government to mandate accredited carbon-emission data from raw-material suppliers, and that about 70% of India's engineering exports to the EU come from MSMEs (ET articleshow 133830040). A Feb 2026 news digest says the Export Promotion Mission will partly fund compliance costs for rules such as CBAM. The digest gives the outlay as Rs 22,060 crore; that figure was not verified. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/abhishekfolder-spec/my-news/main/docs/archive/2026-09-08.html), Feb-Sep 2026)
- **[medium]** CCTS has no traded compliance price as of an Aug 2026 compilation, which weakens any EU Article 9 deduction today. The consolidated CBAM text lets the Commission set default carbon prices for third countries from 2027. Recitals to 2025/2083 limit deductions to carbon price 'effectively paid' and to actual (not default) values. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/EUClimateAdvisoryBoard/ESABCCMethodHub/main/beta/modules/trade-partner-climate/data.generated.ts), Aug 2026)
- **[medium]** Steel exporters face a second barrier: the EU measure that replaced the steel safeguard has applied since 30 Jun 2026 (political agreement 14 Apr 2026). It tightens TRQs, adds country-specific quotas, and raises out-of-quota duties to up to 50%. A third-party JSW case study claims a 9.5% anti-dumping duty on Indian cold-rolled steel from 6 Aug 2026, but the EU trade-defence knowledge base mentions only expiry reviews (15 Sep 2026) of stainless cold-rolled measures on India. Treat the anti-dumping claim as unverified. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/victorsole/brubru/main/backend/knowledge_base/guides/eu_trade_defence.md), Jun-Sep 2026)
- **[low]** An illustrative third-party analysis of JSW cold-rolled exports uses BRSR FY2025-26 Scope 1 data (about 2.06 tCO2e/t crude steel), a 1.370 BF-BOF benchmark, the 97.5% factor and the EUR 75.36 Q1 price. It finds a 2026 CBAM cost of EUR 54.59/t with actual data against EUR 106.58/t with an illustrative default, a 48.8% saving. Its default assumption (2.5 + 10%) is lower than the India HRC default reported above (4.28), so the real gap may be larger. This is not an authoritative source. ([source](https://github.com/laharidumpala/CBAM-and-Anti-Dumping-Exposure-for-Indian-Cold-Rolled-Steel-Exports-A-JSW-Steel-Case-Study), Aug 2026)
- **[medium]** PCF/EPD demand is driven mostly by buyers, because the regulatory triggers are deferred. CPR 2024/3110 GWP (EN 15804) declaration is indicative for 2029, pending a delegated act. The EU Battery Regulation carbon-footprint declaration is pending its delegated acts, though the battery passport is mandatory from 18 Feb 2027. No ESPR delegated act has been adopted for any product group, although the first ESPR working plan (16 Apr 2025) prioritises iron & steel and aluminium. White papers should not claim ISO 14067 is legally mandated for Indian exporters today. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/malinoto/tracepass-dpp-schemas/main/instruments.json), 2026)
- **[medium]** Free 'India CBAM calculator' tools are multiplying, which signals that generic CBAM cost and calculator content is commoditising. GitHub repos created Jul-Sep 2026 include an SEE calculator with India defaults, an MSME PCF estimator, India-EU supplier carbon ratings, a CBAM+CCTS platform prototype and an LCA tool for Indian metals. Acquis Compliance also publishes an India CBAM cost dashboard, and a solo SaaS (BLARAA) claims CBAM+CCTS+RCO coverage. ([source](https://github.com/vasu639/Carbon_bridge), Jul-Sep 2026)
- **[low]** ESG Astraa's own competitive research (unverified internal claims, no URLs in the doc) says: no product reconciles CCTS GEI (BEE method, financial year, gate-to-gate) with CBAM embedded emissions (EU method, calendar year, includes precursors). It also claims: Indian unwrought aluminium exports to the EU fell 41.7% year on year to Jan 2026; GTRI estimates 15-22% price cuts; more than EUR 6bn of Indian exports are CBAM-covered; Indian verifiers gained EU CBAM registry access in Sep 2026; and 255 iron & steel units were in a June 2026 CCTS draft. Re-source every figure before publishing. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool/master/docs/08-competitive-gaps.md), 2026)

### global-disclosure (52 searches run)

- **[high]** METHOD NOTE (read first): WebSearch returned 'budget exhausted (200/200)' on the first call. Firecrawl search and scrape returned 402 (insufficient credits). The egress proxy blocked WebFetch to every regulator and standard-setter host tried: ifrs.org, efrag.org, eur-lex, consilium, sebi.gov.in, icai.org, globalreporting.org, sciencebasedtargets.org, ghgprotocol.org, cdp.net, plus search engines and Wikipedia. Only GitHub was reachable. So discovery used about 52 GitHub code searches, and facts come from (a) GitHub-hosted mirrors, scrapes and articles that quote primary sources with dates, and (b) primary datasets mirrored on GitHub (the GHG Protocol Scope 2 raw feedback file; GRI standard texts). Primary-source URLs named below were taken from those documents; I did not open them myself. To fix: add Firecrawl credits, raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION, or allow the hosts above in the environment's network settings. No search volumes were measured. ([source](https://github.com/jessicacohen554-cyber/scope-2-consult), 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** CSRD Omnibus I is now law. Directive (EU) 2026/470 was published in the Official Journal on 26 Feb 2026 and entered into force on 18 Mar 2026. Mandatory CSRD now applies only to undertakings or groups with more than 1,000 employees (average) AND more than EUR 450m net turnover, for financial years starting on or after 1 Jan 2027. Listed SMEs are removed from scope. Member States must transpose by 19 Mar 2027. The Council signed off on 24 Feb 2026. Several independent secondary sources agree: syntropyearth Sep 2026; firatbarca; brussels-governance-monitor; DreamLab knowledge graph. ([source](https://syntropyearth.com/thinking/csrd-omnibus-non-eu-parent-health-disclosure/), Article 2026-09-11, modified 2026-09-24; cites https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/?uri=CELEX%3A32026L0470)
- **[medium]** Non-EU (third-country) parent test under 2026/470: EU net turnover above EUR 450m (previously EUR 150m) AND an EU subsidiary or branch above EUR 200m. The first reporting year is financial years starting on or after 1 Jan 2028. The separate standard for non-EU groups (NESRS) is still out for consultation, with EFRAG technical advice due by end-January 2027. This is the provision that reaches Indian groups with EU subsidiaries. Separately, an EU subsidiary that itself exceeds 1,000 employees and EUR 450m reports from FY2027. ([source](https://syntropyearth.com/thinking/csrd-omnibus-non-eu-parent-health-disclosure/), 2026-09-24; cites https://www.efrag.org/en/news-and-calendar/news/efrag-resumed-work-on-the-european-sustainability-reporting-standard-for-noneu-groups-and-launches)
- **[medium]** Value-chain cap: undertakings with up to 1,000 employees can decline CSRD data requests that go beyond the voluntary (VSME-based) standard. The cap does NOT protect subsidiaries inside the reporting group's consolidation boundary, so Indian subsidiaries of EU groups still get full intragroup data requests. ([source](https://github.com/firatbarca/personal/blob/5c4a2b27ed5d4ebede2c50cd9becb34505331f26/src/posts/csrd-who-reports-what-changes.md), 2026 (post-Directive 2026/470); corroborated by the syntropyearth article of 2026-09-24)
- **[medium]** Revised (simplified) ESRS: the Commission adopted the delegated act and the voluntary standard on 3 Jul 2026. They apply to financial years from 1 Jan 2027, and FY2026 early use is allowed once the act enters into force. Mandatory datapoints fall by about 61% (roughly 1,073 to roughly 320, per secondary sources). Reasonable assurance is dropped; limited assurance remains. EFRAG's Knowledge Hub was updated with the 2026 text (reported by IAS Plus, 29 Jul 2026). The 'Stop-the-clock' Directive (EU) 2025/794 had earlier postponed waves 2 and 3 by two years. ([source](https://finance.ec.europa.eu/news/commission-adopts-revised-sustainability-reporting-standards-2026-07-03_en), 2026-07-03 adoption; IAS Plus feed 2026-07-29 (https://github.com/pauliusgin/curated-news/blob/20cf26e81bfd2b6e0b80ab3b03d8ab42b839e14f/testdata/feeds/ias-plus.json). The 61% figure comes from https://github.com/DreamLab-AI/knowledgeGraph (secondary).)
- **[medium]** CSDDD after 2026/470 covers EU companies with more than 5,000 employees and more than EUR 1.5bn worldwide turnover, and non-EU companies with at least EUR 1.5bn EU turnover. Transposition is due 26 Jul 2028 and application starts 26 Jul 2029. For Indian suppliers, direct due-diligence pressure is therefore deferred and limited to very large buyers. ([source](https://github.com/kang9307/lotto-generator/blob/4314f26afa9fe2cc58ea182b81449615cdad8fb4/en/posts/korea_esg_corporate_trends.html), 2026 (post-July 2026 update); also https://www.clearygottlieb.com/news-and-insights/publication-listing/climate-energy-eu-policy-regulation-update-2026-04-01)
- **[medium]** The ISSB issued 'Amendments to Greenhouse Gas Emissions Disclosures' (IFRS S2) in December 2025, effective for annual periods starting on or after 1 Jan 2027, with early application allowed. The main changes are relief letting financial institutions limit Category 15 to financed emissions and exclude derivatives, plus GHG Protocol and GWP measurement relief. In July 2026 the ISSB proposed a matching Sustainability Disclosure Taxonomy update, with comments due 28 Sep 2026. ([source](https://www.ifrs.org/content/dam/ifrs/publications/amendments/english/2025/issb-2025-1-amendments-ifrs-s2.pdf), 2025-12 amendment; taxonomy proposal 2026-07-29 (https://www.ifrs.org/news-and-events/news/2026/07/issb-proposes-update-ifrs-sustainability-disclosure-taxonomy/). Read via https://github.com/Y2IN/move_ai_challenge/blob/c849383e8649e13f2f3736c7b9ab33b59db49608/docs/SOURCES.md)
- **[medium]** India has NOT adopted ISSB standards, as far as accessible evidence shows. A 2026 country-by-country disclosure map lists India's regime as SEBI BRSR (mandatory for the top 1,000 listed companies since FY2022-23) plus BRSR Core, with no ISSB adoption. ICAI's CA Final study material for May 2027 teaches IFRS S1/S2 but names no Indian roadmap. ICAI's SSAE 3000 is the assurance standard used for BRSR Core. I could NOT verify any SEBI, MCA or ICAI ISSB-convergence decision, because sebi.gov.in and icai.org were blocked. Treat this as 'no evidence found', not a confirmed absence. ([source](https://github.com/duncanhill1/climate-disclosure-map/blob/201fde9ded88a2b057160c2abd5d750723fdef51/data/disclosures.from-excel.json), Dataset 2026; SEBI sources it cites: https://www.sebi.gov.in/legal/circulars/jul-2023/brsr-core-framework-for-assurance-and-esg-disclosures-for-value-chain_73854.html)
- **[medium]** APAC peers are moving to ISSB-based mandates: - Japan: FSA rules promulgated 20 Feb 2026 require SSBJ standards for Prime companies with 5-year average market cap of JPY 3tn or more from FY ending March 2027, and JPY 1tn or more from March 2028. - Korea: FSC final plan of 8 Jul 2026 makes KSSB disclosure mandatory in 2028 (FY2027) for KOSPI firms with KRW 10tn+ assets; Scope 3 deferred 3 years; assurance from 2030. - Malaysia: climate-first reporting from 2025/2026/2027 by group. - Pakistan: SECP phase I from 1 Jul 2025. - Sri Lanka: SLFRS S1/S2 mandatory for the top 100 from 2025. - Taiwan: from FY2026. - China: MoF Basic Standard (Nov 2024). ([source](https://github.com/jasonhnd/finwiki/blob/0cb154c73ee2880d7a83fdf9066be8aea853eabb/finance/esg-sustainability-cross-domain-framework.md), 2026-07-29 (Japan; cites https://www.fsa.go.jp/news/r7/shouken/20260220/20260220.html). Korea from https://github.com/kang9307/lotto-generator/blob/4314f26afa9fe2cc58ea182b81449615cdad8fb4/en/posts/korea_esg_corporate_trends.html; others from the duncanhill1 map.)
- **[high]** GRI changes, confirmed from the standards' own text: - GRI 101: Biodiversity 2024 has been effective since 1 Jan 2026. - GRI 102: Climate Change 2025 and GRI 103: Energy 2025 are effective for reports published on or after 1 Jan 2027. They supersede GRI 305-1 to 305-5 and GRI 302. - GRI 102 adds an adaptation-plan disclosure (102-2) and a just-transition disclosure (102-3). For Indian FY2026-27 reports, published around mid-2027, the reporting year already under way is covered. ([source](https://www.globalreporting.org/publications/documents/english/gri-102-climate-change-2025/), Standards texts mirrored at https://github.com/S-PANDIYAN/Sustainable-Business-Model-AI (GRI 103 Energy 2025.txt, GRI 101 Biodiversity 2024.txt); 102-2/102-3 detail from https://syntropyearth.com/thinking/reporting-standards-health-access-coverage/ (2026-09-16))
- **[medium]** SBTi Corporate Net-Zero Standard V2.0 is published (by June 2026; translations dated Jul-Aug 2026). - Transition: use V1.3.1 for 2026 submissions; either version from Q1 2027; V2 mandatory for submissions after 31 Jan 2028. - Category A covers large companies everywhere plus medium companies in high-income countries. It requires near-term Scope 3 targets, transition-plan disclosure and limited assurance of base-year GHG data. - Category B covers small companies everywhere plus medium companies in lower-income countries, with more flexibility. - Category A removals obligation (Ongoing Emissions Responsibility) starts 2035. - Scope 2 targets can be absolute or share of low-carbon electricity. For Indian mid-caps, country income level may decide the category. (India is lower-middle income per the World Bank; this is model knowledge, not re-verified in this session.) ([source](https://sciencebasedtargets.org/corporate-net-zero-standard-v2), Read via digest https://github.com/IvanWeissVanDerPol/satellite-paraguay/blob/d31a0e883caa0165d73f6f3891dbc3b0bedee769/research/02-100-ITERATIONS-DIGEST/21-40-vyra/34_sbti_corporate_net_zero_v2.md and Sweep blog dated 2026-06-12 (https://github.com/Broser-ai/Sweep))
- **[high]** GHG Protocol Scope 2 revision: - The public consultation ran Oct 2025 to Jan 2026 and proposed hourly matching, deliverability and residual-mix reform. - The GHG Protocol published the raw feedback on 29 Jul 2026: 1,072 respondents. - My analysis of the published file: 70% of 909 respondents rated mandatory hourly matching 1-2 (oppose) and 22% rated it 4-5; among US respondents 78% opposed; 76% of 748 supported exemptions. - India had only 12 respondents (Japan 80, China 65, Singapore 23). Indian respondents (n=10) averaged 2.7 on hourly matching against 1.77 for US respondents; 89% of 9 Indian respondents backed exemptions. - CSEP (New Delhi) argued that India's integrated, nationally balanced grid dispatch should shape deliverability rules. ([source](https://ghgprotocol.org/sites/default/files/2026-07/S2-PublicConsultationFeedback-RawData-2026.07.29.xlsx), 2026-07-29 data. Processed mirror: https://github.com/jessicacohen554-cyber/scope-2-consult. Statistics computed by me in this session, with small Indian n.)
- **[medium]** GHG Protocol and ISO will combine their corporate carbon-accounting standards into a single harmonised global standard (announced July 2026). The same announcement reported Scope 2 consultation results and early feedback on the Actions and Market Instruments RFI. An integrated public consultation on the future corporate standard is planned for Q2 2027. The Scope 3 progress update of March 2026 includes draft requirements to disaggregate reported emissions by data type. Final standards were earlier expected in late 2027, with a 2-3 year phase-in. ([source](https://ghgprotocol.org/blog/ghg-protocol-announces-key-standard-development-updates), July 2026. Via IAS Plus feed 2026-07-29 (https://github.com/pauliusgin/curated-news/blob/20cf26e81bfd2b6e0b80ab3b03d8ab42b839e14f/testdata/feeds/ias-plus.json); Q2 2027 and Scope 3 detail from https://github.com/firatbarca/personal/blob/5c4a2b27ed5d4ebede2c50cd9becb34505331f26/src/posts/emission-factor-nobody-owns.md (2026-09-14); timeline from https://github.com/endoergic/the_carbon_book/blob/9ed43618e67e5293f9753fa7129c544fae47cadb/CH05_emissions_accounting.md)
- **[low]** CDP: a July 2026 secondary source describes the 2026 questionnaire as aligned with IFRS S2, requested by 540+ investors with about USD 110tn AUM. In the 2025 cycle more than 22,000 companies responded and 887 made the A-list. CDP's own 2026 cycle dates and changes could NOT be verified because cdp.net was blocked. ([source](https://www.cdp.net/en/disclosure-2026), Secondary sources 2026-07-15 (https://github.com/Githamza/teamWheelsLP/blob/6fc34317f4dee8a5d8a8edaa9862953cd8306e21/content/english/blog/corporate-carbon-reduction-planning-decarbonization-action-plan.md) and https://www.slrconsulting.com/afr/insights/cdp-2026-top-tips/)
- **[medium]** PROPRIETARY-STYLE CORPUS SCAN: I downloaded 719 FY2024-25 BRSR texts (and 798 FY2023-24 BRSRs) from a public GitHub corpus and regex-counted framework mentions. - IFRS S1/S2: only 2 (0.3%: JSW Energy, Suzlon). - 'ISSB': 18 (2.5%), of which 15 are a boilerplate SASB footnote. - CSRD/ESRS: 10 (1.4%), mostly double-materiality references (e.g. Vedanta, Godrej Properties). - SBTi: 58 (8.1%). GRI: 117 (16.3%). GHG Protocol: 77 (10.7%, up from 6.3%). - CDP: 29 (4.0%). TCFD: 22 (3.1%). TNFD: 7. CBAM: 5. CCTS: 6. - 'Transition plan': 9 (1.3%). BRSR Core: 218 (30.3%, up from 18.8%). Caveats: third-party corpus, not the full listed universe; a mention is not adoption. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 reports (published 2025); computed 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Global vendor explainers are EU-centric and partly stale. A scrape of ESG software vendor Sweep's site (~1,424 pages where CSRD/ISSB appear) mentions BRSR on only 2 pages. Its 'FAQs about CSRD compliance' page still states pre-final scope ('1,000 employees and... €50M+ turnover or €25M+ balance sheet'), and 'What is the CSRD' still frames the thresholds as 'proposed'. An India-facing consultancy's ESRS guide (syntropyearth) still says the thresholds are 'under revision'. This is evidence that AI answers built on such pages can give outdated post-Omnibus answers. ([source](https://github.com/Broser-ai/Sweep/blob/8170875b3e3b98a66fe40442311a6fe932106030/sweep-scrape/text/blog__faqs-about-csrd-compliance.md), Scrape date not stated (content references 2026 events); syntropyearth guide at https://github.com/syntropyearth/syntropyearth-site/blob/7e1406d1c441c06fd05ccd040d8a4641e7a1db00/guide/frameworks/esrs.html)
- **[medium]** Indian listed subsidiaries of European groups file BRSRs that rarely reference their parents' CSRD/ESRS regime. In the FY2024-25 corpus, ABB India, SKF India, Schaeffler India, Sanofi India, HeidelbergCement India and Grindwell Norton have no CSRD/ESRS mention; only Vesuvius India cites CSRD/ESRS. Intragroup data requests are not shielded by the value-chain cap, which points to an inbound-CSRD readiness gap. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 BRSRs; computed 2026-10-01)
- **[low]** An EU-level limited-assurance standard for CSRD is due for adoption by 1 Jul 2027. SBTi V2 Category A also requires limited assurance of base-year data. Together with BRSR Core assurance phasing in India, assurance-grade lineage becomes a shared requirement across regimes. ([source](https://github.com/firatbarca/personal/blob/5c4a2b27ed5d4ebede2c50cd9becb34505331f26/src/posts/emission-factor-nobody-owns.md), 2026-09-14 (secondary; not verified against EU text))

### finance-sector (40 searches run)

- **[high]** ACCESS LIMITS (read first). WebSearch was unavailable: the session-wide 200-call budget was already spent by parallel agents. Firecrawl returned 402 (insufficient credits). The org egress policy blocked WebFetch to rbi.org.in, sebi.gov.in, pib.gov.in, dea.gov.in, ifc.org, carbonaccountingfinancials.com (PCAF), climatebonds.net, ngfs.net, ieefa.org, google.com, bing.com, duckduckgo.com, the major Indian news sites and every competitor site tested (crediblesg.com, climes.io, thesustainabilitycloud.com). The only open route was GitHub: code search plus raw files. So the regulatory facts below come from GitHub-hosted copies of official documents: parsed RBI PDFs, an RSS-fed SEBI circular mirror and a PCAF signatory-list import. News facts come from headlines and summaries in a BFSI news digest repo; the articles themselves were not read. No SERP, autocomplete, People Also Ask or search-volume data could be collected. To restore access, add the hosts above to the environment's Network access allow-list, or choose a broader access level. ([source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web), 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** RBI issued separate 'Climate Finance and Management of Climate Change Risks' Directions, 2025 for Commercial Banks, Small Finance Banks and NBFCs on 28 Nov 2025, as part of its master-direction consolidation. The commercial-bank version is RBI/DOR/2025-26/172 and came into force immediately. ([source](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=13138&Mode=0), 2025-11-28 (title and date from the RBI notification index captured at https://github.com/nikhilenumukkala-boop/circular-tracker, updated 2026-10-01; text from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Brainstormex/RegTech/HEAD/server/src/raw/master-directions/Commercial-Banks-Climate-Finance-and-Management-of-Climate-Change-Risks.md))
- **[high]** The Directions are a shell. Their stated objective is to let entities 'carry out comprehensive assessment of climate change risks, integrate climate change risk considerations into their extant risk management frameworks'. But the only operative chapter is the green-deposits framework: Board policy, Financing Framework with external review, use of proceeds, annual third-party verification, annual impact assessment and disclosures. No climate-risk disclosure, scenario-analysis or stress-testing chapter appears in the parsed PDF, which is dated 8 Jan 2026. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/piyushsr-0708/RegIntelAI-V2/HEAD/datasets/parsed/MD13138.json), PDF creation date 2026-01-08)
- **[high]** Borrower data demand built into the RBI green-deposit rules. Third-party verification must cover 'validation of all the related / required information provided by the borrower'. Banks must publish an annual Impact Assessment Report, prepared 'with the assistance of external firms', using indicators such as GHG emissions avoided (tCO2e), energy savings (MWh), renewable energy generated (MWh), waste diverted (t) and sequestration (tCO2e). A bank that cannot quantify impact must disclose the reasons, difficulties and time-bound plans. ([source](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=13138&Mode=0), 2025-11-28 (text read via https://raw.githubusercontent.com/piyushsr-0708/RegIntelAI-V2/HEAD/datasets/parsed/MD13138.json))
- **[high]** The NBFC version (Id=12940) applies only to deposit-taking NBFCs (NBFC-D) and deposit-taking Housing Finance Companies. Proceeds must follow 'the official Indian green taxonomy'. Pending its finalisation, an interim list of eligible categories applies, with exclusions such as fossil fuels, nuclear and hydro over 25 MW. ([source](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12940&Mode=0), 2025-11-28 (text via https://raw.githubusercontent.com/piyushsr-0708/RegIntelAI-V2/HEAD/datasets/parsed/MD12940.json))
- **[medium]** RBI's climate-risk DISCLOSURE framework does not appear to be final as of 1 Oct 2026. The IPSF Annual Report 2025 lists it as a 'draft Disclosure Framework on Climate-Related Financial Risks… Final issuance is expected soon'. Among 372 RBI notifications dated 2026 in a third-party index of RBI notifications, there is no climate-titled Direction or amendment; the three 28 Nov 2025 climate Directions carry no 'updated as on' tag. Caveat: the index covers notifications only, not draft press releases, so a 2026 draft could have been missed. Status could not be confirmed on rbi.org.in. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/motazsaad/ESG-RAG-benchmark-ragas/HEAD/data/raw/50872_7142_2024.md), IPSF report 2025; index checked 2026-10-01 at https://github.com/nikhilenumukkala-boop/circular-tracker)
- **[low]** RBI climate scenario analysis and stress-testing guidance: no final or draft guidance text could be found. The IPSF 2025 report mentions only RBI 'capacity-building workshops for financial institutions on climate stress testing, emissions estimation, and transition planning' and a March 2025 policy seminar. Exam-prep flashcards from March 2026 claim RBI already requires climate scenario analysis in ICAAP. That contradicts the RBI notification index and points to public confusion, which is an AEO opening for a dated, accurate status explainer. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/dakguru/ip2026/HEAD/study-planner/src/data/flashcards/currentAffairs/mar2026.ts), 2026-03 flashcard; IPSF 2025)
- **[medium]** RB-CRIS, RBI's climate data repository, was reported in 'final stages' in Aug 2026. The physical-risk module (floods, cyclones, droughts) is expected 'in coming months'; transition risk follows. RBI has described the transition part as including sectoral pathways and a carbon emission intensity database. This is headline-level evidence; the article itself was blocked. ([source](https://bfsi.economictimes.indiatimes.com/articles/rbis-climate-risk-repository-rb-cris-in-final-stages-physical-risk-module-expected-in-coming-months/133069119), 2026-08-10)
- **[medium]** RBI's Financial Stability Report (Dec 2025) flagged climate risk among concerns (ET summary). In June 2026, Deputy Governor Swaminathan J told banks to manage uncertainty from geopolitics, climate change and AI. Supervisory attention is rising even though rules are not final. ([source](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/markets/stocks/news/rbis-swaminathan-warns-banks-on-rising-risks-beyond-balance-sheets/articleshow/131494530.cms), 2025-12-30 FSR coverage (https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/markets/stocks/news/strongly-positioned-country-must-shield-against-looming-risks-rbi/articleshow/126242005.cms); 2026-06-03 speech)
- **[medium]** Banks asked RBI for a CRR cut on green deposits to scale green lending (ET, 1 Sep 2026). An SBI report sought priority-sector-lending reforms to boost green finance (Sep 2026). Axis Bank reported ₹45,511 crore of green lending (Aug 2026). Green books are growing, and each green rupee needs borrower-level eligibility and impact evidence under the Directions. ([source](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/industry/banking/finance/banking/banks-seek-crr-cut-on-green-deposits-to-boost-green-lending-in-india/articleshow/133690431.cms), 2026-09-01 (also https://ddnews.gov.in/en/sbi-report-seeks-major-priority-sector-lending-reforms-to-boost-infrastructure-and-green-finance/ and https://indiacsr.in/esg-axis-bank-reports-%E2%82%B945511-crore-in-green-lending-and-2-76-million-households-reached/))
- **[medium]** PCAF in India. A PCAF signatory-list import dated 26 Aug 2026 (third-party GitHub copy of carbonaccountingfinancials.com/en/signatories) lists at least 11 Indian institutions: - SBI (joined Feb 2025) - HDFC Bank (3 Aug 2026) - Central Bank of India (5 Aug 2026) - Bank of Baroda (Sep 2024) - Union Bank of India (Aug 2024) - IDFC FIRST Bank (Aug 2024) - Indian Bank (Jan 2025) - Indian Overseas Bank (Jan 2025) - UCO Bank (Feb 2025) - ESAF SFB (2019) - PNB (Nov 2024) Only PNB has status 'Disclosed', and its disclosure document is its BRSR 2024. All others are 'Committed' with no disclosure yet. The import is a subset, so other Indian members may be missing. ([source](https://github.com/Motaz3d/HydraShield), 2026-08-26 import; HDFC commitment letter https://carbonaccountingfinancials.com/files/institutions_downloads/Signed-PCAF-CommitmentLetter-3August2026.pdf)
- **[medium]** IndusInd Bank joined PCAF in Aug 2026, so three large Indian banks joined that month (HDFC, Central Bank of India, IndusInd). Exam material from March 2026 also lists L&T Finance as a PCAF signatory (low confidence). Financed-emissions measurement is spreading fast across Indian lenders, and most have not yet disclosed. PCAF's expectation that signatories disclose within three years of joining is from memory and was not re-verified this session. ([source](https://www.businessworld.in/article/indusind-bank-joins-pcaf-to-strengthen-carbon-accounting-619755), 2026-08-18 to 2026-08-22 (also https://smestreet.in/banking/indusind-bank-limited-joins-the-partnership-for-carbon-accounting-financials-pcaf-12394717))
- **[high]** India Climate Finance Taxonomy. The DEA released the Draft Framework on 7 May 2025, with comments due by 25 June 2025. Sectoral annexes listing climate-supportive and transition activities are to follow, and DEA said it would then 'release the Framework'. ([source](https://static.pib.gov.in/WriteReadData/specificdocs/documents/2025/may/doc202557551101.pdf), 2025-05-07 (PIB release text via https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TaranpreetSinghSaggu/Stocklens/HEAD/mof_2025/2025/5/07/Department%20of%20Economic%20Affairs,%20Ministry%20of%20Finance,%20invites%20suggestions%20from%20expertspublic%20on%20Draft.html))
- **[medium]** The taxonomy was still a draft in Jan 2026: IFSCA officers wrote in ICSI's Chartered Secretary that 'India's own Climate Finance Taxonomy remains in draft form', and RBI's Directions say 'pending finalization of the taxonomy'. June 2026 commentary (World Environment Day notes on a Hindu article) still urged government to 'finalise & enact' it. No evidence of a final taxonomy was found up to 1 Oct 2026; this is unverified on dea.gov.in. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hrkartiktomar-netizen/Exam_preparation/HEAD/source_documents/md/07_ICSI_Study_Material/ICSI_CSJ_2026-01_Transition_Bonds_ISSB_S2.md), 2026-01 (also https://raw.githubusercontent.com/selvakkannanjothi/Gayathri_Claude/HEAD/my_notes/appolo%20ca/current-affairs-hindu-june3-5-2026.md, 2026-06))
- **[high]** SEBI ESG debt. SEBI's 5 June 2025 ESG Debt Securities framework covers social, sustainability and sustainability-linked bonds. On 27 Feb 2026, SEBI harmonised third-party reviewer/certifier norms for green debt with those ESG debt rules, amending Chapter IX of the NCS Master Circular of 15 Oct 2025. The reviewer must be independent, conflict-free in remuneration and expert, and the review scope must appear in the offer document. ([source](https://www.sebi.gov.in/legal/circulars/feb-2026/revised-norms-for-appointment-of-an-independent-third-party-reviewer-certifier-for-green-debt-security_100043.html), 2026-02-27 (read via SEBI RSS mirror https://github.com/rhnvrm/stock-market-circulars))
- **[high]** SEBI extended the ESG debt framework to municipal debt securities through the Municipal Debt Securities (Amendment) Regulations, 2026 (new Reg 4F, July 2026). It also set up a Working Group on 18 Feb 2026 to review the regulatory framework for ESG Rating Providers. ESG-labelled issuance is broadening and ESG-rating rules may tighten. ([source](https://www.sebi.gov.in/legal/regulations/jul-2026/securities-and-exchange-board-of-india-issue-and-listing-of-municipal-debt-securities-amendment-regulations-2026_102775.html), 2026-07-01 to 2026-07-08; ERP working group https://www.sebi.gov.in/media-and-notifications/press-releases/feb-2026/sebi-constitutes-working-group-to-review-esg-rating-providers-erps-regulatory-framework_99864.html)
- **[medium]** IFSCA's Framework for Transition Bonds (29 July 2025) targets hard-to-abate issuers at GIFT IFSC. It requires: - credible entity-level transition plans with measurable GHG targets - disclosure of the Scope 3 reporting timeline, capex roadmap and locked-in emissions - mandatory external review Because India's taxonomy is a draft, issuers may reference EU, Japan, Singapore-Asia, ASEAN or Australian taxonomies, or IEA/METI roadmaps. The ICSI authors explicitly link it to the GHG Emission Intensity Target Rules 2025 and CCTS. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hrkartiktomar-netizen/Exam_preparation/HEAD/source_documents/md/07_ICSI_Study_Material/ICSI_CSJ_2026-01_Transition_Bonds_ISSB_S2.md), framework 2025-07-29; article 2026-01)
- **[medium]** The IMF–World Bank FSAP for India, published by SEBI on 3 Dec 2025, called for granular data and adaptive measures for climate-risk management. That gives an external, authoritative hook for 'data gap' content aimed at bank risk teams. ([source](https://www.sebi.gov.in/media-and-notifications/press-releases/dec-2025/india-financial-sector-assessment-program-2024_98131.html), 2025-12-03)
- **[medium]** DFI and foreign PE capital flowing into Indian lenders and climate assets in 2026 brings DFI-style ESG requirements (IFC Performance Standards, ESMS and exclusion lists are standard for these investors; not re-verified this session). Examples: - IFC $1bn MSME line, $500m already released to SIDBI (Sep 2026) - IFC weighing $80m in IDFC FIRST Bank (Sep 2026) - FMO NCDs of ₹380 crore in UGRO Capital and ₹360 crore in MAS Financial (Sep 2026) - KKR's first climate-transition investment in India (PMI Electro/Allfleet, Mar 2026) - BII ₹90 crore into UC Impower (Feb 2026) - NIIF India-Japan Fund planning faster climate bets (Aug 2026) ([source](https://m.economictimes.com/news/economy/finance/finmin-secures-1-billion-ifc-funding-for-msmes-500-million-already-released-to-sidbi/articleshow/133848674.cms), 2026-02 to 2026-09 (also https://www.dealstreetasia.com/stories/ifc-idfc-first-bank-493782/, https://www.entrepreneurindia.com/blog/en/news/ugro-capital-raises-inr-380-cr-from-fmo-in-third-ncd-investment.61071, https://scanx.trade/stock-market-news/companies/mas-financial-allots-360-crore-ncds-to-fmo/40802853, https://www.livemint.com/companies/news/kkr-pmi-electro-investment-electric-buses-11773804750728.html, https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/markets/stocks/news/british-international-invests-rs-90-crore-in-uc-impower-fund/articleshow/127967570.cms, https://www.livemint.com/companies/news/national-investment-and-infrastructure-fund-india-japan-fund-climate-opportunities-infrastructure-investment-niif-ijf-11785920390760.html))
- **[medium]** PE ESG data standard. The ESG Data Convergence Initiative (EDCI) has 375+ GP/LP members (vendor blog, updated May 2025). Its core metrics: - Scope 1 and 2 emissions (Scope 3 optional) - % renewable energy - board and C-suite diversity - work-related injuries, fatalities and days lost - net new hires and employee engagement - a net-zero/decarbonisation-plan metric These overlap heavily with BRSR Core, which supports a 'collect once' crosswalk. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Broser-ai/Sweep/HEAD/sweep-scrape/text/blog__esg-data-convergence-initiative-edci-what-do-you-need-to-know.md), 2025-05-20)
- **[medium]** ESG ratings became a visible funding input for Indian financials in Aug–Sep 2026. Disclosed scores include: - Kotak Mahindra Bank: CRISIL ESG 72 - HDFC Bank: 77.78 (Niche Ninety Nine) - Aadhar Housing: 71 (NSE Sustainability) - MAS Financial: voluntary rating 71 - Can Fin Homes, HUDCO, Yes Bank, SBI Cards and Karur Vysya Bank also disclosed scores HomeFirst Finance is funding 10,000 ESG-rated homes and plans ₹150 crore of ESG debt to attract sustainable capital. ([source](https://www.livemint.com/companies/news/homefirst-finance-is-funding-esg-rated-homes-hopes-it-will-attract-sustainable-capital-11787310076367.html), 2026-08-20 to 2026-09-18 (e.g. https://businessupturn.com/business/kotak-mahindra-bank-secures-crisil-esg-72-leader-rating-for-fiscal-2026/, https://scanx.trade/stock-market-news/companies/hdfc-bank-discloses-77-78-esg-rating-assigned-niche-ninety-nine/50704295, https://www.tipranks.com/news/company-announcements/aadhar-housing-finance-receives-esg-rating-of-71-from-nse-sustainability))
- **[medium]** Financial-sector BRSR filers are publishing FY2025-26 BRSRs with external assurance: Piramal Finance (assured by LKM & Co), IFCI (external assurance), Indostar ('emission cuts'), Aavas, Cholamandalam Financial Holdings and GIC Re. Lenders now have assured own-operations data, so the gap moves to financed (Scope 3 Category 15) data from borrowers. ([source](https://scanx.trade/stock-market-news/companies/piramal-finance-files-business-responsibility-sustainability-report-for-fy26/42650784), 2026-08-25 to 2026-09-03 (also https://scanx.trade/stock-market-news/companies/ifci-ltd-files-fy26-brsr-report-external-assurance/49918958))
- **[medium]** Sovereign green bonds continue: the H2 FY2026-27 borrowing calendar includes ₹15,000 crore of green bonds (Sep 2026). Offshore climate capital is also reaching Indian infrastructure lenders (India Infrastructure Finance, Oct 2026). Both are headline-level evidence. ([source](https://www.niftytrader.in/markets/india-h2-fy27-borrowing-plan-green-bonds-news/), 2026-09-28 (also https://www.vccircle.com/indiainfrastructure-finance-snags-capital-from-offshore-backer-to-fund-climate-projects?amp))
- **[low]** IRDAI. No IRDAI climate-risk or ESG regulation for insurers could be found or verified this session; GitHub code search for 'IRDAI climate risk' returned only product and marketing repos. Insurer climate demand is visible mainly through BRSR filings (e.g. GIC Re FY26) and new climate-hedging products (NCDEX launched India's first SEBI-approved weather derivative, RAINMUMBAI, in May 2026). Insurers look like a low-priority segment for ESG Astraa. ([source](https://scanx.trade/stock-market-news/companies/gic-re-files-fy26-brsr-report-detailing-sustainability-metrics/49746696), 2026-05 to 2026-09 (also https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/markets/stocks/news/ncdex-launches-indias-first-weather-derivatives-contract-based-on-mumbai-rainfall/articleshow/131224804.cms))
- **[high]** Product fit check (local repo singhpratham19/esgtools, read only). There are no lender-facing features: no PCAF/Category 15, lender portal, green-loan impact indicators or EDCI/IFC PS mapping. A grep found one 'financed decarbonization roadmap' subtitle. Relevant assets that do exist: Scope 1-3 accounting, evidence/audit-trail records (67 matches for assurance/evidence/lineage), the supplier module, and TCFD/IFRS S2 and BRSR mappings. Borrower-side content (CFO answering lender and investor requests) fits today; bank-side content needs a 'lender data pack' export. ([source](https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgtools), 2026-10-01)
- **[low]** Content-gap inference (NOT SERP-verified; search tools unavailable). The competitor content documented in the brand context (TSC, Oren, Climes, Credibl, Sprih, Breathe, CBAM specialists) centres on BRSR/CBAM/CCTS software listicles and explainers. None of it was observed addressing the borrower side of finance-sector demand: what PCAF banks will ask borrowers for, the borrower-level impact evidence behind green deposits, or carbon liabilities (CCTS shortfall, CBAM certificates) as credit metrics. This should be confirmed with a SERP check before committing budget. ([source](https://www.thesustainabilitycloud.com/blog/best-cbam-softwares-in-india-2026/), brand context as of 2026-10-01)

### scope3-supply-chain (23 searches run)

- **[high]** How this research was done: WebSearch had already used its 200-call session budget when this agent started, and Firecrawl returned 402 (out of credits). The egress proxy blocked WebFetch to every regulator and standard-setter tried: sebi.gov.in, pib.gov.in, cea.nic.in, ghgprotocol.org, sciencebasedtargets.org, carbon-transparency.org, eur-lex, gov.uk, epa.gov, zenodo, climatiq.io, ieefa.org, sprih.com and wikipedia. Only github.com and raw.githubusercontent.com could be reached. Evidence therefore comes from: (a) primary standards repositories hosted on GitHub (WBCSD PACT, Eclipse Tractus-X/Catena-X, USEPA); (b) GitHub code search across 23 queries; (c) my own text-mining of a public GitHub corpus of Indian BRSR filings; (d) secondary GitHub documents that cite law firms and regulators. Every regulatory fact below that is not from a primary repo is marked medium or low confidence. All of them should be re-verified on the regulator's site before publication. No search volumes were measured. ([source](none (session tooling and egress limits)), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** SEBI value-chain disclosure (circular of 28 Mar 2025, SEBI/HO/CFD/CFD-PoD-1/P/CIR/2025/42, as summarised by a 2026 third-party product strategy doc). Value-chain ESG disclosures are VOLUNTARY for the top 250 listed entities from FY2025-26. 'Value chain' means upstream and downstream partners that each account for at least 2% of purchases or sales by value. Disclosure may be limited to the top partners that together cover 75%. SEBI's own pages could not be opened. One detail in the same doc conflicts with my understanding and should NOT be relied on: it says value-chain disclosure becomes 'mandatory for top 1000 by FY2026-27', which looks like a conflation with BRSR Core assurance. My model knowledge (unverified this session) is that value-chain assessment/assurance is voluntary from FY2026-27. ([source](https://github.com/kenithphilip/Anvil/blob/main/docs/STRATEGIC_BET_07_brsr_value_chain.md), 2026 (doc); circular dated 2025-03-28; see also https://www.sebi.gov.in/sebi_data/faqfiles/apr-2025/1745399101865.pdf and https://www.sebi.gov.in/sebi_data/meetingfiles/dec-2024/1735040682024_1.pdf (not opened this session))
- **[medium]** My analysis of a public GitHub corpus of FY2024-25 BRSR text extracts. There are 729 files, of which 705 are non-empty and 704 contain 'Business Responsibility'. Only 219 of 705 (31%) report a numeric Scope 3 figure in the Principle 6 leadership indicator. Another 289 (41%) include the question but leave it blank, dash it, or say 'not calculated' / 'in process'. 197 (28%) omit the Scope 3 indicator entirely. Leadership indicators are voluntary. The classifier is a heuristic, checked by hand on random samples. The corpus provenance is undocumented, so treat it as a proxy, not an official SEBI or NSE dataset. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 reports (filed mid/late 2025); analysed 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Same corpus, panel of the 612 companies present in both FY2023-24 and FY2024-25 folders: companies reporting a numeric Scope 3 figure rose from 146 (23.9%) to 189 (30.9%) in one year. Scope 3 quantification is growing, but most listed companies still cannot quantify it. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2023-24 vs FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** Scope 3 disclosures lean toward the easy categories (keyword-mention proxy). Of the 219 FY2024-25 BRSRs with a numeric Scope 3 figure: 54 mention business travel, 41 employee commuting and 20 upstream transport. Only 44 (20%) mention purchased goods or Category 1. Only 9 (4%) mention supplier-specific or primary data, and 10 mention spend-based methods. The category that matters most for manufacturers (purchased goods from suppliers) is the least evidenced. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** Supplier assessment is rare. The BRSR Principle 6 leadership indicator asks for the 'percentage of value chain partners (by value of business done) assessed for environmental impacts'. Across 705 FY2024-25 reports: 121 (17%) give a positive percentage (48 above 75%, 29 at 51-75%, 25 at 26-50%, 19 at 25% or below). 226 report nil, 0% or NA. 158 give narrative only, such as 'developing the process' (Union Bank) or 'not yet conducted formal assessments' (Control Print). 200 omit the question. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** MSMEs are a material share of listed-company inputs. The BRSR Principle 8 indicator 'Input material directly sourced from MSMEs/small producers' parsed for 611 of 705 FY2024-25 reports. Median 14%, interquartile range 5% to 29.7%. 186 companies source at least 25% from MSMEs and 76 source at least 50%. Spot checks: Suprajit Engineering 70%, Roto Pumps 47.25%, Shalimar Paints 31%. Any primary-data Scope 3 programme therefore depends on MSMEs. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** Emission-factor provenance named in FY2024-25 BRSRs (705 files, mention counts). CEA or Central Electricity Authority: 102. DEFRA or DESNZ (UK factors): 39. Spend-based: 15. US EPA or USEEIO: 8. India GHG Program: 6. EXIOBASE: 1. Climatiq: 0. UK DEFRA factors are cited by more than a third as many companies as CEA, which points to reliance on non-Indian factors for non-electricity Scope 3. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** PCF data exchange is invisible in Indian listed disclosures. Of 705 FY2024-25 BRSRs: 0 mention Catena-X or WBCSD PACT, 12 mention product carbon footprint or PCF, 6 mention ISO 14067, 6 mention Together for Sustainability, 6 Higg, 43 EcoVadis, 57 SBTi, 50 'supplier engagement', 5 CBAM and 7 CCTS. EcoVadis is the most visible buyer-driven supplier-ESG signal. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** BRSR already contains a value-chain carbon-market question: 'How many Green Credits have been generated or procured… by the top ten (in terms of value of purchases and sales, respectively) value chain partners'. It appears in 263 of 705 FY2024-25 reports. Sample answer (AIA Engineering): 0. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** CEA grid-factor version drift in Indian carbon tools. Several independent 2026 open-source tools cite 'CEA CO2 Baseline Database v21.0, FY2024-25 all-India weighted average 0.710 tCO2/MWh'. Others still hard-code v18, v20.0, or 0.82 labelled 'CEA 2023-24'. A third-party doc notes that CEA refreshes the baseline roughly every December. cea.nic.in was blocked, so v21.0 = 0.710 is corroborated only by these secondary sources. ([source](https://github.com/project-robin/CarbonComply), 2026 repos; also https://github.com/vanshdigitals/VaayuMitra, https://github.com/CodeWithMehru/Carbon-Karma, https://github.com/KrishChothani/Breathe_ESG_Assignment)
- **[medium]** Indian freight and transport factors are old. The India GHG Program's 'India Specific Road Transport Emission Factors' date from 2015 (WRI) and are still embedded in 2026 tools, often mixed with UK DESNZ 2023 factors in the same calculator. This is the Scope 3 Category 4/9 (transport) factor gap for India. ([source](https://github.com/debjithacks/Personal-Carbon-Footprint-Estimator), factors 2015; tools 2026; also https://github.com/RohanS358/copaila-carbon-backend)
- **[high]** USEPA supply-chain factors are US-specific. They are USEEIO-based, and the latest release is v1.3, published as 'Supply Chain GHG Emission Factors v1.3 by NAICS-6' (a US industry classification). EPA states the site 'is NOT intended to provide guidance on GHG reporting'. Applying these USD/NAICS factors to Indian INR spend without currency, price-year and economic-structure adjustment is a misuse risk. That last point is my inference. ([source](https://github.com/USEPA/supply-chain-factors), v1.3 (release page; year not shown))
- **[low]** GHG Protocol Scope 3 revision. A Phase 1 Progress Update was published on 31 Mar 2026. It proposes splitting Scope 3 totals into tiers (specific/primary, secondary, spend-based) and a 95% minimum coverage floor with justified exclusions. A public consultation draft is expected before the final standard, targeted for late 2027. A Corporate Standard Phase 1 update was published in Dec 2025, and a possible 16th category ('other value chain activities') is under discussion. These details come from secondary sources; ghgprotocol.org was blocked. ([source](https://github.com/firatbarca/personal/blob/main/src/posts/spend-based-estimate-deadline.md), 2026-03-31; also https://github.com/endoergic/the_carbon_book/blob/main/CH05_emissions_accounting.md)
- **[low]** SBTi Corporate Net-Zero Standard V2 status is unclear. A May 2026 fact-currency tracker said the second consultation closed on 12 Dec 2025, the final was not yet published, and V2 becomes mandatory for new targets from 1 Jan 2028. A later digest describes V2.0 as adopted by the Board, with the validation portal opening in Q1 2027 and V2.0 mandatory for all submissions after 31 Jan 2028. On Scope 3, a vendor blog (Sweep) describes three target approaches: overarching reduction, supplier/customer alignment, and category- or activity-specific targets. Only 'Category A' companies (large companies everywhere, plus medium companies in high-income countries) must set near-term Scope 3 targets. The sciencebasedtargets.org pages were not reachable. ([source](https://github.com/endoergic/the_carbon_book/blob/main/00_FACT_CURRENCY_SOURCES_2026-05.md), May-Sep 2026; also https://github.com/IvanWeissVanDerPol/satellite-paraguay/blob/main/research/02-100-ITERATIONS-DIGEST/21-40-vyra/34_sbti_corporate_net_zero_v2.md and https://www.sweep.net/blog/the-sbti-net-zero-standard-revisions-and-what-they-mean-for-businesses (not opened directly))
- **[medium]** SBTi Corporate Near-Term Criteria v5.3.1 took effect on 14 Apr 2026, per a copy of the criteria table. It was a non-substantive update to the absolute contraction method for recent base years. V1.3.1 / v5.3.1 remains the live rulebook for 2026 submissions. ([source](https://github.com/IVAN-GOEL/hack-for-the-future), 2026-04-14)
- **[medium]** EU Omnibus I is Directive (EU) 2026/470. It was adopted on 24 Feb 2026, published in the OJ on 26 Feb 2026 and entered into force on 18 Mar 2026. CSRD now applies only to companies with more than 1,000 employees AND more than EUR 450m turnover, with Member State transposition due by 19 Mar 2027. CSDDD now applies to more than 5,000 employees AND more than EUR 1.5bn turnover, with transposition by 26 Jul 2028 and application from 26 Jul 2029. A 'value chain cap' uses the VSME standard as the ceiling on what in-scope companies may request from value-chain companies with up to 1,000 employees. Whether the cap protects non-EU (Indian) suppliers was NOT verified. ([source](https://github.com/zoltan2/brussels-governance-monitor), 2026-02 to 2026-03; corroborating sources cited in https://github.com/endoergic/the_carbon_book/blob/main/00_FACT_CURRENCY_SOURCES_2026-05.md (Gibson Dunn, Covington))
- **[medium]** CBAM default values for the definitive period: the binding values are in Implementing Regulation (EU) 2025/2621, as replaced by Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1740. Mark-ups over the country default are 10% (2026), 20% (2027) and 30% (2028 onward) for cement, iron/steel, aluminium and hydrogen; fertilisers carry 1%. Examples for India: unwrought aluminium CN 7601 10 00 default 1.870 tCO2e/t (Türkiye 1.700, other countries 2.203); grey cement clinker 1.44 tCO2e/t, or 1.584 with the 2026 mark-up. Missing supplier or installation data therefore costs more every year. ([source](https://github.com/Keremozdemirra/cbam-mcp), data retrieved 2026-09-24; also https://github.com/ets-overview/free-allocation-benchmarks)
- **[low]** The Commission has PROPOSED extending CBAM to downstream products (Dec 2025); it has not been adopted. If adopted, Indian makers of steel- and aluminium-intensive downstream goods would need precursor emissions data from their Indian mill suppliers, which turns CBAM into a supplier-data problem. ([source](https://github.com/endoergic/the_carbon_book/blob/main/00_FACT_CURRENCY_SOURCES_2026-05.md), 2025-12 proposal; cites https://www.mayerbrown.com/en/insights/publications/2025/12/european-commission-issues-cbam-operational-rules-and-proposes-downstream-extension-of-the-cbam-scope (not opened))
- **[high]** WBCSD PACT. PACT Methodology v3 and Technical Specifications v3.0.0 were both published on 30 Apr 2025. Patches followed: v3.0.1 (12 Jun 2025), v3.0.2 (18 Jul 2025) and v3.0.3 (9 Dec 2025). v2.x deprecation was set for 1 Apr 2026. v3 added biogenic and CCU attributes and 24 new properties, and renamed 'assurance' to 'verification'. ([source](https://github.com/wbcsd/data-exchange-protocol/releases), 2025-04-30 to 2025-12-09; plan at https://github.com/wbcsd/data-exchange-protocol/blob/main/RELEASE-PLAN.md)
- **[high]** The Catena-X (Eclipse Tractus-X) PCF aspect model v8.0.0 (18 Jul 2025) added verification/certification v2 sections, prospective PCFs and mass balancing, with names 'aligned with PACT and TfS'. v10.0.0 (release label '26.09') changed precedingPfIds to strings to be 'compliant with PACT'. Automotive and chemical PCF exchange is converging on the PACT data model. ([source](https://github.com/eclipse-tractusx/sldt-semantic-models/blob/main/io.catenax.pcf/RELEASE_NOTES.md), 2025-07-18 to 2026 (label 26.09))
- **[high]** ESG Astraa's own research doc names the supplier-data gap. Supplier portals everywhere are 'survey-and-chase'. The real gap is incentives: showing suppliers 'how primary data beats defaults and protects their buyer relationships'. 'Scope 3 spend-based estimation needs breadth' of factors. CCTS and CBAM reconciliation is done in spreadsheets, and 'auditor workspaces don't exist'. ([source](https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool/blob/master/docs/08-competitive-gaps.md), 2026)
- **[high]** Product fit. The ESG Astraa brief's data capture includes 'upload, email-in, phone camera + OCR, connectors, supplier portal'. Its Scope 3 scope is 'the categories the customer can actually evidence', and it states that one activity record feeds the GHG inventory, BRSR Principle 6, CBAM installation data and CCTS. The esgtools repo's SupplierRecord holds spendUsd (USD, not INR), responseRate and status ('Primary data' | 'Estimated' | 'Escalated'). Its seed data tags supplier rows with methodology 'Supplier factor' and sources such as 'Purchase register' and 'Supplier declaration', with quality flags. The building blocks for data-quality tiers exist; INR spend support is a gap. ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool/master/docs/00-product.md), 2026; local file /home/user/esgtools/lib/types.ts (lines 144-153))
- **[medium]** Emerging competition on supplier-side BRSR. 'Anvil', an Indian SME operations platform, plans a BRSR Core value-chain pack. Suppliers fill one BRSR Core form per period and share it with multiple listed buyers, with Scope 2 auto-computed using CEA 0.710. Planned pricing is Rs 4,990/month for suppliers and Rs 1,49,990/month plus Rs 1,500 per supplier per period for buyers. The doc describes Updapt's portal as top-down, where 'each supplier sees a different vendor's UI per buyer'. Another repo (filebrsr) is building upstream and downstream value-chain coverage tracking. ([source](https://github.com/kenithphilip/Anvil/blob/main/docs/STRATEGIC_BET_07_brsr_value_chain.md), 2026; also https://github.com/iamyadavvikas/filebrsr)
- **[medium]** CCTS context for supplier programmes: about 490 obligated entities have notified GHG emission intensity targets (282 notified in Oct 2025, 208 on 13 Jan 2026). These are the large Indian aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali, pulp and paper, refinery, petrochemical and textile producers whose products sit in buyers' Category 1. Their verified intensity data could become a source of supplier-specific factors. That last point is my inference. ([source](https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2217239&reg=3&lang=2), 2026-01-13 (from brand context; not re-opened this session))

### seo-serp (11 searches run)

- **[high]** I could not inspect live search results in this run. WebSearch refused every query because the session's shared budget was used up (200 of 200 calls). The Firecrawl search and scrape tools returned HTTP 402 (insufficient credits). WebFetch was egress-blocked for every search engine and autocomplete endpoint I tried: google.com, bing.com, duckduckgo.com (both html. and the /ac autocomplete endpoint), search.brave.com, mojeek.com, suggestqueries.google.com and news.google.com. It was also blocked for competitor and regulator sites (thesustainabilitycloud.com, orennow.com, climes.io, sebi.gov.in, pib.gov.in, beeindia.gov.in, eur-lex.europa.eu, ifrs.org, wikipedia.org, zenodo.org, huggingface.co). I therefore did not observe SERP features (AI Overview, People Also Ask, featured snippets, video) for any query, and I state none as observed. The only observed rankings below come from category SERPs that the brand-research pass captured today through WebSearch snippets. Every difficulty and demand rating is labelled as an estimate. The 11 searches counted were GitHub code searches, which were the only search surface that worked. To finish the 25-40-query SERP audit, raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION, add Firecrawl credits, or allow google.com/bing.com in the environment's network settings. ([source](n/a (session tool errors: WebSearch budget 200/200; Firecrawl 402; EGRESS_BLOCKED on search engines)), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Observed SERP (brand pass, 2026-10-01) for 'best BRSR reporting software India 2026': The Sustainability Cloud, Oren, ZeroCarbon, Newtral, Breathe ESG and Sentra rank, mostly with vendor-written listicles and buyer's guides. Vendors rank themselves in their own 'best of' lists. Estimated difficulty for a new domain with almost no backlinks is medium-high, because established SaaS blogs hold year-dated listicles. ([source](https://www.thesustainabilitycloud.com/blog/top-5-brsr-softwares/), 2026-10-01 (observed via search snippets, not full SERP))
- **[medium]** Observed SERP (brand pass) for 'CBAM compliance software for Indian exporters': GreenSutra, The Sustainability Cloud ('Best CBAM Software in India 2026'), CbamTrack, Seasaw, o2log and Encarbonsys rank. The SERP is fragmented across young specialist domains, with no regulator or Big 4 page among those captured. Estimated difficulty: medium. ([source](https://www.thesustainabilitycloud.com/blog/best-cbam-softwares-in-india-2026/), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Observed SERP (brand pass) for 'CCTS compliance software India': only four niche players were captured (Oren, Climate Decode TerraNova, CarbonNeeti, Green Curve). This is the thinnest commercial SERP among ESG Astraa's three regimes. Estimated difficulty: low-medium. ([source](https://climate-decode.com/markets/india-ccts), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Observed SERP (brand pass) for India carbon accounting software for manufacturers: KeyESG, Climes ('Carbon Accounting Software in India (2026): An Honest Comparison'), a Tracxn directory page, The Sustainability Cloud, Sentra, GroundESG and Bharat Carbon. Comparison pages and directories win these queries. Estimated difficulty: medium. ([source](https://www.climes.io/carbon-accounting-software-india), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** ESG Astraa's only proven non-brand ranking is a timely regulatory news post. 'The Carbon Turning Point for Indian Business' (4 June 2026) ranked about eighth for a long-tail query on the Indian Carbon Market portal launch and CCTS targets. Fast, fact-dense explainers on Indian regulation are the most realistic organic entry point. ([source](https://esgastraa.com/insights/blogs/2), 2026-10-01 (brand pass))
- **[high]** New 'why now' trigger for steel: a MoEFCC draft notification, G.S.R. 517(E) dated 26 June 2026, proposes a Third Schedule of GHG emission intensity (GEI) targets under CCTS. I counted 255 unique iron and steel obligated-entity IDs (INSOE…) in it, with targets for compliance years 2025-26 and 2026-27 and a 60-day objection period. It builds on G.S.R. 739(E) of 8 Oct 2025 (aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali, pulp & paper) and G.S.R. 25(E) of 13 Jan 2026 (secondary aluminium, refinery, petrochemical, textile). The gazette text is mirrored in a public CC BY 4.0 dataset repo, which totals 745 facilities across 9 sectors and 26 states/UTs. I could NOT verify whether the steel targets were finalised by 1 Oct 2026. Steel is also India's largest CBAM-exposed good, so this is where CCTS and CBAM overlap most. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), Draft dated 2026-06-26; repo read 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** CBAM rules verified from the text of Regulation (EU) 2025/2083 of 8 Oct 2025, read from a GitHub mirror because EUR-Lex was blocked. Key provisions: (1) Article 2a sets a single 50-tonne annual cumulative net-mass threshold per importer, applying from 1 Jan 2026. (2) Certificates for 2026 imports are sold from 2027 (recital 27). (3) Article 24 requires the annual declaration and surrender by 30 September of the following year. (4) The quarterly holding requirement falls from 80% to 50% (recital 28). (5) Article 8(1) and recital 19: verification by accredited verifiers applies only to actual values, and the Commission publishes default values. (6) Recital 21: where default values are used, the carbon-price deduction can only reference yearly default carbon prices. Points 5 and 6 create a 'default vs actual' decision for every Indian exporter. ([source](https://github.com/legalize-dev/legalize-eu/blob/HEAD/eu/32025R2083.md), Regulation of 2025-10-08; read 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** BRSR Core status, from a secondary source: SEBI circular SEBI/HO/CFD/CFD-PoD-1/P/CIR/2025/42 (28 March 2025) replaced mandatory reasonable assurance with 'assessment or assurance'. The top 1,000 listed entities need it from FY2026-27. Value-chain ESG disclosure became voluntary for the top 250 from FY2025-26 and covers partners at 2% or more of purchases or sales. The original glide path (ICAI study material) was top 150 in FY23-24, top 250 in FY24-25, top 500 in FY25-26 and top 1,000 in FY26-27, with nine BRSR Core attributes. ([source](https://github.com/syntropyearth/syntropyearth-site/blob/HEAD/thinking/brsr-core-assessment-or-assurance-sebi/index.html), Page published 2026-09-02, modified 2026-09-24)
- **[high]** Competitors are already building pages for answer engines on BRSR Core. Syntropy Earth's page 'BRSR Core Assessment or Assurance: What SEBI Changed' (2 Sep 2026) uses question-form H2s and FAQPage schema with questions such as 'Who needs BRSR Core assessment or assurance in FY 2026-27?' and 'Is value chain ESG disclosure mandatory under BRSR?'. This is the format ESG Astraa's companion landing pages should match or beat. ([source](https://github.com/syntropyearth/syntropyearth-site/blob/HEAD/thinking/brsr-core-assessment-or-assurance-sebi/index.html), 2026-09-24)
- **[low]** Proprietary-data opportunity: a public GitHub repo holds text extracts of BRSR filings by financial year (104 files for 2021-22, 553 for 2022-23, 821 for 2023-24, 729 for 2024-25). I ran a crude, unvalidated keyword count on the FY2024-25 set (729 files). 'CBAM' or 'carbon border adjustment' appears in only 5 files, CCTS in 7, 'BRSR Core' in 214 and 'Scope 3' in 521. For FY2023-24 (821 files) the counts are CBAM 6, CCTS 2, BRSR Core 149, Scope 3 557. If validated, these counts could anchor a citable data study showing that listed India Inc. barely mentions CBAM in BRSR despite heavy export exposure. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), Counted 2026-10-01; repo has no README describing provenance)
- **[medium]** For category queries (best BRSR software, India carbon accounting, CBAM software, CCTS software), answer-engine summaries named TSC, Oren, Noa/Newtral, Ecodrisil, Breathe ESG, Climes, Sprih, Updapt, StepChange, Sentra, GroundESG, Bharat Carbon, Credibl, GreenSutra, CbamTrack, Climate Decode and CarbonNeeti. They never named ESG Astraa. The brand SERP is diluted by Astra ESG Solutions (Grand View Research) and by ESG Astraa's own public GitHub build repo. ([source](https://astra.grandviewresearch.com/), 2026-10-01 (brand pass))
- **[medium]** Indian Carbon Market portal launch (21 Mar 2026, Prakriti 2026) and roughly 490 obligated entities across Phase I and II (PIB says 'seven energy-intensive sectors'). Adding the 255 steel facilities from the June 2026 draft gives the 745 / 9-sector figure in the dataset repo. Newer content should reconcile these counts explicitly, which ESG Astraa's blog currently does not ('nine hard-to-abate sectors' with 490 entities). ([source](https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2243377&reg=3&lang=1), PIB 2026 (via brand-pass snippet); dataset repo 2026)
- **[low]** Inference, not observed.
