Big 4 and Tier 2 consultancies (PwC India, Grant Thornton Bharat, KPMG India, SES, Astrum) and regulators (SEBI, PIB/BEE, the European Commission) almost certainly hold the head terms 'BRSR', 'what is BRSR', 'CBAM India' and 'carbon credit trading scheme'. A new domain should target long-tail, dated and data-led queries, where young specialist domains already rank (CBAM and CCTS software SERPs). ([source](https://www.pwc.in/consulting/esg-consulting.html), 2026-10-01)

### competitors-india (34 searches run)

- **[high]** ACCESS LIMITS (read first). The session's WebSearch budget was used up (200 of 200) before this researcher's first query. Firecrawl returned HTTP 402 (out of credits). The egress proxy blocked every competitor, regulator and Big 4 domain tried (crediblesg.com, thesustainabilitycloud.com, sebi.gov.in, pib.gov.in, kpmg.com, duckduckgo). Evidence below therefore comes from: (a) public GitHub repositories, via GitHub code search and WebFetch of github.com; (b) primary-text mirrors on GitHub (BRSR filings, CCTS gazette notifications, SEBI circular feeds) that sibling researchers cloned into the shared scratchpad; (c) SERP observations already recorded in the brand context. Live SERP rankings, competitor white-paper libraries and Big 4 India report titles for 2024-26 could NOT be checked first-hand in this run. To fix this, raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION, add Firecrawl credits, or widen Network access in the environment settings. ([source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** PROPRIETARY-STYLE DATA POINT: CBAM barely appears in BRSRs. A keyword scan of a public corpus of 729 FY2024-25 BRSR text files found 'CBAM' or 'Carbon Border Adjustment' in only 5: DEE Development Engineers, Harsha Engineers, Jai Balaji Industries, Ramkrishna Forgings and Vardhman Textiles. The figure for 821 FY2023-24 files was 6. The corpus is a third-party GitHub dataset, not the full universe, and many large caps (Tata Steel, JSW Steel, UltraTech, Reliance, Hindalco) are missing from it. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 BRSRs, filed around Jul-Aug 2025)
- **[medium]** PROPRIETARY-STYLE DATA POINT: CCTS also barely appears. 'Carbon Credit Trading Scheme' or 'CCTS' appeared in 7 of 729 FY2024-25 BRSRs: BPCL, Crompton Greaves Consumer, Godawari Power & Ispat, Hindustan Zinc, Ircon, JK Lakshmi Cement and TNPL. The FY2023-24 count was 3 of 821. Of 14 listed cement, aluminium, refinery, paper, textile and steel companies found in the corpus, only 3 mention CCTS; SAIL, Vedanta, NALCO, ACC, Ambuja and JK Cement mention neither CCTS nor CBAM. Caveat: these filings came out before the GEI targets were notified on 8 Oct 2025, so the FY2025-26 cycle is the real test. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 BRSRs)
- **[medium]** Industry bodies own the canonical 'how to compute BRSR Core' content. 238 of 729 FY2024-25 BRSRs (about 33%) cite the 'Industry Standards Note (December 2024) on BRSR Core, jointly issued by ASSOCHAM, CII and FICCI'. TCS's BRSR links it to a SEBI circular dated 20 Dec 2024. Vendor content therefore has to build on that note; it cannot replace it. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble/blob/main/2024%20-%2025_txt_files/MOIL_Limited_2024.txt), Dec 2024 note; FY2024-25 filings)
- **[low]** The assurance and assessment market in the BRSR text leans towards certification bodies over the Big 4. Counting firms named within 200 characters of assurance or assessment wording in FY2024-25 BRSRs: TÜV 34, SGS 19, DNV 16, Bureau Veritas 14, Intertek 10, KPMG 10, Grant Thornton 8, EY 7, PwC 5, Deloitte 4. This is an indicative regex count, not a coded dataset. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[high]** Sprih is active as a BRSR independent assessor, not just a SaaS vendor. The India Cements FY2024-25 BRSR states 'Independent assessment has been carried out by Sprih' against the BRSR assessment and assurance questions. That puts it directly against ESG Astraa's advisory-plus-platform hybrid model. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble/blob/main/2024%20-%2025_txt_files/THE_INDIA_CEMENTS_LIMITED_2024.txt), FY2024-25 BRSR)
- **[medium]** Sprih's own content is ungated HTML, aimed at queries ESG Astraa also targets. Known pages: 'BRSR Core vs BRSR Comprehensive' guide, 'CBAM compliance 2026 definitive phase' blog, and a SEBI value-chain relaxation post. An EU-focused 2026 competitive-intelligence scan lists Sprih among CBAM declaration SaaS vendors, alongside CarbonChain, Zevero, Climease and sentra.world. Funding cited at about $3M (Leo Capital-led round per a March 2024 headline). These URLs were cited by third-party documents and not opened in this run. ([source](https://www.sprih.com/blogs/brsr-core-vs-brsr-comprehensive-guide/), 2025-2026)
- **[medium]** Glocert publishes an HTML 'BRSR Core Assurance Readiness Guide'. Assurance-readiness checklists are an occupied content format (Glocert, thefootnotes.in checklist, Syntropy Earth article). Evidence-lineage design is not. ([source](https://www.glocertinternational.com/resources/guides/brsr-core-assurance-readiness-guide/), 2025-2026 (cited in a May 2026 doc))
- **[medium]** Who wins the core non-brand SERPs, per the brand-context checks run earlier in this session. All formats are ungated HTML listicles, comparisons or guides: - BRSR software: The Sustainability Cloud ('Top 5 BRSR softwares'), Oren (BRSR buyer's guide), Newtral, Breathe ESG ('Breathe ESG vs Sprih'), Sentra - CBAM software: TSC ('Best CBAM software in India 2026'), GreenSutra, CbamTrack, Seasaw, o2log, Encarbonsys - Carbon accounting: Climes ('Carbon accounting software in India (2026): an honest comparison') - CCTS: Oren, Climate Decode, CarbonNeeti, Green Curve No gated white paper from an India SaaS vendor was seen ranking for these queries. ([source](https://www.thesustainabilitycloud.com/blog/best-cbam-softwares-in-india-2026/), Observed 2026-10-01 (brand-context research))
- **[low]** 'AI does the BRSR for you' is getting crowded. Public startup repos from 2026 show new entrants: - FileBRSR: AI extraction from annual reports. Prices EY/Deloitte/PwC at ₹10-25L a year and Updapt/ESGRisk.ai at ₹3-8L, and claims '95% cheaper, 99% faster'. - Praxya: 'CA-verifiable data lineage', chemical process factors, ₹3-8L a year. Lists Breathe ESG at ₹5-15L and Sprih at ₹8-20L. - Anvil: supplier-side BRSR Core value-chain forms. All prices are unverified, self-reported claims. ESG Astraa's differentiation therefore has to rest on governance and lineage, not on 'AI' alone. ([source](https://github.com/iamyadavvikas/filebrsr), May 2026)
- **[low]** A competitor doc names Updapt the 'closest direct competitor' for the value-chain partner module. Updapt sells top-down to listed companies, which then onboard their suppliers. The stated gap: each supplier sees a different vendor portal for each buyer. The same doc says Convergence (convergence.com/india-brsr-core) ships an assurer 'Assurance Workspace' and PwC iTrust ships a similar workflow, so assurer-in-the-loop tooling already exists. ([source](https://github.com/kenithphilip/Anvil/blob/main/docs/STRATEGIC_BET_07_brsr_value_chain.md), 2026-05-10)
- **[high]** Small Indian ESG advisories are already optimising for answer engines. Syntropy Earth (a Bengaluru advisory) publishes an llms.txt (updated 11 Sep 2026) and Article JSON-LD keyworded 'BRSR Core assessment or assurance, SEBI circular 28 March 2025, BRSR value chain voluntary, Industry Standards Forum assessment, BRSR Core top 1000 FY 2026-27'. ESG Astraa has no equivalent yet. ([source](https://github.com/syntropyearth/syntropyearth-site/blob/HEAD/llms.txt), 2026-09-11)
- **[high]** CCTS scope is expanding to iron & steel. A MoEFCC DRAFT notification, G.S.R. 517(E) dated 26 June 2026, proposes a Third Schedule of GEI targets for the iron & steel sector (about 255 entities per the CCTS-745 parse), with a 60-day objection window. Added to G.S.R. 739(E) of 8 Oct 2025 (282 entities in aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali and pulp & paper) and G.S.R. 25(E) of 13 Jan 2026 (208 entities in secondary aluminium, refinery, petrochemicals and textiles), the open CCTS-745 dataset counts 745 facilities across 9 sectors. Whether the steel targets have been FINALISED could not be verified. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), Draft 2026-06-26; dataset DOI 10.5281/zenodo.21671592)
- **[medium]** Phase II textiles is large: about 173 textile units are obligated under CCTS (per the CCTS-745 parse), the biggest block in the 13 Jan 2026 notification. Textiles is not a CBAM sector, yet Vardhman Textiles is one of only 5 FY2024-25 BRSRs that mention CBAM. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), 2026-01-13 notification)
- **[medium]** ESG Astraa's only indexed blog says '490 entities across nine sectors'. That conflicts with PIB's 'nearly 490 obligated entities across seven energy-intensive sectors'. It is also now stale: the 26 June 2026 draft would take coverage to roughly 745 entities across 9 sectors. The post needs a dated update; that is itself a quick AEO win. ([source](https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2243377&reg=3&lang=1), 2026)
- **[medium]** The BRSR Core assurance phase-in is confirmed in ICAI study material (BOS 83914): top 500 listed entities in FY2025-26, top 1,000 in FY2026-27. This matches the SEBI framework of 28 Mar 2025 ('assessment or assurance'). ([source](https://github.com/rounakjain2604/fr-study-os), 2025-2026)
- **[medium]** SEBI set up a multi-stakeholder Working Group on 18 Feb 2026 (PR 13/2026) to review the ESG Rating Providers framework. On 27 Feb 2026 it aligned third-party reviewer/certifier norms for green debt securities with the ESG debt securities framework of 5 Jun 2025. Both were read via an LLM-summarised mirror of SEBI feeds, not on sebi.gov.in. ([source](https://www.sebi.gov.in/media-and-notifications/press-releases/feb-2026/sebi-constitutes-working-group-to-review-esg-rating-providers-erps-regulatory-framework_99864.html), 2026-02-18 / 2026-02-27)
- **[medium]** No new BRSR-specific SEBI circular turned up in a mirror of SEBI circulars and regulations covering Jul 2025 to 30 Sep 2026. However, the LODR (Second Amendment) Regulations 2026 of 14 Jul 2026 were not parsed by the mirror, so any BRSR changes in them are UNVERIFIED and need a first-hand check. ([source](https://www.sebi.gov.in/legal/regulations/jul-2026/securities-and-exchange-board-of-india-listing-obligations-and-disclosure-requirements-second-amendment-regulations-2026_102872.html), 2026-07-14)
- **[medium]** GTRI, a think tank, owns the CBAM headline numbers for India, and they are widely syndicated (Business Standard, Times of India, 31 Dec 2025): - CBAM may force Indian steel and aluminium exporters to cut prices by 15-22% - steel and aluminium exports to the EU fell 24.4% in FY25 These were cited via third-party docs and not opened here. ([source](https://www.business-standard.com/industry/news/cbam-may-force-steel-aluminium-firms-cut-prices-gtri-125123101012_1.html), 2025-12-31)
- **[low]** Mongabay India (June 2026) reportedly counts 3,000-4,000 direct and 25,000-30,000 indirect Indian iron & steel exporters affected by CBAM, with MRV costs of about ₹25 lakh a year per firm. This sizes the MSME CBAM audience. It was cited via a third-party doc and not opened. ([source](https://india.mongabay.com/2026/06/european-union-border-tax-tests-indias-small-iron-and-steel-businesses/), 2026-06)
- **[low]** Content-gap signal from an independent 2026 competitive-intelligence scan of CBAM tooling: CBAM is 'a cost-accounting/forecasting problem (certificate liability, provisioning, pass-through pricing) as much as a data problem; crowded on data collection, thin on the finance side.' ([source](https://github.com/JDCH-S/IDAS), 2026)
- **[medium]** Interplay content between CCTS and CBAM (one MRV dataset; CBAM Article 9 deduction for carbon prices paid in the country of origin) shows up mostly in GitHub research repos and exam current-affairs notes, not in vendor white papers. Examples: a 'CCTS & EU CBAM Enterprise Climate Risk Engine' that models CCTS penalties plus CBAM Art. 9, and an Aug 2026 current-affairs note on 'domestic tax rebates against EU CBAM duties'. This is white space for an authoritative, vendor-grade explainer. Legal status of Art. 9 recognition for CCTS: NOT verified. ([source](https://github.com/SaChIn5419/INDIA-CCTS-CARBON-RISK-ENGINE), 2026)
- **[low]** Big 4 and industry-body content could be catalogued only partially: - PwC India's net-zero/ESG page has an 'ESG Reporting and BRSR' tab (site mirror), and PwC runs iTrust. - EY India has an ESG & sustainability assurance services page and has announced use-case expansions of its EY ESG Compass platform (Aug 2024). - CEEW published an open-access (CC BY-NC) Issue Brief, 'Unlocking India's Voluntary Carbon Market' (Aug 2025). - TERI publishes open PDFs. Specific KPMG, EY, Deloitte, PwC and GT Bharat India white-paper titles for 2024-26, and whether they are gated, could NOT be verified in this run. ([source](https://github.com/vmrisck/pwc_in), 2024-2026)
- **[low]** Trade context, low reliability: a 2026 current-affairs dataset says the India-EU FTA was concluded or signed on 27 Jan 2026. How CBAM is treated under the FTA was NOT verified; check before any white paper cites it. ([source](https://github.com/dakguru/ip2026), 2026-01-27)
- **[medium]** Saturated topics (many ungated HTML pieces from TSC, Oren, Sprih, Breathe, Climes, Newtral, CbamTrack, Encarbonsys, CarbonNeeti, Glocert): - What is BRSR / BRSR Core - BRSR Core vs comprehensive - 'Best BRSR / CBAM / carbon accounting software' listicles - CBAM 2026 basics for exporters - CCTS 'complete guide' explainers - assurance-readiness checklists White space (no vendor piece found): - a CCTS×CBAM single-dataset crosswalk - data-driven BRSR disclosure benchmarks on carbon-market readiness - CBAM finance and pricing for CFOs - an evidence-lineage standard for BRSR Core assurance - a supplier 'collect once' standard spanning BRSR value chain + CBAM + Scope 3 - governance of human-approved AI in ESG reporting - facility-level CCTS target-gap analytics using the open gazette data This is a synthesis judgement drawn from the findings above. ([source](https://www.climes.io/carbon-accounting-software-india), 2026-10-01 synthesis)

### competitors-global (29 searches run)

- **[high]** METHOD NOTE, read first. Normal web research was not possible in this run. WebSearch had already hit the session budget (200 of 200 calls), Firecrawl returned 402 (out of credits), and the egress proxy blocked every vendor domain tried (watershed.com, persefoni.com, sweep.net, greenly.earth, workiva.com, pib.gov.in, bing, duckduckgo and wikipedia). Only github.com and raw.githubusercontent.com were reachable. All evidence therefore comes from GitHub-hosted material: (a) api-evangelist repos that mirror vendor blog RSS feeds (titles, URLs, dates, one-paragraph summaries); (b) Broser-ai/Sweep, a full-text scrape of 894 sweep.net pages; (c) vibewatch/startup, a Watershed due-diligence dossier whose evidence URLs were checked as live on 2026-05-19; (d) Gazette PDF copies in prakulhiremath/ccts-745. I could NOT verify any flagship 2024-26 reports from Sphera, Novisto, Position Green (for example an 'ESG 100' index), Dcycle, Salesforce Net Zero Cloud, SAP Green Ledger or IBM Envizi. No backlink counts or search volumes were measured. Claims of 'earns backlinks or citations' below rest on observed third-party references, not link data. To unblock: add these domains to the environment's network allow-list, or top up Firecrawl credits. ([source](https://github.com/api-evangelist/persefoni), 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** Sweep's flagship asset is the 'State of Sustainability in Businesses 2026' report, produced with Sustainability Magazine from a survey of 'more than 120 enterprises globally'. Headline stats: 91% produce annual carbon accounts; 65% finish reporting within 3 months; 69% struggle to collect supplier emissions data; 59% depend on spreadsheets; 32% are resource-constrained; only 31% use AI or automation; only 40% say they are well prepared; 42% report cost savings from better carbon data. Lesson: a small sample (n~120) is enough to anchor a citable, press-friendly state-of report. ([source](https://www.sweep.net/blog/the-state-of-sustainability-in-2026-progress-pressure-and-the-path-forward), 2026-05-11)
- **[high]** Sweep splits that survey into gated regional editions: Global, EU, UK, US and Nordic 'State of Sustainability in Businesses 2026'. The 894-page scrape contains no India or APAC edition. This is the clearest proven format with an India-shaped hole. ([source](https://www.sweep.net/reports/global-state-of-sustainability-in-businesses-2026-report), 2026)
- **[high]** Sweep's earlier press-driving study: an independent Censuswide survey commissioned by Sweep with Capgemini, released 24 Sep 2024. It polled 554 sustainability professionals in the US, UK, France and Germany. Headline: 81% (more than 80%) say they must transform to survive the low-carbon economy; 78% still use spreadsheets; 72% do not track Scope 3; 61% spend 4+ hours a week on data tasks. The release discloses methodology, country split and revenue bands. This is the template for a headline-stat survey that gets press coverage. ([source](https://www.sweep.net/newsroom/81-of-businesses-in-four-major-economies-say-they-wont-survive-in-the-low-carbon-economy-without-transformation-sweep-capgemini-research), 2024-09-24)
- **[high]** Sweep's evergreen lead-gen formats: - Interactive tools: US and UK Sustainability Laws Compass ('3 minutes'), SB 253 Navigator, CSRD Materiality Builder, Interactive Guide CDP 2026. - Sector 'state of carbon accounting' guides for manufacturing, retail, financial services and energy. - An ESG software buyer's guide and RFP guides. - An expert co-authored guide, 'Your guide to US climate reporting, using one dataset' (26 Jun 2026), written with former CARB chair Liane Randolph, former EFRAG SRB chair Patrick de Cambourg and David Carlin. Its 'one dataset, four frameworks' framing is ESG Astraa's 'collect once' thesis, applied to the US. ([source](https://www.sweep.net/guides/us-climate-reporting-guide), 2026-06-26)
- **[medium]** India coverage gap at Sweep, measured by my grep across the 894-page scrape: - 'BRSR' appears on 2 pages, one of which is a one-line entry ('Mandatory ESG disclosures for the top 1000 listed companies… by 2023') in a 15 Sep 2025 disclosure roundup. - 'SEBI' and 'CCTS' appear on 0 pages; 'SSBJ' also returns 0. - 'Singapore exchange'/'SGX' appear on 33 pages and 'AASB S2'/'ASRS' on 10. The scrape date is not stated, but the content runs to mid-2026. ([source](https://github.com/Broser-ai/Sweep), 2026 (scrape, exact date unknown))
- **[medium]** Watershed's thought-leadership stack: - 'State of corporate sustainability 2026: AI edition'. - An AI-emissions guide claiming inference drives more than 90% of lifecycle AI impact and that lower-carbon regions can cut operational AI emissions by 30-80%. - Monthly policy updates (for example the June 2026 update on Simplified ESRS and Mexico's NIS). - An RFP guide. - Analyst badges: 2026 IDC MarketScape leader, Verdantix 2026. The URLs were checked as live on 2026-05-19 by a third-party dossier; I could not read the reports' contents. ([source](https://watershed.com/state-of-corporate-sustainability-ai-2026), 2026)
- **[medium]** Watershed's total-cost-of-ownership piece 'The hidden cost of manual sustainability platforms' (2 Jul 2026) estimates that typical 2-3 person sustainability teams spend 294-700 hours a year (about 7-18 weeks) on manual tasks such as emission-factor mapping and spreadsheet Scope 3 modelling. It argues total cost of ownership, not subscription price, is the right metric. The format suits CFO buyers. ([source](https://watershed.com/blog/the-hidden-cost-of-manual-sustainability-platforms), 2026-07-02)
- **[high]** Watershed's strongest link and citation magnet is an open dataset, not a PDF. Open CEDA (launched May 2025) is a free EEIO model covering 148 countries, 400 sectors and 95% of global GDP. The 2025 version uses base year 2023; it is licensed CC BY-SA, updated annually, and listed in the AWS Open Data Registry with an S3 bucket and explorer. Third-party repos already embed it, for example jianchuanqi/global-lca-asset. Format lesson: a free, licensed, annually refreshed dataset plus a methodology page earns references from developers, academics and AI pipelines. ([source](https://github.com/awslabs/open-data-registry/blob/main/datasets/open-ceda.yaml), 2025-05 (annual updates))
- **[medium]** Persefoni's content engine, from 55 unique blog posts dated 28 Jul 2025 to 31 Aug 2026 in its RSS mirror: - 'Best X software in 2026' listicles: carbon accounting, Scope 3, CDP, ESG, emissions management, decarbonisation, financed emissions, free carbon calculators. - Country ISSB explainers: Singapore, Japan SSBJ, Korea KSDS, Australia ASRS, Canada CSDS, Mexico. Plus California SB 253/261, New York, UK SECR and France Bilan Carbone. - A monthly 'Climate Policy Roundup' (June, July and August 2026) and a Year in Review. - A free Persefoni Pro tier. There are zero posts on India, BRSR, SEBI or CCTS. A grep for 'India' across all mirrored posts from Persefoni, Terrascope, Watershed, Emitwise and Normative returned nothing. Caveat: an RSS mirror may not include every post. ([source](https://github.com/api-evangelist/persefoni/tree/main/blogs), 2025-07 to 2026-08)
- **[medium]** AI governance has become a content and trust theme for global players: - Persefoni: ISO/IEC 42001 certification for AI management (24 Aug 2026); 'AI in Carbon Accounting [+ What to Ask Your Vendor]' (21 Nov 2025); Analytics Agent (5 May 2026); Data Agent (20 Aug 2026). - Watershed: new AI features (24 Jun 2026). - Sweep: a 'Responsible AI in Sustainability Management' guide. - Microsoft: a secondary source reports that, per Microsoft Learn (updated 2026-05-11), Copilot in Sustainability Manager was still in preview and 'doesn't support creating any sustainability data'. ([source](https://www.persefoni.com/blog/iso-iec-42001-certification-ai-management), 2025-11 to 2026-08)
- **[medium]** Terrascope (Singapore-founded, acquired by XeleratedFifty on 26 Feb 2026) runs an APAC-heavy country series titled '<Country>'s Climate Disclosure Rules: What You Need to Know'. Covered: Australia, Hong Kong, Indonesia, Malaysia, New Zealand, Mexico, Switzerland and the UK (Jun-Sep 2026). India is absent. It also publishes 'The 10 best carbon management platforms for enterprise supply chains (2026)', 'Spreadsheet, consultancy, software or Terrascope?', and timely standard explainers: the GHG Protocol LSR Standard; SBTi Net-Zero V2 (26 Jun 2026); 'Scope 3 reporting may become mandatory' (31 Aug 2026). ([source](https://github.com/api-evangelist/terrascope/tree/main/blogs), 2025-09 to 2026-09)
- **[medium]** Global CBAM content is written from the EU-importer or US side, not for Indian exporters: - Terrascope's case study: BBR VT, an importer headquartered in Switzerland, obtained primary data from 50% of suppliers and supplier-specific embedded emissions for 80% of imported goods. - Persefoni: 'EU CBAM: What to Expect' (30 Jan 2026). - Sweep: 'CBAM: what US businesses need to know'. Indian exporters are the data suppliers in these workflows, yet no global player in the sample addresses them. ([source](https://www.terrascope.com/casestudies/bbr-vt-preparing-for-eu-cbam-and-strengthening-carbon-reporting-across-its-supply-chain), 2025-11-06)
- **[medium]** Workiva runs an annual executive survey franchise: - '2025 Executive Benchmark on Integrated Reporting'. A third-party blog cites it: 85% of executives who intended to disclose emissions will proceed regardless of politics; 97% say strong sustainability reporting gives competitive advantage. - '2026 Predictions for Finance, Risk, and Sustainability'. A third-party dossier cites it: 9 in 10 finance, risk and sustainability professionals believe leaders underestimate the reporting risk from fragmented data. This is a CFO-targeted benchmark that third parties cite. Both stats are second-hand; Workiva pages were not reachable. ([source](https://www.workiva.com/resources/2026-predictions-finance-risk-and-sustainability), 2025-2026)
- **[medium]** Microsoft's ungated playbooks travel into AI tooling: - 'Accelerating Sustainability with AI: A Playbook' (Nov 2023) and 'Accelerating Sustainability with AI: Innovations for a Better Future' (Jan 2025, aka.ms/AcceleratingSustainabilitywithAI2025) are linked from Microsoft's Cloud Adoption Framework docs. - A 'sustainable-ai-pdf' folder ships in Azure-Samples/azure-search-sample-data, which Microsoft's RAG and multimodal tutorials use. A third-party demo repo names 'Accelerating Sustainability with AI 2025' as one of its documents. AEO lesson: open, well-structured PDFs with clear titles get reused by AI pipelines. ([source](https://github.com/MicrosoftDocs/cloud-adoption-framework/blob/main/docs/strategy/inform/sustainability.md), 2023-11 / 2025-01)
- **[medium]** Free calculators spread through partner hubs: - Normative underpins the SME Climate Hub 'Business Carbon Calculator' (per Sweep's 2026 US software listicle). - Emitwise blogged 'SME Climate Hub Launches Free Carbon Measurement Suite' (3 Apr 2025). - Greenly runs 'Leaf Media' data stories (for example 'The environmental impact of artificial intelligence'), which third-party repos cite, plus an ebook and webinar library and a CBAM product page. Calculators and data stories are the formats outsiders reference most in the GitHub-visible corpus. ([source](https://emitwise.com/resources/blog/sme-climate-hub-launches-free-carbon-measurement-suite/), 2025-04-03)
- **[low]** Glossary and academy pages rank. In the OriginalityAI AI-citation study's July 2025 Google SERP samples: - Plan A Academy's 'difference between carbon-neutral, net-zero and climate positive' page ranked #1 for one query, though it was not cited in that AI Overview. - Plan A's climate-risk page and Persefoni's climate-risk explainer also appeared in organic results. This is weak, anecdotal evidence that evergreen definitional hubs earn visibility. ([source](https://github.com/OriginalityAI/ai-citation-study), 2025-07)
- **[medium]** Analyst reprints are standard authority content. Sweep says it 'led all 17 vendors' in the IDC MarketScape: Worldwide Carbon Accounting and Management Applications 2026 (blog dated 6 May 2026), while Watershed also claims Leader status in the same report. Verdantix Green Quadrants (ESG reporting 2025; carbon management 2026) are cited the same way by Sweep, Watershed and Sphera. Indian vendors cannot yet use this lever, so it is not a white-paper format for ESG Astraa. ([source](https://www.sweep.net/blog/understanding-the-2026-idc-marketscape-for-carbon-accounting-and-management), 2026-05-06)
- **[medium]** The global field is consolidating: - Terrascope acquired by XeleratedFifty (26 Feb 2026). - Emitwise acquired by Green Project Technologies (July 2025, per the api-evangelist profile). - Diginex signed a non-binding MOU to acquire Plan A (2 Dec 2025, GlobeNewswire item mirrored on GitHub). None of these signals new India investment. ([source](https://www.terrascope.com/blog/terrascope-acquisition), 2025-07 to 2026-02)
- **[high]** India why-now, from primary Gazette copies (read first-hand from PDFs mirrored on GitHub): - G.S.R. 739(E), 8 Oct 2025: GHG Emission Intensity Target Rules for aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali and pulp & paper (282 facility IDs). - G.S.R. 25(E), 13 Jan 2026: Amendment Rules adding secondary aluminium, petroleum refinery, petrochemicals and textiles (208 IDs). - G.S.R. 517(E), 26 Jun 2026: a DRAFT notification by MoEFCC under the Environment (Protection) Act proposing GEI targets for iron & steel (255 IDs, including SAIL plants and AM/NS India), with a 60-day comment window. Total: 745 facilities. Iron & steel is also a CBAM sector, so CCTS and CBAM now overlap on the same Indian plants. Whether the steel targets have been finalised was NOT verified as of 2026-10-01. An independent open dataset of these facilities already exists (CCTS-745, Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.21671592, CC BY 4.0). ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745/blob/main/274012.pdf), 2025-10-08 / 2026-01-13 / 2026-06-26)
- **[low]** Pilot of a proprietary-data hook, from my keyword count on a third-party corpus of BRSR text files: - FY2024-25: 729 filings. 'reasonable assurance' appears in 198; 'Scope 3' in 521 (this partly reflects template wording); 'CBAM'/'carbon border' in only 6 (DEE Development, Harsha Engineers, Jai Balaji, Ramkrishna Forgings, Vardhman Special Steels, Vardhman Textiles); 'CCTS'/'carbon credit trading' in 8. - FY2023-24: 821 filings, with 161, 557, 7 and 3 respectively. Caveats: the corpus is incomplete (Tata Steel, JSW Steel, Hindalco and UltraTech are absent), text extraction is unvalidated, and keyword presence is not disclosure quality. Even so, it shows that a BRSR disclosure index built from public filings is feasible. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2023-24 / FY2024-25 filings)

### voc-questions (35 searches run)

- **[high]** METHOD LIMIT (read first). I could not reach the primary VoC channels the task names. WebSearch returned 'used its web search budget (200 of 200)'. Firecrawl search returned HTTP 402 (out of credits; the user needs to add credits). The egress proxy blocked WebFetch to reddit.com, quora.com, linkedin.com, caclubindia.com, icsi.edu, icai.org, sebi.gov.in, youtube.com, climes.io, duckduckgo.com, bing.com and suggestqueries.google.com. The GitHub search API through gh is also restricted. To unblock, change Network access in the cloud environment settings (environment menu in the session title bar, then Edit) or add those hosts. The usable channel was GitHub: GitHub code search, raw.githubusercontent.com and git clone. So the questions and pain points below come from proxies, not Reddit, Quora or LinkedIn threads: (a) regulator FAQs mirrored on GitHub, (b) Indian ESG and CBAM vendor FAQ and pain copy in public repos, (c) companies' own wording in about 1,550 BRSR filings (a GitHub text corpus), and (d) Economic Times headlines and summaries saved in a GitHub news archive. No Reddit, Quora, LinkedIn, CA Club India or People Also Ask boxes were read directly. No search volumes were measured. ([source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** Near-verbatim exporter pain (supplier data chain). EEPC, the engineering exports body, asked the government to mandate accredited carbon-emission data from raw-material suppliers. ET's summary: raw material suppliers 'need to be able to provide accredited carbon emission data but they're unable to provide that due to which exporters can't have test certificates'. EEPC's Pankaj [surname truncated in the source] is quoted: 'We need accredited carbon emission data from them otherwise we can't have test certificates'. ET also says about 70% of India's engineering exports to the EU come from MSMEs. I read the ET headline and summary through the GitHub news archive (abhishekfolder-spec/my-news, 2026-09-07 and 2026-09-08 pages), not the ET page itself. ([source](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/economy/foreign-trade/engineering-goods-exporters-seek-mandate-on-carbon-emission-certificate-under-eu-norms/articleshow/133830040.cms), 2026-09-07)
- **[medium]** The government's own CBAM awareness agenda shows the question clusters exporters need answered. ET summary: sessions covered 'applicability and covered products, obligations for exporters, calculation of embedded emissions, data collection and reporting requirements, and accreditation and verification mechanisms', with iron and steel case studies. Read via the GitHub news archive (2026-08-19 page). ([source](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/economy/foreign-trade/govt-organises-awareness-session-on-eu-cbam-for-exporters/articleshow/133329985.cms), 2026-08-19)
- **[medium]** Verification capacity and a common misconception. ET (16 Sep 2026) reports that India set up a committee to help exporters meet EU CBAM requirements and that 'this month, Indian verifiers are granted access to the EU's carbon tax registry'. It also says 'from September 2027, domestic exporters will be mandated to submit returns'. That last framing conflicts with the CBAM design, where the EU authorised declarant files and surrenders by 30 Sep 2027 and the non-EU operator supplies installation data. This is a confusion an AEO answer can correct. Read via the GitHub news archive (2026-09-16 page). ([source](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/economy/foreign-trade/india-ramps-up-preparedness-for-european-unions-carbon-tax-verifiers-begin-eu-registry-access/articleshow/134269246.cms), 2026-09-16)
- **[medium]** Carbon-price recognition is the newest buyer question. ET reported on 9-10 Sep 2026 that the UK will recognise India's CCTS, so British importers can seek carbon-price relief under the UK CBAM. On 1 Aug 2026 ET reported that the India-EU FTA includes a 'dedicated framework' or annexure on CBAM, under which 'India and the EU will engage on taking into account carbon prices paid within India', with support aimed at SMEs. Both read via the GitHub news archive. I did not verify the legal texts of the UK recognition or the FTA annex. ([source](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/economy/foreign-trade/uk-recognises-indias-ccts-carbon-price-to-be-accepted-under-cbam-regime-comm-secy/articleshow/133966978.cms), 2026-09-10)
- **[medium]** Geopolitical uncertainty is now part of the buyer conversation. India and 17 other members reserved third-party rights in Russia's WTO dispute against EU CBAM, and ET says the WTO Dispute Settlement Body agreed to establish a panel (ET, 28-29 Sep 2026). BRICS leaders opposed 'punitive, discriminatory' carbon border taxes (ET, 13 Sep 2026). Both read via the GitHub news archive. Exporters will reasonably ask 'will CBAM be struck down or delayed?', and the honest answer is that compliance obligations run regardless. ([source](https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/economy/foreign-trade/india-reserves-third-party-rights-in-russia-eu-cbam-trade-dispute-in-wto-official/articleshow/134488568.cms), 2026-09-28)
- **[high]** The European Commission's CBAM Q&A is a regulator-compiled list of real stakeholder questions. The mirrored version is dated 24 Oct 2024 (transitional period). Exporter-voiced questions in it include 'I am an installation operator outside the EU. How can I best share data with EU reporting declarants?', 'My supplier is not sending me the necessary information before the report is due. What should I do?', 'Which emission factors for electricity should be used to determine indirect emissions?', 'Can market-based certificates (Guarantee of Origin, Renewable Energy Certificates, etc.) be used to justify the use of actual emission factors?', 'How will the carbon price paid in a third country be discounted from the CBAM?' and 'What are the default values? How does this work?'. I read it via the GitHub mirror NirDiamant/GenAI_Agents. The current definitive-period Q&A on taxation-customs.ec.europa.eu could not be opened (host blocked). ([source](https://github.com/NirDiamant/GenAI_Agents/blob/HEAD/data/CBAM_Questions%20and%20Answers.txt), 2024-10-24 (definitive-period update not verified))
- **[medium]** Vendor FAQ on the importer side shows the same supplier-data anxiety. Kinzoku (NL) asks 'What if my supplier cannot provide actual emissions data?', 'Do I need to become a CBAM declarant myself?', 'Can I use the same emissions data for multiple shipments?' and 'What happens if I do not surrender certificates on time?'. It lists mill evidence buyers want ('Mill test certificates', 'Heat numbers linked to chemical analysis', 'Continuous casting records'). Its dates are 31 Mar 2026 for the declarant-application grace deadline and 30 Sep 2027 for first surrender of 2026 certificates, with a 2026 CBAM factor of 97.5%. These dates are secondary and not re-verified against the EC. ([source](https://github.com/ArmaanSinghThakur/kinzoku-website/blob/HEAD/content/blog/cbam-2026-complete-guide-steel-importers.md), 2026 (undated page))
- **[medium]** Indian exporter-side vendor copy names the paperwork pain. ExportOps AI's problem statement: 'EU buyers asking for CBAM and origin proof — nowhere to file it'. Sylithe's FAQ targets 'What is the best MRV platform in India?' and 'Can Sylithe help Indian exporters with CBAM and Scope 3 reporting?'. Both signal that Indian vendors are already chasing these answer-engine queries. ([source](https://github.com/abhinavsinha00/ExportOps-AI/blob/HEAD/app/page.js), 2026)
- **[medium]** BRSR Core questions after SEBI's 2025 reset, as phrased in a 2 Sep 2026 explainer (Syntropy Earth): 'Is reasonable assurance of BRSR Core still mandatory?', 'Who needs BRSR Core assessment or assurance in FY 2026-27?', 'Is value chain ESG disclosure mandatory under BRSR?', 'How should a company choose between assessment and assurance?' and 'Is assessment a softer check than assurance?'. Facts it reports: circular SEBI/HO/CFD/CFD-PoD-1/P/CIR/2025/42 (28 Mar 2025) allows assessment or assurance. Value-chain disclosure becomes voluntary from FY2025-26, with partners defined as those at 2% or more of purchases or sales and coverage cappable at 75%. Value-chain assessment or assurance is voluntary from FY2026-27. The glide path is top 150 (FY23-24), 250 (FY24-25), 500 (FY25-26) and 1,000 (FY26-27). It cites a SEBI master circular of 30 Jan 2026. I read this via the publisher's GitHub repo; sebi.gov.in was blocked, so the SEBI texts were not read first-hand. ([source](https://github.com/syntropyearth/syntropyearth-site/blob/HEAD/thinking/brsr-core-assessment-or-assurance-sebi/index.html), 2026-09-02)
- **[high]** Verbatim BRSR pain points in companies' own filings (Scope 3 and value chain). From the FY2023-24 and FY2024-25 BRSR text corpus on GitHub: 'The Company is in the process of initiating data collection to calculate Scope 3 emissions.' (Carysil, FY24). 'For this reporting year, MBFSL has not computed the scope 3 emissions.' (Mrs. Bectors, FY24). 'NLCIL is in the process of collating data for accounting scope 3 emissions' (NLC India, FY24). 'No measurement activity of scope 3 emissions was conducted in current and previous financial year' (Chaman Lal Setia Exports, FY25). 'Value chain partners have not been assessed for environmental impacts.' (Balmer Lawrie, Shree Digvijay Cement, Anjani Portland Cement and others). 'Data on Green Credit generation or procurement by our top ten value chain partners is not available.' (Nuvama, FY25). Using a value-chain 'not assessed / not available / in process' phrase pattern, 26 of 729 FY2024-25 filings matched. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2023-24 and FY2024-25 filings (corpus committed Feb 2026))
- **[low]** Indicative assurance-readiness signal (heuristic parse; low confidence, not publishable as-is). For the Scope 1+2 'independent assessment/evaluation/assurance' note in FY2024-25 filings, my parser classified 423 of 729 files: 247 'Yes' and 176 'No' (about 58% yes). The rest were unparseable. Of the 260 filings where the Scope 3 answer could be classified, 108 gave a figure and 70 said NA, not computed or in process. The corpus is a sample whose selection is unknown. A clean XBRL-based rerun would make this a credible proprietary statistic. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 filings)
- **[medium]** Disclosure gap on the regimes ESG Astraa sells into. In the same corpus, only 5 of 729 FY2024-25 BRSR filings mention CBAM (6 of 821 in FY2023-24), and only 6 mention CCTS or the Carbon Credit Trading Scheme. Those 6 include BPCL, Godawari Power & Ispat, Hindustan Zinc and JK Lakshmi Cement; SAIL, NALCO and Vedanta filings in the corpus do not mention CBAM. Verbatim examples: Ramkrishna Forgings, 'Undertaken CBAM Reporting for European Customers in the Financial Year 2024-25'. Harsha Engineers, 'If unaddressed CBAM leads to financial penalties'. JK Lakshmi Cement says its Udaipur unit moved from PAT and 'recently got included in 1 Cycle of CCTS with baseline year 2023-24 and target years 2025-26 and 2026-27'. Caveat: text-extraction coverage varies by file. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble/blob/HEAD/2024%20-%2025_txt_files/Ramkrishna_Forgings_Limited_2024.txt), FY2024-25 filings)
- **[high]** CCTS scope expansion (primary gazette text mirrored on GitHub). G.S.R. 517(E), dated 26 June 2026, is a DRAFT Third Schedule that proposes GHG emission intensity targets for the Iron and Steel sector, with a 60-day objection period. I counted 255 steel registration IDs (INSOE…), with compliance-year 2026-27 targets; for example, the AM/NS India Hazira baseline is 2.2701 and the target 2.1695 tCO2e per tonne of equivalent product. Added to G.S.R. 739(E) of 8 Oct 2025 (aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali, pulp & paper) and G.S.R. 25(E) of 13 Jan 2026 (secondary aluminium, refinery, petrochemical, textile), the CCTS-745 dataset counts 745 facilities across 9 sectors. A secondary knowledge base dated 19 Aug 2026 still describes steel as being in 'draft amendment status'. Whether it has been finalised could NOT be verified. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), 2026-06-26 (finalisation unverified))
- **[low]** Unverified CCTS market-infrastructure dates from a secondary knowledge base (CarbonAlpha SIH KB, snapshot 19 Aug 2026): CERC trading regulations for Carbon Credit Certificates (Regulation No. 205, Gazette 27 Apr 2026); BEE approved-methodology list updated 7 Jul 2026 (12 offset methodologies); BEE Detailed Procedure for Compliance Mechanism v1.0 (Jul 2024). Treat these as leads to verify on cercind.gov.in and beeindia.gov.in (both unreachable here). Whether CCC trading has actually started is still unverified. ([source](https://github.com/Max-Rex-2006/temp/blob/HEAD/CARBONALPHA_SIH_MVP_MASTER_KNOWLEDGE_BASE(2).md), 2026-08-19)
- **[medium]** CCTS questions as Indian vendors phrase them (CarbonCounsel CCTS Tracker FAQ): 'Is my company covered under CCTS?', 'What sectoral target applies?', 'What is my reporting obligation?', 'What happens if there is a shortfall?', 'Can my project generate credits?' and 'Can I buy or sell carbon credit certificates?'. A CCTS persona spec lists 'What is my modelled carbon position?', 'What is my projected gap/surplus under the selected target scenario?', 'What evidence/MRV gaps exist?' and 'Buy vs Build vs Hybrid'. ([source](https://github.com/adisharma1976-cloud/carboncounsel/blob/HEAD/src/pages/CCTSTracker.tsx), 2026)
- **[medium]** BRSR cost and Excel pain as framed by a direct competitor (FileBRSR; vendor claims, unverified): 'Consultants send Excel questionnaires, compile over months'; '337 datapoints to fill (279 Essential + 58 Leadership)'; 'Manual compilation takes 4–8 weeks'; 'Consultants charge ₹5–15L per company per year'. On the supplier side: '5–10 different questionnaires from different buyers, all different formats', 'No ESG team' and 'Risk of losing contracts'. Price anchors: Growth ₹49,999/yr; 'Assurance-Ready' from ₹2,00,000/yr. Its FAQ includes 'How is this different from consultants?' and 'How do supplier assessments work?'. ([source](https://github.com/iamyadavvikas/filebrsr/blob/HEAD/frontend/src/app/page.tsx), 2026)
- **[medium]** Assurance-readiness pain as stated by an India advisory site (Northrop India, BRSR Core insight): 'Aggregating verifiable ESG data across decentralized business units often reveals critical gaps in internal control systems'; 'Requirement to establish SOX-style controls for environmental and social metrics'; 'Most firms lack the audit-ready documentation required for ISAE 3000 assurance, leading to qualified opinions.' ([source](https://github.com/Northrop-man/northrop-india/blob/HEAD/src/pages/insights/BrsrCoreAssurance.jsx), 2025-2026 (undated))
- **[medium]** Emission-factor confusion is a recurring practitioner question. An India Scope 2 guide (3 Jul 2025) carries these FAQs: 'What is the most reliable emission factor to use in India?', 'How do I handle electricity consumption from multiple states?', 'Can I use international emission factors for Indian operations?', 'How do I account for transmission and distribution losses?', 'What should I do if emission factors change between reporting periods?' and 'What documentation is required for BRSR reporting?'. The guide publishes state factors without citing a CEA database version, which is a content-quality gap ESG Astraa can fill. ([source](https://github.com/VinothrajaT3/threadsafe/blob/HEAD/src/content/blog/how-to-calculate-scope-2-emissions-india.md), 2025-07-03)
- **[medium]** Cost pressure figure buyers will ask about: GTRI estimates 15-22% CBAM price pressure on Indian steel and aluminium exporters (Business Standard, 31 Dec 2025, as cited in a GitHub-hosted market-research note). The same note claims un-verified plants face default values '30 to 80% above actual'. That claim is unverified and should not be repeated without the EC default-value tables. ([source](https://www.business-standard.com/industry/news/cbam-may-force-steel-aluminium-firms-cut-prices-gtri-125123101012_1.html), 2025-12-31)
- **[medium]** Pain-point frequency across the sources gathered (counts of independent sources showing each pain; qualitative proxy, not volume): supplier/precursor data 7 (EEPC/ET, EC Q&A, Kinzoku, FileBRSR, BRSR filings, ExportOps, Syntropy); assurance readiness and evidence 5 (Syntropy, Northrop, FileBRSR, BRSR filings, ET verifiers); CBAM paperwork and verification 5 (ET awareness session, ET verifiers, Kinzoku, ExportOps, EC Q&A); emission factors 3 (threadsafe, EC Q&A electricity factors, BRSR filings 'emission factor' in 108 of 729 FY25 files); CCTS coverage and targets 4 (gazette, CarbonCounsel, JK Lakshmi filing, CarbonAlpha KB); cost 3 (FileBRSR, GTRI, Kinzoku); Excel or manual compilation 2 (FileBRSR, Northrop). Supplier data and assurance evidence are the strongest clusters. ([source](https://github.com/abhishekfolder-spec/my-news/tree/HEAD/docs/archive), 2025-07 to 2026-09)

### sectors-india (2 searches run)

- **[high]** RESEARCH LIMITATION (read first): The shared WebSearch budget was already used up when this task started (200 of 200). My 3 WebSearch calls were refused, and Firecrawl search and scrape returned 402 'insufficient credits'. The egress proxy blocked WebFetch for pib.gov.in, egazette.gov.in, beeindia.gov.in, sebi.gov.in, eur-lex.europa.eu, climes.io, thesustainabilitycloud.com, ieefa.org, wikipedia, zenodo and huggingface. Only github.com and raw.githubusercontent.com could be reached, and I ran 2 GitHub repository searches there. So the sector analysis rests on: (a) Gazette CCTS notifications mirrored on GitHub (primary text, which I parsed); (b) a GitHub mirror of about 730 FY2024-25 BRSR filings, which I keyword-scanned; (c) facts verified by the brand-context researcher (PIB, EC, SEBI URLs); (d) GitHub repos that quote Eurostat, CEA and Ministry of Steel data, which I did not re-query. The following could NOT be re-verified this session: NHS supplier roadmap dates, the EU ESPR/textile and Battery Regulation timelines, EUDR application dates, any CBAM downstream-scope extension, CBAM default values for India, IGBC/GRIHA rule changes, and whether the steel draft has been finalised. To fix this, add the hosts above to the environment's network allowlist and add Firecrawl credits. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** CCTS Phase I (G.S.R. 739(E), dated 8 Oct 2025; Gazette CG-DL-E-09102025-266804) set GHG emission intensity (GEI) targets for 282 entities: aluminium 13, cement 186 (131 integrated plants plus 55 grinding units), chlor-alkali 30 and pulp & paper 53. The baseline year is 2023-24, and the compliance years are 2025-26 and 2026-27. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), 2025-10-08 (gazette text read via GitHub mirror))
- **[high]** CCTS Phase II (G.S.R. 25(E), dated 13 Jan 2026, published 16 Jan 2026; Gazette CG-DL-E-16012026-269375) added 208 entities. These are 3 secondary aluminium, 21 petroleum refineries, 11 petrochemical crackers and 173 textile units. The textile units split into 60 spinning, 46 composite, 34 processing and 33 fibre. Together with Phase I this gives the 490 obligated entities PIB reports. ([source](https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2217239&reg=3&lang=2), 2026-01-13)
- **[high]** Iron & steel is in a DRAFT third schedule: G.S.R. 517(E), MoEFCC draft notification dated 26 June 2026 (Gazette CG-DL-E-02072026-274012, digitally signed 2 July 2026). It names 255 iron & steel obligated entities with registration IDs INSOE0001-0256, a 2023-24 baseline GEI, and a target for compliance year 2026-27 only (no 2025-26 target). The objection period is 60 days. I could not verify whether it has been finalised as of 1 Oct 2026. A Sept 2026 third-party research note also still treats it as draft. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745/blob/main/274012.pdf), 2026-06-26 / 2026-07-02)
- **[medium]** My computation from the parsed gazette schedules (indicative; parsing errors possible): - Iron & steel draft: about 359 MtCO2e of 2023-24 baseline emissions, implying about 20 MtCO2e of reductions by FY2026-27 (average intensity cut about 5.6%). - Steel would grow CCTS-covered baseline emissions by about 75%, from about 480 Mt to about 839 Mt. - 201 of the 255 steel entities emit 0.1-1 Mt (median about 0.3 Mt). This is a long tail of mid-size DRI, sponge iron and secondary producers. - Steel clusters: Chhattisgarh 62, Odisha 61, Karnataka 33, West Bengal 25. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), computed 2026-10-01 from 2026-06-26 draft)
- **[medium]** Sector comparison, also my computation from the gazette tables: - Cement, integrated plants: 131 entities, about 260 Mt baseline, average cut 2.7% by FY2026-27; mostly at least 1 Mt each. - Aluminium: 16 entities, about 75 Mt, average cut 4.8%. Primary smelter baseline GEI is about 13.9-15.7 tCO2e/t. - Refineries: 21 entities, about 82 Mt. - Textiles: 173 entities, about 18 Mt, average cut 6.5% (among the steepest). 127 of 173 units emit under 0.1 Mt. Clusters: Rajasthan 40, Gujarat 35, Tamil Nadu 17, Dadra & Nagar Haveli 17. - Pulp & paper: 53 entities (20 in Uttar Pradesh). - Chlor-alkali: 30 entities (12 in Gujarat). Textiles, pulp & paper and chlor-alkali are mid-market-heavy, with weak in-house MRV capacity (inference). ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), computed 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** The GEI Target Rules (G.S.R. 739(E)) attach costs to data gaps: - Rule 4: if an obligated entity fails to submit documents, its achieved GEI is taken as its baseline GEI. The whole target gap then becomes a shortfall it must cover by buying Carbon Credit Certificates. - Rule 6: CPCB imposes environmental compensation equal to twice the average CCC trading price on any shortfall, payable within 90 days. - Credits issued or certificates purchased = (difference between target and achieved GEI) x equivalent product output. This gives every sector white paper a defensible 'cost of missing data' hook. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), 2025-10-08)
- **[high]** Listed obligated names that overlap BRSR filers: - Textiles schedule: Vardhman, Trident, Welspun, Arvind, KPR, Sangam, RSWM, Nahar, Banswara, Raymond, Indo Count, Himatsingka, Grasim and Reliance units. - Steel draft: JSW, Tata Steel, SAIL plants (Bhilai, Bokaro, Rourkela), AM/NS Hazira, RINL, Jindal Stainless, Shyam Metalics, Jai Balaji, Electrosteel, Godawari, Sunflag, Welspun, Prakash, Sarda. So the same companies face both CCTS and BRSR, and many also face CBAM or EU buyer demands. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), 2026)
- **[medium]** Keyword scan of a GitHub mirror of FY2024-25 BRSR filings (700 usable reports) and FY2023-24 (779): - CBAM was mentioned in only 6 (FY24-25) and 7 (FY23-24) filings. - CCTS or 'Indian carbon market' was mentioned in 7 and 2. - Only 2 of 27 steel-classified FY24-25 filers mentioned CBAM (Jai Balaji, Vardhman Special Steels). No aluminium-classified filer did. This is a large disclosure-readiness gap. Caveats: the corpus is a subset, not all 1,000 filers; the scan is keyword-based; FY24-25 reports were mostly published before the Oct 2025 notifications. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 filings (published mid-2025))
- **[high]** Across both BRSR years, CBAM appears more often in downstream engineering and auto-component filings than in steelmakers' filings: Ramkrishna Forgings, Minda Corporation, Wheels India, Harsha Engineers, DEE Development Engineers (piping) and Thejo Engineering. Ramkrishna Forgings' FY2024-25 BRSR says it 'Undertaken CBAM Reporting for European Customers in the Financial Year 2024-25'. CBAM data requests are reaching Tier-1/Tier-2 component exporters. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** Textile and apparel buyer pressure shows in BRSRs: - ZDHC, Higg, OEKO-TEX or GOTS appear in 9 of 25 textile/apparel/leather-classified FY24-25 filings (Trident, Sangam, Vardhman Textiles, Gokaldas, Kitex, Nandan Denim, Lux, Jindal Worldwide, Axita). - Only Vardhman Textiles mentions CBAM. It names customer sustainability demands and 'Eco-design Directives and CBAM' as risks. - Gokaldas Exports reports 87% export turnover to US/European brands. - None of these textile filings mentions CCTS, although many of the same groups are CCTS-obligated. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[high]** Tyre makers CEAT, Balkrishna Industries and TVS Srichakra cite EU Deforestation Regulation (EUDR) compliance for natural rubber in their FY2024-25 BRSRs. EU traceability rules are reaching Indian auto supply chains outside CBAM. I did not verify EUDR application dates this session. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** Real estate & construction: - 'Embodied carbon' appears in about 10 of 700 FY24-25 filings (4 of 779 in FY23-24). Most are developers: Godrej Properties, Max Estates, Marathon Nextgen, Aditya Birla Real Estate, Signatureglobal, J. Kumar Infraprojects. - IGBC, GRIHA or LEED appear in 11 of 51 real estate/construction-classified filings. - Godrej Properties says construction has 'a high embodied carbon footprint' and that it has set an SBTi supply-chain Scope 3 target. Demand is emerging, but measurement practice is still immature. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[high]** Company BRSRs show the PAT-to-CCTS transition and the next sectors in the pipeline: - JK Lakshmi Cement says its Udaipur unit left the PAT cycles and was included in CCTS cycle 1 with a 2023-24 baseline and 2025-26/2026-27 targets. - Hindustan Zinc says CCTS targets 'are yet to be assigned' and a BEE baseline study is underway. Non-ferrous metals beyond aluminium are a likely next wave (inference). ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25)
- **[medium]** EU imports of CBAM-covered goods from India, from Eurostat Comext DS-045409 as compiled in a public repo (I did not re-query): - Iron & steel: EUR 4,755.9M (2023), 4,610.7M (2024), 3,649.7M (2025, -20.8%). - Hot-rolled flat (7208): -56% in 2025. - 2024 downstream lines: seamless tubes 7304 EUR 205.9M, fittings 7307 EUR 184.8M, structures 7308 EUR 133.4M, tubes 7306 EUR 127.6M. - Aluminium: EUR 1,666.1M (2023), 835.7M (2024), 903.9M (2025). ([source](https://github.com/herrrickshaw/eu-ets-compliance-platform), 2023-2025 data, compiled Sept 2026)
- **[high]** CBAM's definitive period started 1 Jan 2026. Other key dates and rules: - 50-tonne annual de minimis threshold. - CBAM certificate sales start 1 Feb 2027. - First annual declaration and surrender for 2026 imports is due by 30 Sep 2027. - Covered goods: iron & steel, aluminium, cement, fertilisers, hydrogen, electricity. All of these were verified by the brand-context researcher via EC pages. ([source](https://taxation-customs.ec.europa.eu/news/reminder-cbam-goes-live-1-january-2026-2025-12-23_en), 2025-12-23)
- **[low]** The Ministry of Steel Green Steel Initiative reportedly certified 12.4 MMT of green steel (threshold below 2.2 tCO2e per tonne of finished steel) across 90 units as of 31 Mar 2026. I saw this only through a third-party repo citing https://steel.gov.in/green-steel-initiative; I did not read the ministry page. ([source](https://github.com/herrrickshaw/eu-ets-compliance-platform), 2026-03-31)
- **[medium]** CEA's CO2 Baseline Database v21.0 (Nov 2025) reportedly gives an FY2024-25 weighted-average grid emission factor of 0.710 tCO2/MWh, with a combined margin of 0.736. I saw this through a third-party repo citing https://cea.nic.in/wp-content/uploads/baseline/2025/12/User_Guide_V_21.0.pdf. It is relevant to Scope 2 for every BRSR and CCTS filer. ([source](https://github.com/herrrickshaw/eu-ets-compliance-platform), 2025-11)
- **[medium]** BRSR Core assessment or assurance applies to the top 500 listed entities from FY2025-26 and the top 1,000 from FY2026-27, under the SEBI circular of 28 Mar 2025 as cited in brand context. Value-chain disclosures remain voluntary. This creates cross-sector demand in 2026-27 for evidence-grade data, especially in pharma, auto, FMCG and real estate, which lack CCTS obligations. ([source](https://www.sebi.gov.in/sebi_data/faqfiles/apr-2025/1745399101865.pdf), 2025-03-28)
- **[low]** Competition for SERPs and answers, as observed by the brand-context researcher (not re-checked): - Generic 'CBAM for Indian exporters' content is crowded: The Sustainability Cloud, Encarbonsys, Seasaw, GreenSutra, CbamTrack, o2log. - So is generic 'CCTS India' content: CarbonNeeti, Climate Decode, Green Curve, Climes, Oren. - I could not check whether sector-specific guides exist (steel CCTS draft schedule, textile GEI targets, CBAM for forgings and tubes, embodied carbon with Indian plant data). They are probably thin, given how recent the notifications are (inference). ([source](https://www.thesustainabilitycloud.com/blog/best-cbam-softwares-in-india-2026/), 2026 (brand-context SERP snapshot))
- **[medium]** Practitioner and academic interest in facility-level CCTS data is rising: - An open CCTS-745 dataset (Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.21671592, Aug 2026) consolidates all 745 facilities. - GitHub prototypes include a cement CCTS gap analysis (May 2026), a JSW CCTS optimisation (Mar 2026) and a CCTS+CBAM risk engine (Aug 2026). The raw facility list is therefore not exclusive. ESG Astraa's hook has to be analysis, segmentation and data-readiness benchmarks, not the list itself. ([source](https://github.com/sneha-tatiwala/carbon-intelligence-india), 2026-03 to 2026-08)
- **[medium]** Sector pain ranking for 2026-27 (my synthesis): 1. Iron & steel: CCTS draft for 255 plants plus CBAM definitive plus green steel. Highest pain and best fit. 2. Downstream steel and aluminium articles (auto and engineering components): CBAM data requests via EU customers. 3. Textiles: 173 CCTS units plus EU brand demands. 4. Aluminium: CCTS plus CBAM, but few large players, so a smaller mid-market buyer pool. 5. Cement: CCTS for 186 units. Large incumbents have in-house teams, so moderate fit. 6. Real estate: embodied carbon, BRSR and green ratings. 7. Pharma: BRSR Core assurance and buyer Scope 3. 8. Power & renewables: Scope 2 and grid factors for everyone else. 9. Chemicals, FMCG, logistics, IT/ITeS: lower regulatory pain in this window. IT/ITeS leaders already publish mature reports, and no GLEC or ISO 14083 mention appeared in any of 700 BRSRs. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), 2026-10-01)

### emerging-themes (32 searches run)

- **[medium]** THEME RANKING (my judgement: timing x demand proxy x fit with ESG Astraa). 1) ESG data governance, assurance-readiness and human-approved AI: deadlines now (BRSR Core for the top 1,000 from FY2026-27; ISSA 5000 from 15 Dec 2026). 2) Anti-greenwashing and claim substantiation: EU EmpCo has applied since 27 Sep 2026; India CCPA guidelines from Oct 2024. 3) Internal carbon pricing derived from CBAM and CCTS: CBAM certificate sales start Feb 2027; the theme fits the CBAM/CCTS store best. 4) Renewable procurement and market-based Scope 2: GHG Protocol consultation results came out Jul 2026; the RCO trajectory runs to FY2029-30. 5) Physical risk at plant level (heat plus water): seasonal peak Mar-Jun. 6) Social data and just transition: Labour Codes since 21 Nov 2025; EU CSDDD applies from 2029. 7) Circularity and EPR: high compliance demand but a weaker platform fit. 8) AI's own footprint: niche. 9) Nature/TNFD: watch item; ISSB nature exposure draft targeted for CBD COP17 (Oct 2026), low uptake in India so far. Proxies: regulatory event dates below, BRSR mention prevalence, and counts of 2026 government releases. None of this is search volume. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), Synthesis as of 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** BRSR corpus proxy (my analysis of public BRSR text files; FY2023-24 n=779, FY2024-25 n=700; share of filings mentioning a term). BRSR Core: 19.1% to 30.6%. 'Reasonable assurance': 20.7% to 28.1%. AI ('artificial intelligence' or 'AI'): 15.1% to 20.7%. Greenwashing: 3 to 11 filings (0.4% to 1.6%). Internal carbon price: 2 to 5 filings (0.3% to 0.7%; FY24-25 filers are Apollo Tyres, GHCL, Godrej Properties, Harsha Engineers and Himadri Speciality Chemical). TNFD: 6 to 8 filings (1.1%; includes Adani Power, Hindustan Zinc, Vedanta, TVS Motor, Tata Consumer, Suzlon, Huhtamaki India). Physical climate risk: 5.3% to 7.0%. Heat stress or heatwave: 4.5% to 5.1%. Climate scenario analysis: 1.2% to 1.9%. Market-based Scope 2: 6 to 12 filings (1.7%). REC or I-REC: 2.1%. Open access: 5.1%. PPA or captive: 8.1% to 10.6%. CCTS: 2 to 7. Just transition: 1 to 4. Caveat: the corpus is not a complete census of the top 1,000. Terms that appear in the BRSR template itself (EPR, water stress, Scope 3, human-rights due diligence, green credits) inflate raw mention counts, so those were not read as disclosure rates. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2023-24 and FY2024-25 filings (corpus commit Feb 2026))
- **[low]** Answer-level heuristic on the same corpus (rough, directional only). Among filings where the Scope 3 leadership-indicator answer could be classified, those reporting a numeric Scope 3 figure rose from 52% (187/357) in FY23-24 to 61% (207/342) in FY24-25. The rest said not applicable, not monitored or not calculated. For facilities in water-stress areas, about half of classifiable FY24-25 filings gave data (185/368); the other half said not applicable or none. Many filings could not be parsed, so treat these as directional numbers to motivate a rigorous proprietary study, not as publishable statistics. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2023-24 and FY2024-25 filings)
- **[high]** SEBI circular of 28 Mar 2025 (SEBI/HO/CFD/CFD-PoD-1/P/CIR/2025/42) did four things. It let BRSR Core be checked by either 'assessment or assurance'. It made value-chain disclosure voluntary from FY2025-26, with its assessment or assurance from FY2026-27. It phased Core assurance by size. It added a voluntary green-credit leadership indicator from FY2024-25. Both circulars were later folded into SEBI's LODR master circular of 30 Jan 2026. The primary PDF is cited in the GitHub source as https://www.sebi.gov.in/sebi_data/attachdocs/mar-2025/1743159419610.pdf; it was not opened directly because of the egress block. ([source](https://github.com/hariom-s27/arpipe/blob/3b266c0aea193aa85912cc8545c30d1cd096a6d1/docs/phase3/PHASE3_CLAIMS_SCOPE_RESEARCH.md), Circular Mar 2025; master circular 30 Jan 2026)
- **[medium]** Master-circular consolidation of 30 Jan 2026 (covering circulars up to 30 Dec 2025, superseding the Nov 2024 version) is described in an explainer that also restates the assessment-or-assurance change and the value-chain deferral. ([source](https://github.com/syntropyearth/syntropyearth-site/blob/7e1406d1c441c06fd05ccd040d8a4641e7a1db00/thinking/brsr-core-assessment-or-assurance-sebi/index.html), 2026)
- **[medium]** ISSA 5000 (IAASB) applies to sustainability assurance engagements for periods beginning on or after 15 Dec 2026 and replaces ISAE 3000/3410 use for sustainability. It requires assurers to evaluate the reliability of information used as evidence and to understand internal controls, even in limited-assurance engagements. Both GitHub sources are secondary; iaasb.org was blocked. India's ICAI has its own SSAE 3000, which appears as the governing standard in BRSR assurance contexts. ([source](https://github.com/RebalanceImpact/RebalanceImpact.github.io/blob/05ca0a255f3f430a3470c01fe7a090180ede3c14/src/data/articleContent.js), 2026 article; standard effective Dec 2026)
- **[high]** The EY CSRD barometer (May 2025) independently confirms the ISSA 5000 effective date: reporting periods starting on or after 15 Dec 2026. ([source](https://github.com/youssefbenlallahom/EY_RAG/blob/cfc0d6e479e0e73f3607d2c49912b42c66b9aac3/output/ey-gl-ey-csrd-barometer-05-2025.md), May 2025)
- **[high]** EU anti-greenwashing status. Directive (EU) 2024/825 (EmpCo) had a transposition deadline of 27 Mar 2026 and member states must apply it from 27 Sep 2026. It bans generic environmental claims without recognised excellent performance, product-level neutrality claims based on offsets, self-made sustainability labels, and future-performance claims without detailed plans and independent verification. The Commission withdrew the separate Green Claims Directive proposal (COM(2023)166) in June 2025; the withdrawal intent was announced 20 Jun 2025. Footnoted primaries: eur-lex Directive 2024/825 and EESC 'Withdrawal of the Green Claims Directive' (Sep 2025). ([source](https://github.com/microsoft/cat-agent-skills/blob/54647c5d4cfa6908d17864cfa1c62196277ac6e5/submissions/global-greenwashing-claim-auditor/references/greenwashing-analysis-research.md), 2026 (EmpCo applies from 27 Sep 2026))
- **[high]** UK: under the DMCC Act 2024 the CMA has had direct consumer enforcement powers since April 2025, with penalties of up to 10% of worldwide turnover. In 2026 the CMA issued 'Making green claims: getting it right across the supply chain', which says manufacturers, brands, distributors and retailers can be responsible for claims they originate or repeat. This matters to Indian suppliers whose data feeds UK buyers' claims. ([source](https://github.com/microsoft/cat-agent-skills/blob/54647c5d4cfa6908d17864cfa1c62196277ac6e5/submissions/global-greenwashing-claim-auditor/references/greenwashing-analysis-research.md), Apr 2025 powers; 2026 guidance)
- **[medium]** India: the Central Consumer Protection Authority issued 'Guidelines for Prevention and Regulation of Greenwashing or Misleading Environmental Claims, 2024' dated 15 Oct 2024. This is evidenced by ASCI's advisories page as captured in a 25 Aug 2026 audit. The final text's detailed provisions were NOT re-verified here; consumeraffairs.nic.in and ascionline.in were blocked. ASCI's own green-claims guidelines could not be verified in this session. ([source](https://github.com/abhinandansethi/TMT-Tracker/blob/0dc2b77afa6400d1ffbaaf171fb0aefda7579b83/engine/audit/integrity_2026-08-25.json), Guidelines Oct 2024; captured Aug 2026)
- **[high]** SEBI anti-'purpose-washing' in capital markets. The ESG Debt Securities framework (circular of 5 Jun 2025) covers social, sustainability and sustainability-linked bonds and requires independent reviewers. On 27 Feb 2026 SEBI revised the reviewer/certifier norms for green debt securities to harmonise them with the other ESG debt types. ([source](https://github.com/rhnvrm/stock-market-circulars/blob/a84843c82f1d300d612af070a29436deebce0bbb/hugo-site/content/circulars/sebi/2026/sebi-2026-02-27-7f39962b1b6b1077-revised-norms-for-appointment-of-an-independent-third-party-reviewer-certifier-f.md), 27 Feb 2026)
- **[medium]** Under Omnibus I, the EU CSDDD now applies to companies with more than 5,000 employees and more than EUR 1.5bn turnover. Transposition is due by 26 Jul 2028 and compliance by 26 Jul 2029. The CSRD Omnibus (Directive (EU) 2026/470, OJ 26 Feb 2026, in force 18 Mar 2026) set CSRD thresholds at more than 1,000 employees and more than EUR 450m turnover. ([source](https://github.com/zoltan2/brussels-governance-monitor/blob/611ed93f9318eca5c8a68a33c33364cca704bc75/content/dossiers/rse.en.mdx), 2026)
- **[medium]** CSRD Omnibus publication details (Directive (EU) 2026/470, 26 Feb 2026; revised ESRS due about Sep 2026) come from a May 2026 fact-currency sweep. That sweep cites Gibson Dunn and Covington; their pages were not opened. The same sweep says SBTi Corporate Net-Zero Standard V2 was not yet final as of May 2026 and becomes mandatory for new targets from 1 Jan 2028. It also gives a Q1 2026 CBAM certificate price of EUR 75.36. ([source](https://github.com/endoergic/the_carbon_book/blob/9ed43618e67e5293f9753fa7129c544fae47cadb/00_FACT_CURRENCY_SOURCES_2026-05.md), May 2026)
- **[medium]** Jul 2026 EU and standard-setter updates, read from an IAS Plus feed snapshot. The Commission adopted revised ESRSs and a voluntary standard for undertakings protected by the 'value chain cap' (delegated regulation of 3 Jul 2026); the revised ESRSs take effect 1 Jan 2027. EFRAG published an exposure draft on ESRS for certain non-EU undertakings (Art 40a) on 23 Jul 2026. GHG Protocol and ISO will merge their corporate standards into one harmonised standard and published Scope 2 consultation results plus Actions and Market Instruments (AMI) feedback (primary: ghgprotocol.org/blog/ghg-protocol-announces-key-standard-development-updates). ([source](https://github.com/pauliusgin/curated-news/blob/20cf26e81bfd2b6e0b80ab3b03d8ab42b839e14f/testdata/feeds/ias-plus.json), Jul 2026)
- **[medium]** GHG Protocol Scope 2 revision. Public consultation ran Oct 2025 to Jan 2026 and proposed hourly matching and deliverability for market-based accounting; the final standard is expected around 2027. The GitHub source cites ghgprotocol.org/blog/upcoming-scope-2-public-consultation-hourly-matching-and-deliverability. ([source](https://github.com/JoshuaElliott11/hourly-matching-studio/blob/234060e6fc967e4a229b46bdba01a9912944e504/README.md), 2025-2026)
- **[high]** India renewable consumption obligation: the Ministry of Power has notified the RPO-then-RCO trajectory up to 2029-30. It applies to all designated consumers under the Energy Conservation Act 2001, carries penalties for non-compliance, and includes a decentralised-RE quantum (PIB, 16 Mar 2026). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2240737&reg=48&lang=1), 16 Mar 2026 (PIB text read via GitHub mirror))
- **[medium]** RCO targets rise from 29.91% (FY2024-25) to 43.33% (FY2029-30) under the revised MoP framework (notified about 27 Sep 2025). CERC issued Virtual PPA guidelines on 24 Dec 2025 that let designated consumers meet RCO through VPPAs and RECs. Source is a secondary policy tracker citing beeindia.gov.in; the primary was not opened. ([source](https://github.com/ceekay-munshot/solardash/blob/7d12c537f9a34c63c9993aa4b1cd15a0e44e03cd/js/real-data-policy.js), Sep-Dec 2025)
- **[high]** Under CCTS, obligated entities must disclose Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions (Ministry of Textiles Lok Sabha reply, 10 Mar 2026). The same reply cites the Eco-Mark Scheme 2024, which covers textiles, and a pilot on Product Environmental Footprint for SMEs. ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2237705&reg=48&lang=1), 10 Mar 2026 (PIB text via GitHub mirror))
- **[high]** India's NDC for 2031-35 was approved by Cabinet on 25 Mar 2026: a 47% cut in emissions intensity of GDP by 2035 from 2005, 60% non-fossil installed power capacity by 2035, and a 3.5-4.0 GtCO2e forest and tree carbon sink. Non-fossil capacity was already 52.57% in Feb 2026. The NDC also has an expanded adaptation section. ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2245213&reg=48&lang=1), 25 Mar 2026)
- **[high]** Heat as a 2026 workplace issue. The Ministry of Labour & Employment issued a nationwide heatwave advisory around 28 Apr 2026 asking States/UTs to direct employers to reschedule hours, provide water, rest and cooling, and have factory and mine managements slow work pace; fortnightly status reports were requested. The NHRC wrote to 21 states and Delhi citing NCRB data of 3,712 heat or sunstroke deaths in 2019-23 (PIB 2256123). IMD issued heatwave guidance on 25 Apr 2026 (PIB 2255487). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2256186&reg=48&lang=1), Apr 2026)
- **[high]** Water. At the World Water Day Conclave on 23 Mar 2026 (theme 'Industry for Water') the government released the National Water Data Policy 2026 and a Compendium of Good Practices on Industrial Water Use Efficiency. A 6 Aug 2026 Rajya Sabha reply on AI data centres said water availability in water-stressed regions is weighed in Environmental Clearance appraisal. It also said groundwater extraction is regulated under MoJS notification S.O. 3289(E) of 24 Sep 2020, as amended 29 Mar 2023 (PIB 2295675). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2244153&reg=48&lang=1), Mar and Aug 2026)
- **[high]** India's EPR regimes as listed in a 24 Mar 2026 Lok Sabha reply: waste tyres (2022); Battery Waste Management Rules 2022 (amended 2023 and 2024); E-Waste Rules 2022 (amended 2023); Plastic Waste Management Rules (amended Apr 2023); used oil (Sep 2023); End-of-Life Vehicles (6 Jan 2025); Construction and Demolition Waste (2 Apr 2025); non-ferrous metal scrap (1 Jul 2025). The rules impose annual recycling and recycled-content targets. NITI Aayog published three circularity reports in Jan 2026, and a Rs 1,500 crore critical-mineral recycling scheme exists. Solid Waste Management Rules 2026 are being rolled out (IEC campaign, PIB 2267715, 1 Jun 2026). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2244104&reg=48&lang=1), 24 Mar 2026)
- **[high]** Trade link. On 31 Jul 2026 the Department of Commerce held a Trade and Sustainable Development workshop that tied CCTS, the Indian Carbon Market and the EPR framework to preparing for 'sustainability-related trade disciplines' (carbon border measures, standards) in FTAs. This makes circularity and carbon data an export-competitiveness topic. ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2292786&reg=48&lang=1), 31 Jul 2026)
- **[high]** Social dimension of ESG. India's four Labour Codes took effect on 21 Nov 2025. At an IICA/ILO inter-ministerial consultation in Mar 2026, MoLE flagged changes on minimum wages, appointment letters and social-security coverage, including ESIC applying pan-India and to hazardous industries regardless of size. Participants proposed integrating labour data from PLFS, ESIC and EPFO with SEBI's BRSR framework (PIB 2240319). At NCRBC in Jul 2026 a SEBI CGM said BRSR must evolve as sector-specific materiality and data gaps emerge (PIB 2286148). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2240319&reg=48&lang=1), Mar and Jul 2026)
- **[high]** Labour Codes effective date of 21 Nov 2025 is corroborated by an HR compliance source that cites PIB and Ministry of Labour releases. ([source](https://github.com/Trust-Code-System/Atlas-HR/blob/427b4dce602237cb5ee834878f79b0fd12a5e9ee/src/content/countries/india.mdx), 2025-2026)
- **[high]** AI governance in India. MeitY constituted the AI Governance and Economic Group (AIGEG) on 17 Apr 2026 to give effect to India's AI Governance Guidelines, supported by a Technology and Policy Expert Committee (PIB 2253322). An India AI Impact Summit working group on 'Resilience, Innovation & Efficiency' is examining energy, water and environmental resilience of AI infrastructure (PIB 2295675). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2252739&reg=48&lang=1), Apr-Aug 2026)
- **[high]** AI footprint baseline: the IEA 'Energy and AI' report (Apr 2025) puts data-centre electricity at about 415 TWh in 2024 (about 1.5% of global demand), projected to about 945 TWh by 2030 in the Base Case. The GitHub source quotes iea.org/reports/energy-and-ai, which was blocked here. ([source](https://github.com/baobab-tech/research-ai-energy/blob/1436b8c8ea0ac211ae7cb33b4a23811e2de2fb9f/research/ghg/023-iea-2025-energy-and-ai-datacentre-electricity.md), Apr 2025)
- **[medium]** Global competitors are turning AI governance into a trust signal. Persefoni announced ISO/IEC 42001 (AI management system) certification on 24 Aug 2026 and published 'AI in Carbon Accounting [+ What to Ask Your Vendor]' (Nov 2025). Watershed launched AI features on 24 Jun 2026. No India-specific equivalent from Indian ESG vendors was checked or found in this session. ([source](https://www.persefoni.com/blog/iso-iec-42001-certification-ai-management), Aug 2026 (read via a sibling agent's saved blog crawl))
- **[medium]** Nature. The ISSB plans a nature-related disclosures exposure draft by about Oct 2026, ahead of CBD COP17 (Yerevan, 19-30 Oct 2026). TNFD signed an MoU with the IFRS Foundation in Nov 2025 and is completing its technical work by Q3 2026. Whether the exposure draft has been published as of 1 Oct 2026 could NOT be verified (ifrs.org was blocked). ([source](https://github.com/ferryhe/climate_monitor_wiki/blob/125ec13c7cfb63f0b6b4a3c7a1463a5886ebd1aa/sources/climate-monitor-2026-04-01.md), Apr 2026 source)
- **[high]** CBD COP17 dates confirmed as 19-30 Oct 2026 in Yerevan, Armenia, from GBIF's Data to Action Pavilion page. ([source](https://github.com/gbif/hp-cbd-cop17/blob/c8a3976b34d2078b960fbdad2579ea415a2cd1de/en/home.md), 2026)
- **[medium]** Nature in India in practice. Tata Steel keeps a dedicated TNFD reports page that was updated in Jun 2026 (document-monitor diff). The GHG Protocol Land Sector and Removals Standard was finalised with a 1 Jan 2027 effective date (Watershed blog, 2 Jun 2026), which affects agri-linked and land-intensive supply chains. ([source](https://www.tatasteel.com/sustainability/governance/taskforce-on-nature-related-financial-disclosures-tnfd-reports/), Jun 2026)
- **[low]** Physical-risk regulation for lenders: the RBI's 'Draft Disclosure Framework on Climate-related Financial Risks' (Feb 2024) covers governance, strategy, risk management and metrics, and the RBI launched RB-CRIS, a climate risk data portal. Final issuance status as of Oct 2026 could NOT be verified; rbi.org.in was blocked and no GitHub source showed a final circular. ([source](https://github.com/motazsaad/ESG-RAG-benchmark-ragas/blob/66d9587d50794e7a0fa36387c9a17750fbc1f89a/data/raw/50872_7142_2024.md), 2024-2025 source; status unverified)
- **[medium]** India's Department of Economic Affairs invited comments on a Draft Framework of 'India's Climate Finance Taxonomy' by 25 Jun 2025 (PIB 2127562, 7 May 2025). Whether a final taxonomy was issued was not verified. A taxonomy matters for green-claim credibility in lending and bonds.
