([source](https://www.pib.gov.in//PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2127562&reg=3&lang=1), May 2025; final status unverified)
- **[medium]** Proxy for government attention: counts of 2026 releases mentioning each theme, from a UPSC-filtered PIB subset (7,483 releases, 25 Feb to 1 Oct 2026). Water conservation/groundwater 219; biodiversity 198; AI with energy, water or data centres 129; EPR/circular economy 127; heat/heatwave 40; Labour Codes 35; BRSR, responsible business or ESG 26; open access/RCO/REC 17; carbon market/CCTS 11; CBAM 8; greenwashing 0. This measures how much the government communicates on each theme, not search demand. It suggests water, circularity and heat are policy-salient while greenwashing is driven by regulation rather than news in India. ([source](https://github.com/Ankitbuilder/upsc-current-affairs-pipeline), Feb-Oct 2026)
- **[high]** Not verified because of access limits: whether trading of Carbon Credit Certificates has started on power exchanges; final RBI climate disclosure directions; ASCI green-claims guideline text; the CCPA guideline provisions in detail; India's final climate finance taxonomy; publication of the ISSB nature exposure draft; EU EmpCo transposition status by member state; plastic EPR target percentages for FY2025-26 and later (a secondary source cites 60% for 2024-25 and 80% for 2026-27 recycling; not used). ([source](https://github.com/Ankitbuilder/upsc-current-affairs-pipeline), As of 2026-10-01)

### whitepaper-playbook (21 searches run)

- **[high]** ACCESS LIMITS (read first). WebSearch had already used its 200-call session budget when this researcher started; all 3 WebSearch calls returned 'budget exhausted'. Firecrawl returned 402 (low credits). The egress proxy blocked WebFetch and curl to nseindia.com, bseindia.com, sebi.gov.in, mca.gov.in, pib.gov.in, developers.google.com, arxiv.org, contentmarketinginstitute.com, ahrefs.com, edelman.com, schema.org and more; only GitHub and package registries were reachable. Evidence therefore comes from (a) GitHub code search (21 queries) and (b) public GitHub repos that hold primary data (BRSR XBRL manifests, the SEBI taxonomy, Gazette PDFs, EU CBAM default values) or verbatim captures of primary pages. Where only a secondary capture was available, the finding says so and its confidence is lowered. No search volumes or backlink counts were measured. ([source](https://github.com/ai-robots-txt/ai.robots.txt), 2026-10-01 session)
- **[high]** BRSR filings ARE machine-readable XBRL on NSE and BSE. NSE's BRSR page is a React app backed by the JSON endpoint /api/corporate-bussiness-sustainabilitiy (NSE's own spelling). Each row gives company, symbol, financial-year range, submission and revision dates, a PDF URL (attachmentFile) and an XBRL URL (xbrlFile). Files are hosted at nsearchives.nseindia.com/corporate/xbrl/. BSE serves the same filings at bseindia.com/XBRLFILES/BRSRUploadDocument/BRSR_<scrip>_<timestamp>.xml, plus a PDF. A public manifest dated 2 Jul 2026 shows FY2025-26 BRSR XBRL already filed: TCS on BSE 15 May 2026, Reliance on BSE 28 May 2026 (NSE copy 6 Jun 2026). The legal basis cited in that code is LODR Reg 34(2)(f). MCA: no evidence found that BRSR is filed in XBRL on MCA21, and mca.gov.in was blocked, so treat MCA as NOT verified. Practical note: NSE returns 403 without session cookies and a Referer header (no login needed). One open-source team keeps automated NSE access behind a 'fail-closed' legal approval gate at 0.5 requests/second, so review exchange terms of use before bulk collection. ([source](https://github.com/Jayadityas/Financial-results-extraction-using-NSE-BSE-APIs/blob/80a93617604a043936c03d1fca88aef558fdacca/data/metadata/brsr_manifest.jsonl), 2026-07 (manifest fetched 2026-07-02); corroborated by https://github.com/shruti-kamma/Blackbox/blob/b8767644d54900dce054b35dd2cd55e94c2808cc/docs/plans/brsr-accessibility-pipeline.md and https://github.com/singr7/brsr-analytics/blob/8333f63035850c78d4e915fb6637442a615e9c94/docs/operations/NSE_BRSR_INGESTION.md)
- **[high]** The SEBI BRSR XBRL taxonomy can support quantitative benchmarks. The core schema (in-capmkt.xsd) has about 2,600 named element definitions, including: - TotalScope1Emissions, TotalScope2Emissions, TotalScope3Emissions - TotalScope1AndScope2EmissionsIntensityPerRupeeOfTurnoverAdjustedForPurchasingPowerParity (the BRSR Core metric) - the name of the external assurance or assessment agency for GHG and for Scope 3 - per-disclosure 'AssuranceSubTypeFor…' elements and an AssessmentOrAssuranceProviderAxis - value-chain elements (e.g. % of value-chain partners assessed; green credits of the top-ten value-chain partners) Taxonomy versions seen: 2021-09-30, 2024-04-30 and 2025-05-31. A parser test fixture for an FY2025-26 filing (1 Apr 2025 to 31 Mar 2026) uses a namespace dated 2026-02-28. A filing-level assurance tracker, a GHG-intensity benchmark and a Scope 3 disclosure study can therefore be built without PDF scraping. ([source](https://github.com/sriiiyanshu/XML-XBRL-Generator/blob/fd288631c344ddf337c5ae4a9ca1619551a7839f/Taxonomy_BUSINESS_RESPONSIBILITY_SUSTAINABILITY_REPORTING/core/in-capmkt.xsd), taxonomy 2025-05-31; FY2025-26 namespace 2026-02-28 per https://github.com/lariv-in/resummarized/blob/9922dad3d62baf914a571a82d15fb3cc8922c84b/src/nse/brsr.rs)
- **[high]** Proof that a top-1000 BRSR dataset is feasible, and that a data-quality layer is essential. One open project parsed FY2024-25 BRSR XBRL for the NIFTY 100 (state as of 16 Aug 2026): - 93 filings parsed and 204,880 raw XBRL facts - 7 companies with no FY25 BRSR under their NSE symbol (e.g. ABB, Siemens; calendar-year filers or demerged entities) - 16 values withheld: 7 ambiguous turnover scales, and 9 energy facts filed under a bare 'J' unit at inconsistent magnitudes (TVS Motor 676,660 vs Jindal Steel 239.51) A second project (as of 27 Jul 2026) found 413 companies with BRSRs for all of FY2023-24, FY2024-25 and FY2025-26 on NSE, and 870 companies with partial history. Implication: any ESG Astraa benchmark must publish its cohort rules, unit normalisation and withheld-value counts. Those method notes are themselves citable content. ([source](https://github.com/singr7/brsr-analytics/blob/8333f63035850c78d4e915fb6637442a615e9c94/docs/operations/NSE_BRSR_INGESTION.md), 2026-08-16 and 2026-07-27)
- **[medium]** Illustrative pilot on public BRSR text extracts. A public GitHub corpus holds 821 FY2023-24 and 729 FY2024-25 BRSR PDF-to-text files; its provenance is undocumented, it is not the full top-1000, and the two years are not a matched panel. My own keyword scan found: - 'CBAM' in 6 of 821 filings (0.7%) and 5 of 729 (0.7%) - CCTS or Indian Carbon Market in 2 (0.2%) and 7 (1.0%) - 'BRSR Core' in 149 (18.1%) and 214 (29.4%) - 'reasonable assurance' in 161 (19.6%) and 198 (27.2%) - Scope 3 in 68.1% and 71.6% (inflated, because the BRSR format itself asks about Scope 3) These are directional only. They suggest clear hooks: a CBAM and CCTS disclosure gap among listed companies, and fast growth in BRSR Core assurance language. Re-run on exchange XBRL before publishing any figure. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2023-24 and FY2024-25 filings; scan run 2026-10-01)
- **[high]** Formats that work for AI citation: Google says no special AEO markup is needed, and original data is what gets cited. Google's 'AI features and your website' page (read via a verbatim mirror) says: - 'There are no additional requirements to appear in AI Overviews or AI Mode' - 'You don't need to create new machine readable files, AI text files, or markup… no special schema.org structured data' Best practices it lists: crawlable pages, internal links, important content 'in textual form', and structured data that 'matches the visible text'. AI Mode uses 'query fan-out' across subtopics. Separately, the GEO paper (Aggarwal et al., arXiv 2311.09735) reports gains of 'up to 40%' in generative-engine visibility. The methods it tested include adding statistics, adding quotations and citing credible sources; per-method results could not be re-verified because arXiv was blocked. Playbook: publish every white paper as a full HTML report. Use one finding per H2/H3, a one-sentence answer-first summary, the exact statistic with its date and denominator, and tables in HTML rather than images. Treat the PDF as a companion file, not the primary artefact. ([source](https://github.com/lesishu/seo-guide-skill/blob/ab8af275605f249b8a3eb620ba0cd1b74eb01436/references-google/ai-features.md), Google doc mirror read 2026-10-01; GEO repo https://github.com/GEO-optim/GEO)
- **[medium]** Recent AI-citation shifts favour primary-source research over listicles. Peec AI data, shared via Lily Ray in August 2026 (secondary; vendor data), says that after the ChatGPT 5.6 release, listicles fell from 15.77% to 7.80% of ChatGPT citations (-50.5%) and comparison/'vs' pages from 9.08% to 6.17% (-32.1%). Over the same period, 'site:' and 'official' searches rose. The source rates 'original research + data' as strong on ChatGPT, Google AIO, Gemini and Perplexity. Implication for ESG Astraa: competitors currently win Indian category SERPs with 'best BRSR/CBAM software 2026' listicles (per the brand research). An original-data white paper is the more durable AEO play than copying those listicles. ([source](https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills/blob/HEAD/skills/ai-seo/references/format-volatility.md), 2026-08)
- **[medium]** Gating: use a hybrid model. Put full findings in ungated HTML and gate only the data pack. Evidence: - 6sense 2025 Buyer Experience Report (about 4,000 respondents, published 12 Nov 2025): 94% of buyers ranked their shortlist before engaging sellers; the preferred pre-contact vendor won 77% of the time; first seller contact came 61% of the way through the journey. - G2 (company-stated, 8 Jan 2026), citing an August 2025 survey: 87% of B2B software buyers say AI chatbots have changed how they research, and half now start with an AI chatbot rather than Google. - Gated pages and PDF-only content are not visible as crawlable text (Google guidance above). Recommendation: - Ungated: the HTML report, all headline statistics, charts and methodology. - Gated (email): the company-level CSV/XLSX extract, sector cuts, and a 'benchmark your company' self-assessment. These produce identified leads without hiding the citable claims. Both buyer surveys were read via GitHub-preserved captures; the primary URLs are 6sense.com/science-of-b2b/buyer-experience-report-2025/ and learn.g2.com/g2-traffic-momentum. ([source](https://github.com/spdaly/brain/blob/c5898e726c4c025076bb535dd85b82af078d429d/wiki/sources/SRC-2026-09-23-025.md), 2025-11 (6sense); 2026-01 (G2, via https://github.com/VAD37/market-research-2026-09-22/blob/c7eb06306b407dbad83e0d80251657d3de6ee006/docs/raw/e-case-c9-g2-traffic-momentum-2026-09-22.md))
- **[medium]** Who reads thought leadership matches ESG Astraa's buyers. Figures from the Edelman-LinkedIn 2025 B2B Thought Leadership Impact Report (secondary summary; the primary edelman.com page could not be opened): - 71% of 'hidden decision-makers' (finance, legal, compliance, operations) say thought leadership shows vendor capability better than conventional marketing - 64% trust it more than marketing materials - 79% are more likely to support proposals from vendors who publish strong thought leadership These hidden roles map directly to ESG Astraa's CFO and Company Secretary buyers, who own BRSR sign-off. White papers should therefore be written for finance and compliance readers: risk, cost, deadlines and audit evidence, not sustainability vision. ([source](https://github.com/sociilabs/claude-content-writer/blob/53e42c979797fc28c5d2ff09dbafc63227e12cbe/references/content-frameworks.md), 2025 report; summary updated 2026-05)
- **[medium]** Making white papers rank needs a hub-and-spoke cluster plus technical hygiene: - One canonical pillar page per report at a keyword slug (e.g. /research/brsr-core-assurance-tracker-2026), not the current numeric /insights/blogs/2 pattern. - 6-10 spoke posts, one per finding or AEO question, each linking back to the pillar with descriptive anchors, and the pillar linking out to them. - An FAQ block that answers the buyer questions verbatim. - Article or Report JSON-LD (schema.org 'Report' is a subtype of Article, 'a Report generated by governmental or non-governmental organization') and Organization/author markup. Markup must match visible text and does not by itself win AI placement. - A Dataset description for the downloadable data. - The PDF with proper document title metadata and a plain filename (the current H&M PDF has no title metadata and an '&' in its name). - Desk-research case studies labelled as such, to avoid AI misattribution. ([source](https://github.com/DeploySolutions/deploy_pocos_schema_org/blob/1fee11bbc6d0f23b5d4c7fd6cebab00903668a8f/src/Deploy.Schema.Org/Types/Report.cs), 2026; brand hygiene issues from https://esgastraa.com/case-studies/H&M.pdf (brand-context research))
- **[high]** Crawler access for AI citation. Allow the retrieval and search bots in robots.txt and CDN rules: OAI-SearchBot ('surface as results in SearchGPT'), ChatGPT-User, Claude-SearchBot, PerplexityBot, and Googlebot. Training bots (GPTBot, ClaudeBot) and Google-Extended can be decided separately; Google-Extended 'does not impact a site's inclusion or ranking in Google Search'. llms.txt v2 (Jeremy Howard, modified 10 Aug 2026) is widely adopted and recommends .md copies of key pages, but Google states no AI text files are needed. Treat llms.txt as a cheap optional extra listing the research hub, not a ranking lever. ([source](https://github.com/ai-robots-txt/ai.robots.txt), 2026; llms.txt spec https://github.com/AnswerDotAI/llms-txt)
- **[high]** Measurement: track AI visibility and impressions, not only clicks. Pew Research (July 2025; 68,879 Google searches by about 900 US adults in March 2025) found: - users clicked a traditional result on 8% of visits when an AI summary appeared, vs 15% without - links inside the summary got about 1% of clicks First-party tools now exist: - Google added generative AI performance reports to Search Console on 3 June 2026: impressions in AI Overviews, AI Mode and Discover; no clicks yet; UK rolled out first, so Indian properties may not see data yet. - GA4's default channel group has an 'AI Assistant' channel (medium 'ai-assistant'; ChatGPT, Gemini, DeepSeek, Copilot, Grok). AI Overview and AI Mode clicks stay in Organic Search, and many assistant clicks arrive as Direct. Recommended KPI stack per white paper: 1. AI share of voice: a fixed panel of about 50 buyer prompts across ChatGPT, Perplexity, Gemini and Google AI Mode, run monthly, logging mention and citation of esgastraa.com vs competitors. 2. Search Console AI and organic impressions for pillar and spokes. 3. GA4 AI-assistant and organic sessions. 4. Data-pack downloads leading to MQLs and SQLs. 5. Assisted pipeline: opportunities whose contacts viewed report pages before first meeting. 6. A self-reported 'How did you hear about us?' field with an AI-assistant option. 7. Referring domains and press citations. 8. Branded search growth for 'ESG Astraa', given the Astra ESG name collision. ([source](https://www.pewresearch.org/short-reads/2025/07/22/google-users-are-less-likely-to-click-on-links-when-an-ai-summary-appears-in-the-results/), 2025-07 (Pew, read via verbatim mirror); 2026-06 (GSC: https://github.com/We-Amp/pagespeed-optimizer/blob/8aa9cccc2cc55704de5698c06953a2fc30589600/website/src/content/blog/search-console-ai-performance-report-vs-page-speed.md); 2026-09 (GA4: https://github.com/rybbit-io/rybbit/blob/fcbaf461a63f3267f98ea5517f7af7346a3fa88b/docs/content/blog/track-chatgpt-traffic.mdx))
- **[medium]** Distribution: LinkedIn is the main channel and also an AI-citation surface. - Semrush tracked the top 25 most-cited domains across ChatGPT Search, Google AI Mode and Perplexity for 13 weeks (230,000+ prompts, 100M+ citations). Reddit, Wikipedia, LinkedIn, YouTube and Google properties led, and no brand product sites made the list. - LinkedIn's own AEO guidance, reported September 2026 (platform-reported), says LinkedIn is the most-cited outlet for professional topics, Articles produce about 60% of its citations vs about 40% for Posts, and a post's opening words become its URL slug. - CMI 2024 (via Ahrefs' compilation): 85% of B2B marketers rate LinkedIn the best-performing social platform. Playbook: publish each report's findings as founder-bylined LinkedIn Articles (not just posts) that link to the pillar; run a LinkedIn newsletter timed to filing and regulatory dates; seed one data chart per week. Not verified online this session (sites blocked): Indian industry-body routes. Candidates are ICSI (company secretaries own BRSR), the ICAI Sustainability Reporting Standards Board, CII/FICCI sustainability councils, and EEPC India/FIEO for CBAM exporters. Also list on OneStopESG and Indian business-press data desks for earned citations. ([source](https://github.com/elmohq/elmo/blob/ff99cd2e6f34f2c5099e54a1dd95301be8ac6cd1/packages/docs/content/blog/where-ai-citations-come-from.mdx), 2026 (Semrush primary: https://www.semrush.com/blog/most-cited-domains-ai/; LinkedIn guide via https://github.com/coreyhaines31/marketingskills/blob/HEAD/skills/ai-seo/references/format-volatility.md))
- **[low]** Original research is under-used but tends to pay off (vendor surveys via Ahrefs' 105-statistics compilation, updated 22 Dec 2025): - Orbit Media 2025: almost half of content programs now publish original research, and 25% of those report strong results; original research is among the most effective and least used tactics - Datalily 2025: 86% of marketers plan to raise proprietary-research budgets in 2026; 64% report higher conversion from content-sourced leads since adopting proprietary research - LinkedIn/Ipsos 2025 B2B Marketing Benchmark (1,500 senior B2B marketers): 94% say trust is important to B2B success Treat all of these as vendor-sourced directional evidence, not causal proof. ([source](https://github.com/0xenzyme/awesome-seo-articles/blob/HEAD/ahrefs-blog/content-marketing/content-marketing-statistics.md), 2025-12 (primary https://ahrefs.com/blog/content-marketing-statistics/ not reachable directly))
- **[medium]** Cadence anchored to the Indian compliance calendar: - Annual flagship BRSR data report, published Sept-Oct after the May-August filing season. In the manifest above, FY2025-26 BRSRs landed 15 May to 6 Jun 2026, and Reliance's FY2024-25 BRSR on NSE on 7 Aug 2025. - Annual CBAM report before the 1 Feb 2027 start of certificate sales and refreshed before the 30 Sep 2027 first declaration. - CCTS update after each GEI-target Gazette notification. - Quarterly 'regulatory pulse' refresh of each pillar with a visible 'last updated' date. - 2-4 spoke posts a month. One flagship plus three or four derivative white papers a year is realistic for a small team. The XBRL pipeline makes yearly refreshes cheap, and comparing years compounds the citation value. ([source](https://github.com/Jayadityas/Financial-results-extraction-using-NSE-BSE-APIs/blob/80a93617604a043936c03d1fca88aef558fdacca/data/metadata/brsr_manifest.jsonl), 2026)
- **[medium]** Regulatory why-now (BRSR). SEBI circular CIR/2025/42 of 28 Mar 2025 phases BRSR Core 'assurance or assessment': - top 150 from FY2023-24 - top 250 from FY2024-25 - top 500 from FY2025-26 - top 1,000 from FY2026-27 Value-chain disclosures are voluntary for the top 250 from FY2025-26, and their assurance is voluntary from FY2026-27. Only value-chain partners at 2% or more of purchases or sales count, with coverage capped at those making up 75% cumulatively. The circular also introduced voluntary green-credit disclosure. sebi.gov.in was blocked, so this was read via a May 2026 research note that cites the SEBI circular URLs (SEBI CIR/2025/42: https://www.sebi.gov.in/legal/circulars/mar-2025/measures-to-facilitate-ease-of-doing-business-with-respect-to-framework-for-assurance-or-assessment-esg-disclosures-for-value-chain-and-introduction-of-voluntary-disclosure-on-green-credits_93102.html). It matches the phase-in in the brand research. ([source](https://github.com/kenithphilip/Anvil/blob/1f45d83f51a0ff246dd74bf04b33a40d44edd558/docs/STRATEGIC_BET_07_brsr_value_chain.md), 2026-05-10 synthesis of 2023 and 2025 circulars)
- **[high]** Regulatory why-now (CBAM). The CBAM default values are in Implementing Regulation (EU) 2025/2621, Annex I, as replaced by Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1740 (OJ L, 31 Jul 2026; adopted 20 Jul 2026; some aluminium values corrected retroactively to 1 Jan 2026). When defaults are used to count certificates, total-emission defaults are marked up as follows: - cement, iron and steel, aluminium, hydrogen: 10% for 2026, 20% for 2027, 30% for 2028 onward - fertilisers: 1% Example, unwrought aluminium (CN 7601 10 00), before mark-up: India 1.870 tCO2e/t vs 'other countries' 2.203 vs the Annex IV 'origin unknown' 3.198. This was read from a tool's dated quotation of the consolidated EUR-Lex text and the Commission's default-value Excel (v2, 6 Aug 2026), retrieved 24 Sep 2026; EUR-Lex itself was blocked. Combined with the definitive period from 1 Jan 2026, certificate sales from 1 Feb 2027 and the first declaration by 30 Sep 2027 (EC sources in the brand research), this gives a precise 'cost of not measuring' storyline. ([source](https://github.com/Keremozdemirra/cbam-mcp), 2026-07-31 regulation; data retrieved 2026-09-24)
- **[high]** Regulatory why-now (CCTS), newer than the brand brief. On 26 June 2026, MoEFCC published DRAFT notification G.S.R. 517(E) (Gazette CG-DL-E-02072026-274012) proposing GHG emission intensity targets for the Iron & Steel sector. It covers 255 unique obligated-entity IDs (INSOE…), including SAIL plants, Tata Steel, JSW Steel, AM/NS and JSPL, with a 60-day objection window. Earlier notifications: G.S.R. 739(E) of 8 Oct 2025 (aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali, pulp & paper) and the 13 Jan 2026 notification (secondary aluminium, refineries, petrochemicals, textiles). A CC BY 4.0 open dataset, 'CCTS-745' (Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.21671592), standardises all 745 facilities across Phases I-III. Steel is both a CBAM good and now proposed for CCTS. Whether the steel draft has been finalised could NOT be verified. ([source](https://github.com/prakulhiremath/ccts-745), 2026-06-26 draft; final status unverified)

### international-markets (37 searches run)

- **[high]** METHOD AND ACCESS LIMITS (read first). The shared WebSearch budget was used up (200/200) before this researcher started, so all 4 WebSearch calls failed. Firecrawl returned HTTP 402 (no credits). The egress proxy blocked WebFetch to every regulator and news domain I tried: acra.gov.sg, sgx.com, sc.com.my, ifrs.org, moccae.gov.ae, cma.org.sa, ojk.go.id, gov.uk, eur-lex.europa.eu, pib.gov.in, wikipedia, pwc.com and lexology. Only GitHub could be reached. I therefore ran 37 GitHub code and repository searches, read public GitHub-hosted corpora that quote primary texts, and read a scraped PIB press-release dataset that another researcher had stored in the session scratchpad. No regulator page was opened directly; each finding below names the corpus it came from. Confidence is capped at medium where only a secondary source exists. No search volumes were measured. ([source](https://github.com/saimali7/ZeroCarbon.gov), 2026-10-01)
- **[medium]** UAE Federal Decree-Law No. 11 of 2024 on the Reduction of Climate Change Effects. It was issued 28 Aug 2024, published 30 Aug 2024, and entered into force 30 May 2025. Sources had one year to bring their status into line, so the default adjustment window closed on 30 May 2026. The Cabinet may extend it; law firms reported an extension was expected, but the corpus found no extension resolution. Article 6(1) requires designated sources to measure GHG emissions, keep an emissions inventory, submit periodic reports and reduction measures on Ministry forms, and keep records for 5 years. Article 15 sets fines of AED 50,000 to 2,000,000, and Article 16 doubles them for a repeat offence within 2 years. Article 17 administrative penalties await a Cabinet resolution. Source: a curated corpus quoting the official portal (https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/2558, not opened directly). ([source](https://github.com/saimali7/ZeroCarbon.gov/blob/HEAD/apps/api/data/regulations.json), 2026)
- **[medium]** UAE Cabinet Resolution No. 67 of 2024 (National Register for Carbon Credits) has been in force since 28 Dec 2024. It is mandatory for entities emitting 0.5 MtCO2e or more per year across Scope 1+2. These entities must run IPCC-based MRV, file an annual GHG report, and have emission-reduction reports verified to ISO 14065. Annex 2 fines run from AED 500,000 to 2,000,000. Primary source cited by the corpus: https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/2521 (not opened). ([source](https://github.com/saimali7/ZeroCarbon.gov/blob/HEAD/apps/api/data/regulations.json), 2026)
- **[medium]** Abu Dhabi facility-level MRV (EAD Technical Guidance v8, 27 Feb 2026) implements Article 6 of the Decree-Law. Scope: facilities in power and water, oil and gas, industry and transport that emit 25,000 tCO2e or more of Scope 1 a year. Only CO2 and CH4 count, the test is per facility, and the boundary is operational control; free zones are included. The annual report is due 31 March, with a grace period to 14 April per EAD's March 2026 workshop. Third-party verification (ISO 14065/17029) is not obligatory until 2027. Methane uses AR5 GWP of 28. Activity-data tiers mirror the EU ETS (±7.5%, ±5%, ±2.5%, ±1.5%), and the guidance sets de minimis and minor source-stream rules and a mandatory data-gap table. Primary PDFs cited by the corpus: https://facilitymrv.ead.ae/appDocuments/20260227%20-%20Technical%20Guidance%20v8.pdf and https://facilitymrv.ead.ae/appDocuments/20260312_EAD%20MRV_%20Workshop%20Presentation.pdf (not opened). ([source](https://github.com/saimali7/ZeroCarbon.gov/blob/HEAD/apps/api/data/regulations.json), 2026-02 to 2026-03)
- **[low]** Unverified: a claim that the UAE SCA has mandated IFRS/ISSB sustainability standards for listed companies from 2025 appears only on a low-quality consultant blog. No regulator or law-firm source confirmed it. Treat UAE listed-company ISSB status as unverified. ([source](https://github.com/Mdbush/Mdbush/blob/HEAD/shop/app/blog/freelance-sustainability-consultant-rates-uae/page.tsx), 2026)
- **[medium]** Saudi Arabia: a policy tracker checked on 1 Aug 2026 lists the CMA/Tadawul sustainability disclosure regime as voluntary. Its entries are 'comply-or-explain' and 'recommends GRI/SASB-aligned disclosure'. The only mandatory item listed is the CMA green, social and sustainability-linked bond disclosure rules (2025), which apply to in-scope bond issuers only. The Saudi Exchange ESG Disclosure Guidelines PDF is at https://www.saudiexchange.sa/wps/wcm/connect/d0922cd6-e0bf-4554-ad4d-03f0c2eea5ad/ESG_Disclosure_Guidelines_EN.pdf (not opened). I found no evidence of a mandatory ISSB-based regime in Saudi Arabia as of Aug 2026. ([source](https://github.com/peternam35/eu-policy/blob/HEAD/index.html), 2026-08-01)
- **[medium]** Singapore tiered climate reporting: - STI constituents report ISSB-aligned climate disclosures from FY2025, with Scope 3 from FY2026. - Other listed issuers with market cap of S$1bn or more report from FY2028; the remaining listed issuers from FY2030. - Large non-listed companies report from FY2030. - Scope 3 stays voluntary outside the STI tier. The dataset cites https://www.sgx.com/regulation/sustainability-reporting (blocked, not opened). A separate EY-competitor dataset independently gives the same STI FY2025 and Scope 3 FY2026 dates and says others were deferred to FY2028-2030. ([source](https://github.com/Adigo10/CutCarbon/blob/HEAD/app/data/frameworks.json), 2026)
- **[medium]** Malaysia's NSRF uses IFRS S1/S2 with climate-first reporting: - Group 1 (Main Market, market cap of RM2bn or more) from FY2025. - Group 2 (other Main Market issuers) from FY2026. - Group 3 (ACE Market plus non-listed companies with revenue of RM2bn or more) from FY2027. - Full IFRS S1/S2 from 2027, 2028 and 2030 respectively. Two independent secondary sources agree. Terrascope published a dedicated explainer on 11 Jun 2026. The dataset cites Bursa's 23 Dec 2024 media release and the IFRS jurisdiction profile (neither opened). ([source](https://github.com/duncanhill1/climate-disclosure-map/blob/HEAD/data/disclosures.from-excel.json), 2026-06)
- **[medium]** Malaysia's Simplified ESG Disclosure Guide (SEDG) for SMEs is voluntary. Version 2 aligns with the ASEAN Simplified ESG Disclosure Guide launched by the ASEAN Capital Markets Forum in April 2025. This is a ready-made supplier-data template that listed buyers push down to suppliers for Scope 3. ([source](https://github.com/infometa/workbuddyskills/blob/HEAD/experts/malaysia-finance-tax/Reference_Texts/esg_sustainability_guide.txt), 2026-07)
- **[medium]** Indonesia: DSK IAI's PSPK 1 and PSPK 2 (IFRS S1/S2-aligned) take effect 1 Jan 2027 (Terrascope, 16 Jun 2026). OJK's draft ISSB-aligned mandate, an amendment to POJK 51/2017, ran a public consultation to 13 Mar 2026. As of Jul 2026 OJK was still drafting, aiming to publish before end-2026 with a 1 Jan 2027 effective date. Terrascope describes a phase-in from FY2027 to FY2029. ([source](https://github.com/peternam35/eu-policy/blob/HEAD/index.html), 2026-07 to 2026-09)
- **[medium]** Sri Lanka: SLFRS S1/S2 (localised IFRS S1/S2) could be used voluntarily from 1 Jan 2024. They became mandatory from 1 Jan 2025 for the top 100 CSE-listed companies and broaden in 2026-2027. In July 2026 the Prime Minister referred to 'mandatory sustainability disclosure standards under SLFRS S1 and SLFRS S2 for leading listed companies', and CA Sri Lanka published a Preparers' Guide to SLFRS S1 & S2. ([source](https://github.com/nuuuwan/lk_news_digest/blob/HEAD/data/articles/article-2026-07-22-dailymirrorlk-a50c7c78.txt), 2026-07)
- **[low]** Bangladesh: a 5 Sep 2026 research note summarising FRC 2026 notifications says IFRS S1 and S2 are 'permitted', not mandated. A climate-disclosure map found only a Bangladesh Bank sustainability guideline for banks, with no jurisdiction-wide ISSB mandate. In practice, demand for supplier data comes from buyers, not from the domestic regulator. ([source](https://github.com/bemoshiur/TakaBooks/blob/HEAD/docs/research/bd-tax-research-2026-09-05.md), 2026-09-05)
- **[medium]** Vietnam: - Decree 06/2022/ND-CP set a carbon-market roadmap: pilot from 2025, full operation from 2028, with offsets capped at 10% of allowances. - Decision 01/2022/QD-TTg obliged 1,912 facilities to do GHG inventories. - Decision 232/QD-TTg (24 Jan 2025) approved the carbon-market scheme. - Decree 119/2025/ND-CP makes the Ministry of Agriculture and Environment responsible for the national registry and national emission-factor database. The updated obligated-facility list and pilot ETS allocation decrees could not be verified. ([source](https://github.com/khanhthanhdev/carbon_handbook/blob/HEAD/content/en/01.part-1-concepts-and-legal-documents-related-to-carbon-credits/05.vietnamese-legal-documents-related-to-the-carbon-market.md), 2025-2026)
- **[medium]** United Kingdom: UK SRS S1 and S2 were published 25 Feb 2026 and are voluntary today. The FCA proposes mandatory UK SRS S2 climate disclosure for about 515 listed companies in three listing categories, for periods beginning on or after 1 Jan 2027 (first reports in 2028), with comply-or-explain for Scope 3. Requirements for private companies are subject to a separate government consultation. This matters for UK subsidiaries of Indian groups, which today fall under SECR and the Companies Act climate disclosure rules if large. ([source](https://www.terrascope.com/blog/uk-climate-disclosure-rules-what-you-need-to-know), 2026-09-16)
- **[medium]** UK CBAM starts 1 Jan 2027 and covers aluminium, cement, fertilisers, hydrogen, and iron & steel; glass and ceramics are excluded from the initial scope, per the UK government consultation response. Source: an EU Climate Advisory Board dataset marked 'AI-compiled, pending verification' that cites https://www.gov.uk/government/consultations/introduction-of-a-uk-carbon-border-adjustment-mechanism-from-january-2027 (blocked, not opened). Whether the primary legislation has passed could not be verified. ([source](https://github.com/EUClimateAdvisoryBoard/ESABCCMethodHub/blob/HEAD/beta/modules/cbam-leakage-watch/data.generated.ts), compiled 2026-08)
- **[high]** The India-UK CETA entered into force on 15 Jul 2026 (signed 24 Jul 2025). It gives zero-duty access on about 99% of tariff lines and removes UK tariffs of up to 12% on textiles and clothing and up to 18% on engineering goods. Separately, new UK steel measures on 188 tariff lines took effect 1 Jul 2026, covering about 14% (USD 137m) of India's steel exports to the UK. On 26 Jun 2026 the government announced it would deploy 1,000 advisory personnel to help businesses use the CETA (PRID 2278276). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2284878&reg=48&lang=1), 2026-07-15)
- **[high]** The India-EU FTA was concluded at the India-EU Summit on 27 Jan 2026. It is not yet in force: on 22 Sep 2026 India was still seeking the European Parliament's 'early signing and ratification' (PRID 2313468). CBAM cooperation was on the agenda of the Commerce Minister's July 2026 EU visit (PRID 2286360). PIB lists the FTA as 'awaiting implementation', with preferential treatment across 97% of EU tariff lines (PRID 2300733). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2235725&reg=48&lang=1), 2026-09-22)
- **[high]** The Department of Commerce, with NABCB and EEPC, ran an EU CBAM awareness session for about 100 exporters on 18 Aug 2026. It covered applicability, embedded-emissions calculation, data collection and accreditation/verification, which shows government-recognised demand for practical verification-ready CBAM guidance. The India-EFTA TEPA has been in force since 1 Oct 2025 (PRID 2254367). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2301032&reg=48&lang=1), 2026-08-19)
- **[medium]** CSRD after Omnibus I: Directive (EU) 2026/470 was published in the OJ on 26 Feb 2026 and has been in force since 18 Mar 2026. - Scope: undertakings and groups above EUR 450m net turnover and an average of 1,000 employees. - Non-EU groups: in scope if EU turnover exceeds EUR 450m (previously EUR 150m) and an EU subsidiary or branch exceeds EUR 200m, for financial years beginning on or after 1 Jan 2028. - Value-chain cap: 'protected undertakings' with 1,000 or fewer employees may decline requests beyond the voluntary standard. - Transposition deadline: 19 Mar 2027. Read from an article based on the directive text; EUR-Lex (https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dir/2026/470/oj) was blocked. ([source](https://syntropyearth.com/thinking/csrd-omnibus-non-eu-parent-health-disclosure/), 2026-09-11)
- **[medium]** The Commission adopted the revised, simplified ESRS as delegated acts on 3 Jul 2026, with about 61% fewer mandatory datapoints, applying from FY2027 and in Parliament/Council scrutiny at the time. The voluntary VSME standard was adopted the same day; it is the reference for what suppliers outside CSRD scope can be asked for. ([source](https://github.com/Sushegaad/Claude-Skills-Governance-Risk-and-Compliance/blob/HEAD/plugins/csrd/skills/csrd/SKILL.md), 2026-08)
- **[low]** CSDDD thresholds after Omnibus conflict across the secondary sources I found. One says more than 5,000 employees and EUR 1.5bn turnover, with application 26 Jul 2029. Another says more than 1,000 employees and EUR 450m. This was not verified, and no CSDDD figure should be published without checking EUR-Lex. ([source](https://github.com/victorsole/brubru/blob/HEAD/backend/knowledge_base/guides/csddd_corporate_sustainability_due_diligence.md), 2026)
- **[medium]** EU ESPR (Reg (EU) 2024/1781): from 19 Jul 2026, large companies (250+ staff and more than EUR 50m) may not destroy unsold apparel, clothing accessories and footwear. Medium-sized companies follow from 19 Jul 2030. Article 24 requires annual disclosure of discarded unsold products by category and reason, with a standard format under Implementing Reg (EU) 2026/2 that applies from 2 Mar 2027. This pushes traceability and product-data demands onto garment suppliers. ([source](https://github.com/Cleo-Labs-IA/cleo-maquettes-edge/blob/HEAD/pages/blog/espr-unsold-textiles-destruction-ban-en.html), 2026)
- **[medium]** Buyer pressure on garment and textile suppliers, as shown in brands' own 2024-25 reports: - Lululemon targets 25% renewable electricity among core Tier 1 and Tier 2 suppliers (more than 75% of production volume) for its SBTi Scope 3 target. - Puma reports that of 23 factories approached, 13 submitted data and only 6 could be validated because 7 lacked supporting documents. Supplier data with an evidence trail is the bottleneck. ([source](https://github.com/mandyjsun/green-claims-nlp/blob/HEAD/data/labeled/ambiguous_passages.jsonl), 2025)
- **[high]** India-UAE bilateral merchandise trade crossed US$100bn for the first time, reaching US$101.25bn in FY2025-26. Both governments target US$200bn by 2032. Cumulative UAE FDI into India was US$25.19bn (April 2000 to March 2025). India's textile exports grew 2.1% in FY2025-26 (PRID 2254367). ([source](https://pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2261611&reg=48&lang=1), 2026-05-16)
- **[medium]** Data hook I computed: a regex scan of a public GitHub corpus of 729 FY2024-25 BRSR text files found: - CBAM in 5 filings (0.7%), CSRD/ESRS in 10 (1.4%), ISSB/IFRS S1-S2 in 20 (2.7%), and UK SRS or SECR in 0. - 201 filings (28%) place 'subsidiar*' within 200 characters of a foreign geography: UAE 78, Singapore 82, UK 68, EU countries 60, Bangladesh/Sri Lanka/Vietnam 31, Saudi 8. - 73 filings (10%) mention UAE/Dubai/Abu Dhabi anywhere. - FY2023-24 (821 files): CBAM 6 (0.7%), CSRD 6 (0.7%), ISSB 15 (1.8%). The proximity test is crude and includes false positives, the corpus is not the full top-1,000, and the counts need manual validation before publication. ([source](https://github.com/praveen-kumar-inti/fictional-tribble), FY2024-25 filings (corpus commit Feb 2026))
- **[medium]** Competitor coverage of international markets. Terrascope, Singapore-based and acquired Feb 2026, runs a 'Climate Disclosure Rules: What You Need to Know' series: Hong Kong (5 Jun 2026), Malaysia and New Zealand (11 Jun), Australia and Indonesia (16 Jun), Switzerland (15 Sep) and UK (16 Sep). Persefoni covers Singapore (22 Dec 2025), Japan, Korea, Mexico, Canada and Australia, plus an EU CBAM explainer (30 Jan 2026). Neither feed (Terrascope 45 posts, Persefoni 95 posts) has a post on the UAE climate law, Saudi Arabia, Vietnam, Bangladesh, Sri Lanka or India's BRSR/CCTS. I checked only these mirrored feeds; Big 4 and law-firm content could not be checked. ([source](https://github.com/api-evangelist/terrascope), 2026-09)
- **[medium]** Ranking pages are moving to comparison and selection formats: Terrascope published 'Spreadsheet, consultancy, software or Terrascope' (28 Aug 2026) and 'The 10 best carbon management platforms for enterprise' (17 Sep 2026); Persefoni published several 'best ... software in 2026' listicles. Multi-jurisdiction comparison tables are the format competitors are betting on. ([source](https://github.com/api-evangelist/persefoni), 2026-09)

## 6. Brand recon

**Company name**

ESG Astraa (the site's SERP title and page titles use "Esgastraa"; body copy and blog use "ESG Astraa")

**Website**

https://esgastraa.com/

**What they do**

ESG Astraa sells ESG advisory and a SaaS platform together, aimed at Indian companies. I could not open esgastraa.com directly (see uncertainties), so everything below comes from search-engine snippets of the site.

1) ADVISORY. Mission line: "turn ESG complexity into strategic advantage through advisory, reporting, and platform expertise" (https://esgastraa.com/). Services sit in three groups:
- Strategy & Advisory: ESG Strategy & Transformation, BRSR & ESG Compliance, Carbon Advisory & Credits, ESG Risk & Governance, Sustainable Finance Advisory, ESG Data & Intelligence.
- Finance & Risk: ESG Financial Analysis, ESG Tax Advisory, Green Finance & Bonds, ESG-Linked Financing, ESG Due Diligence, Cyber Risk & ESG Privacy.
- Governance & Social: Governance & Board Advisory, ESG Policy Frameworks.
The engagement process is Assess, Strategise, Measure, Sustain (https://esgastraa.com/).

2) PLATFORM. The "Smart ESG Platform" claims:
- tracking of "1000+ ESG KPIs with real-time data ingestion and automated validation"
- modules: Audit Automation, Reporting Dashboard, Data Management, Compliance Tracker
- Scope 3 and supplier ESG with "automated onboarding"
- science-based target setting and decarbonisation roadmaps
- "automated disclosures for SASB, TCFD, ESRS, CSRD, and CDP in seconds"
- framework alignment to BRSR, GRI, SASB and the UN SDGs
- headline claim that it "cuts BRSR reporting time by 70%… 3 weeks in under a day"
(all from https://esgastraa.com/)

Sectors named on the site: Manufacturing, Energy & Power, Healthcare & Pharma, Infrastructure & Real Estate (https://esgastraa.com/).

3) PRODUCT UNDER BUILD (public GitHub mirror, https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool):
- Brief line: "Astraa does the work; the human approves it; the trail shows it."
- Three layers: a record layer (collection, lineage, approval, versioning, filing); in-app AI on Gemini that reads documents and drafts proposals with approve/edit/reject gates and confidence scores tied to sources; and an MCP bridge to Claude, ChatGPT or Copilot for scenario and board narrative.
- 55 screens, including a CBAM wizard, supplier portal, materiality survey and audit log; seed dataset "Novaferro", a manufacturer.
- Frameworks in the brief: BRSR, GHG inventories, CBAM, CCTS.
- Stated go-to-market rule: "Sell CBAM first."

The owner's private repo "astraa-build" (seen only through authenticated GitHub search, not public) has the description: "ESG Astraa — collect activity data once, serve BRSR, GHG inventories, CBAM and CCTS from one store."

**Positioning**

Messaging as indexed: a hybrid of advisory and platform that "turn[s] ESG complexity into strategic advantage through advisory, reporting, and platform expertise" across "compliance, climate action, governance, and measurable sustainability outcomes" (https://esgastraa.com/). Proof claim: "cuts BRSR reporting time by 70%" ("3 weeks in under a day"). Breadth claim: "1000+ ESG KPIs" and multi-framework disclosures (BRSR, GRI, SASB, SDGs, TCFD, ESRS/CSRD, CDP). Thought leadership leans on India's carbon market: the blog post "The Carbon Turning Point for Indian Business", 4 June 2026 (https://esgastraa.com/insights/blogs/2).

Product positioning from the build repos is sharper and differs from the site:
- "collect activity data once, serve BRSR, GHG inventories, CBAM and CCTS from one store" (private astraa-build repo description)
- AI that does the work under human approval with full data lineage: "Astraa does the work; the human approves it; the trail shows it" (https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool)
- CBAM as the wedge ("Sell CBAM first") because exporters feel the financial pain and hold budget

Gap (my inference): the website reads as a broad ESG consultancy with a generic platform. The product strategy is a narrow India-compliance data spine (BRSR + CCTS + CBAM) with AI and audit lineage. Few Indian competitors span all three Indian or India-relevant regimes in one ledger. The Sustainability Cloud, Oren and Climes each publish content on BRSR, CBAM and CCTS, so the differentiator would need to be "one activity-data store, human-approved AI, evidence lineage" rather than coverage alone.

Local repo note: singhpratham19/esgtools ("ESG Impact OS") covers GHG Scope 1-3, BRSR, CSRD/ESRS, GRI, SASB, TCFD/IFRS S2, CDP and Climatiq factors. A grep found no CBAM or CCTS references, so CBAM/CCTS capability lives in the newer repos, not this one.

**Seo observations**

The footprint is very small: the site is indexed, but it is visible only for brand searches.

1) Indexed URLs. A site:esgastraa.com search surfaced only three URLs: the homepage, /insights/blogs/2 and /case-studies/H&M.pdf. No About, team, pricing, solution or sector pages surfaced as separate results, so those sections are probably homepage sections or not indexed (inference).

2) Title tags. The homepage SERP title is just "Esgastraa", with no category keyword (BRSR, ESG software, carbon accounting, CBAM, CCTS). The brand is spelled two ways: "Esgastraa" in titles, "ESG Astraa" in copy. The blog URL showed up once as "Esgastraa" and once with its real headline, which suggests client-side or default meta titles (inference). The PDF's indexed title is "1 www.esgastraa.com Rewriting the Fashion Equation", meaning no document title metadata. The PDF filename contains an ampersand ("H&M.pdf"). Blog slugs are numeric (/insights/blogs/2), not keyword slugs.

3) Category rankings. ESG Astraa did not appear for any non-brand category search I ran:
- "best BRSR reporting software India 2026": The Sustainability Cloud, Oren, ZeroCarbon, Newtral, Breathe ESG, Sentra
- "ESG software India carbon accounting platform for manufacturers": KeyESG, Climes, Tracxn, TSC, Sentra, GroundESG, Bharat Carbon
- "CBAM compliance software for Indian exporters": GreenSutra, TSC, CbamTrack, Seasaw, o2log, Encarbonsys
- "CCTS compliance software India": Oren, Climate Decode, CarbonNeeti, Green Curve
- "Credibl ESG platform India BRSR": Credibl dominates; ESG Astraa absent
Competitors win these SERPs with comparison and listicle content ("Best CBAM Software in India 2026", "Carbon Accounting Software in India (2026): An Honest Comparison", "Sprih vs Breathe ESG").

4) The one non-brand hit. The CCTS blog post appeared, about eighth, for the timely news query "Indian Carbon Market portal launched 2026 CCTS trading start GHG emission intensity targets notified entities". It also appeared for "Astraa" + BRSR/CBAM/CCTS. Timely India-regulation news is the only proven organic entry point.

5) Off-site mentions and backlinks. Excluding the domain, the only search result citing "esgastraa.com" was the GitHub mirror. There is no Crunchbase, Tracxn, Zaubacorp, OneStopESG (which relaunched 28 Sep 2026 with 1,600+ companies) or ensun listing found, and no press or PR. I could not measure backlinks: no Ahrefs, Semrush or Search Console access, and no search volumes were measured.

6) Name collisions dilute the brand SERP:
- "ESG Astra" and "Astraa ESG" queries are dominated by Astra ESG Solutions (Grand View Research; astra.grandviewresearch.com, LinkedIn, PR Newswire)
- linkedin.com/company/astraa is an unrelated data-analytics firm (6,628 followers per snippet)
- astraa.co.uk ("astraa-ai") is an Edinburgh AI vision-security company
- Indonesian Astra group ESG pages also compete
- No indexed results found for esgastraa.in or esgastraa.ai. Registration status is unknown (WHOIS not checked).

7) Brand SERP hygiene risk. The public GitHub mirror ranks for "ESG Astraa" queries and exposes internal strategy ("Sell CBAM first", 15-22% price-concession framing, build package for Claude Code).

8) Accuracy check on the blog, based on a snippet only. The blog reportedly says CCTS covers "490 entities across nine hard-to-abate sectors". PIB says "nearly 490 obligated entities across seven energy-intensive sectors" (https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2243377&reg=3&lang=1), and secondary sources say eight or nine. Aligning with the PIB wording would protect credibility.

**Aeo observations**

An AI answer engine would very likely not mention or recommend ESG Astraa for any category question today.

1) Brand-only recognition. The search tool's AI summarizer could describe ESG Astraa only when the exact brand string was in the query ("ESG Astraa", "ESGAstraa", "Astraa" + ESG/BRSR/CCTS). For "ESG Astraa founder OR CEO" it said it "could not find any company specifically called ESG Astraa" and listed unrelated Astra companies. For "ESG Astraa LinkedIn" it returned Astra ESG Solutions (Grand View Research) and the unrelated Astraa analytics firm.

2) Absent from category answers. For "best BRSR reporting software India 2026", "ESG software India carbon accounting", "CBAM software for Indian exporters" and "CCTS compliance software", the AI summaries named TSC, Oren, Noa/Newtral, Ecodrisil, Breathe ESG, Climes, Sprih, Updapt, StepChange, Sentra, GroundESG, Bharat Carbon, Credibl, GreenSutra, CbamTrack, Climate Decode and CarbonNeeti. ESG Astraa did not appear in any of them.

3) Entity confusion. For "what is ESG Astraa reviews pricing demo" and "esgastraa.in OR esgastraa.ai", the AI summary described ESG Astraa as "a build package… to start building the product in Claude Code", taken from the GitHub mirror. Answer engines may currently describe the brand as a GitHub dev project rather than a company. Separately, one summary framed the H&M PDF as evidence of "their work with major global brands", a misattribution risk.

4) Missing entity signals. There is no indexed About or team page, no named founders, no LinkedIn company page, no Crunchbase, Tracxn or MCA-visible legal entity, no Wikipedia/Wikidata entry, no third-party reviews (G2, Capterra, SoftwareAdvice searches found nothing), and no directory listings. I could not verify Organization or FAQ schema because the site could not be fetched.

5) Self-check on model knowledge. My own training data (cutoff June 2026) contains no knowledge of ESG Astraa. This is an observation about one model and does not predict other engines.

6) Positive. The answer engine did extract and paraphrase the CCTS blog accurately: portal launch at Prakriti 2026 and MRV scope. Timely, fact-dense India-regulation explainers are extractable and could be cited.

7) Overall: low AEO visibility (my qualitative estimate). Proxies: absence from AI summaries on 6 category queries, presence only on exact-brand queries, and AI conflation with Astra-branded entities.

**Inferences and uncertainties**

ACCESS LIMITS (read first):
- The environment's network policy blocked WebFetch for esgastraa.com, web.archive.org, icapcarbonaction.com, esgtoday.com and taxation-customs.ec.europa.eu. To allow them, change Network access in the cloud environment's settings (environment menu in the session title bar, then Edit) and add these hosts or pick a broader access level.
- The Firecrawl MCP returned "insufficient credits"; more credits are needed to scrape.
- A direct curl attempt was denied by the permission system and was not retried.
- So every website fact above comes from WebSearch result snippets and AI-generated summaries of esgastraa.com, not first-hand page reads. Wording may be paraphrased, page structure is unknown, and testimonials, client names, pricing, CTAs, footer, address, social links and schema markup could NOT be verified.
- GitHub MCP access was limited to singhpratham19/esgtools. The public mirror was read through raw.githubusercontent.com and github.com via WebFetch. The private repo "astraa-build" was seen only as a search-result description; it is not public evidence.

FOUNDERS / TEAM: Not found publicly. All ESG Astraa repos (esgastraa-tool, esg-astraa-nextjs, astraa, astra, esgtools, private astraa-build) belong to GitHub account singhpratham19. I did not link that handle to any named person; searches for that link returned unrelated people.

UNVERIFIED CLAIMS:
- "1000+ ESG KPIs", "70% faster BRSR (3 weeks to under a day)" and automated SASB/TCFD/ESRS/CSRD/CDP disclosures "in seconds" are marketing claims with no evidence found.
- My inference: these go beyond what the esgtools repo implements (no CBAM/CCTS; seeded demo data). They may be aspirational or rely on the newer repos.
- Whether a separate testimonial name exists could not be verified.

INFERENCES (labelled):
(a) The business is early stage and pre-scale or pre-launch. Basis: three indexed URLs, no third-party mentions, no social presence, product still being built in public and private repos with seed datasets (Novaferro in the mirror; the esgtools demo is an Indian industrial manufacturer with Mumbai HQ and Pune, Nashik and Chennai plants).
(b) Advisory probably funds or opens doors for the platform (a hybrid like Credibl).
(c) The strategic wedge has shifted from BRSR (site) to CBAM plus CCTS (brief: "Sell CBAM first"). The website does not yet reflect that.

REGULATORY FACTS (verified as of 2026-10-01 via search snippets of primary sources; full pages not readable):
- CBAM definitive period since 1 Jan 2026; 50-tonne annual threshold; certificate sales from 1 Feb 2027; first declaration and surrender by 30 Sep 2027 for 2026 imports (EC Q&A and news). The 50 t threshold's legal basis, Reg (EU) 2025/2083, comes from secondary sources (https://cbamguide.com/compliance/de-minimis/).
- Indian Carbon Market Portal launched 21 Mar 2026 at Prakriti 2026 (PIB).
- GEI targets: 282 entities in four sectors (Oct 2025) plus 208 entities on 13 Jan 2026, giving 490 (PIB). The sector count is inconsistent: PIB says seven, secondary sources say eight or nine; some secondary sources also list iron & steel, which I could not confirm.
- NOT verified: whether CCC trading on power exchanges has actually started (a secondary source said it was expected around Oct 2026: https://www.saurenergy.com/solar-energy-news/indias-carbon-market-edges-toward-its-first-trade-12482615), and the CERC March 2026 trading regulations (secondary only).
- BRSR: top 1,000 listed entities; BRSR Core "assessment or assurance" per the SEBI circular of 28 Mar 2025; top 500 in FY2025-26 and top 1,000 in FY2026-27 (secondary sources for the phase-in); value-chain disclosures voluntary. A pending SEBI consultation on further BRSR changes was mentioned by IEEFA/ORF; its final status was not verified.

NO SEARCH VOLUMES OR BACKLINK COUNTS were measured; none are claimed.

**Target customers**

- Stated on the site: companies doing BRSR and ESG compliance in India (https://esgastraa.com/). SEBI requires BRSR from the top 1,000 listed companies by market cap. BRSR Core assessment or assurance applies to the top 500 from FY2025-26 and the top 1,000 from FY2026-27 (SEBI circular of 28 Mar 2025, referenced at https://www.sebi.gov.in/sebi_data/faqfiles/apr-2025/1745399101865.pdf; phase-in per https://thefootnotes.in/articles/brsr-core-assurance-fy2026-27-checklist and https://www.orfonline.org/research/enhancing-the-interoperability-of-india-s-business-sustainability-reporting-with-international-standards)
- Stated on the site: Manufacturing, Energy & Power (power generation and utilities), Healthcare & Pharma, Infrastructure & Real Estate (https://esgastraa.com/)
- Stated in the product brief: Indian manufacturers, exporters and listed mid-market companies (https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool)
- Inferred from the brief's 'Sell CBAM first' and its CBAM wizard: Indian exporters of CBAM goods (steel, aluminium, cement and so on) to the EU. CBAM's definitive period started 1 Jan 2026, and certificate sales start 1 Feb 2027 (https://taxation-customs.ec.europa.eu/news/reminder-cbam-goes-live-1-january-2026-2025-12-23_en, https://taxation-customs.ec.europa.eu/document/download/013fa763-5dce-4726-a204-69fec04d5ce2_en?filename=CBAM_Questions+and+Answers.pdf)
- Inferred from the CCTS blog post and the brief: the 490 CCTS obligated entities with notified GHG emission intensity targets (282 entities in aluminium, cement, chlor-alkali and pulp & paper notified Oct 2025; 208 in refineries, petrochemicals, textiles and secondary aluminium notified 13 Jan 2026) (https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2217239&reg=3&lang=2)
- Inferred buyer personas: Head of Sustainability/ESG, CFO or finance controller, Company Secretary or compliance lead (who owns BRSR in the annual report), and export or commercial heads (who face CBAM price pressure; the brief cites 15-22% price concessions) (https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool)
- Inferred from the Scope 3 'automated onboarding' module and the supplier portal: suppliers in large companies' value chains

**Geographies**

- India: primary market. The site, blog and brief are all India-focused (https://esgastraa.com/, https://esgastraa.com/insights/blogs/2, https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool)
- European Union: regulatory destination for Indian exporters through CBAM, and the site claims ESRS/CSRD disclosures (https://esgastraa.com/). This is inferred as a secondary market; there is no evidence of EU customers.
- No office address, city or legal entity could be found online. Searches on Zaubacorp, Crunchbase and Tracxn returned nothing for ESG Astraa or Esgastraa (https://www.zaubacorp.com/ESG-SOLUTIONS-PRIVATE-LIMITED-U40108GJ2022PTC135958 is an unrelated company).

**Existing content**

- Homepage with services, sectors, process, platform features and an unnamed testimonial-style claim ('cuts BRSR reporting time by 70%') (https://esgastraa.com/)
- Insights / blog section, described as 'strategic ESG perspectives, compliance updates, and practical advisory thinking from the ESG Astraa team'. Only one post was found indexed: 'The Carbon Turning Point for Indian Business: The Indian Carbon Market Is No Longer a Future Event', dated 4 June 2026, covering the Indian Carbon Market Portal launch at Prakriti 2026 and CCTS (https://esgastraa.com/insights/blogs/2). The numeric slug /2 implies at least one other post, which was not found indexed.
- Case-study PDF 'Rewriting the Fashion Equation' about H&M Group, based on H&M's public data (FY2024 Scope 1+2 down 41%, 96% renewable electricity, etc.). It reads as a desk-research case, not a client engagement (https://esgastraa.com/case-studies/H&M.pdf)
- No white papers, webinars, YouTube channel, podcast, newsletter, LinkedIn company page, Instagram, X, Medium or Substack presence found (searches for each returned nothing attributable to ESG Astraa)
- Public GitHub mirror with the full product brief, design laws, 55-screen map, AI spec and build order. It is indexed and appears in brand searches (https://github.com/singhpratham19/esgastraa-tool)
- Other public repos under the same GitHub owner that appear related: esg-astraa-nextjs, astraa ('esg. tool'), astra ('esgtool'), esg ('esg website'), esgtools ('esg software env') (https://github.com/singhpratham19)

**Competitors**

- Credibl: AI ESG data platform plus ESG advisory, BRSR software, AI assurance readiness; closest hybrid model (https://www.crediblesg.com/, https://www.crediblesg.com/esg-advisory/)
- The Sustainability Cloud: BRSR, BRSR Core and CBAM software; publishes 'best BRSR' and 'best CBAM software in India 2026' listicles that rank (https://www.thesustainabilitycloud.com/blog/top-5-brsr-softwares/, https://www.thesustainabilitycloud.com/blog/best-cbam-softwares-in-india-2026/)
- Oren (Oren Sustainability Hub): BRSR buyer's guide, XBRL, CCTS content (https://www.orennow.com/blog/best-software-for-brsr-reporting-comprehensive-guide-for-sustainability-reporting-in-india, https://www.orennow.com/blog/carbon-credits)
- Breathe ESG: Bengaluru BRSR/ESG suite (https://www.breatheesg.com/resources/breathe-esg-vs-sprih)
- Sprih: India carbon and ESG platform (http://www.sprih.com/blogs/sebis-value-chain-relaxation-prioritizing-lasting-impact-over-ease-of-compliance/)
- Updapt: India ESG suite (https://tracxn.com/d/companies/updapt/__Sew9g-JfjG77yovhqHopBIDAVOTugfAeVCQV5nTTwhg)
- Climes (Carbon-OS): India carbon accounting and CCTS; publishes an 'honest comparison' page (https://www.climes.io/carbon-accounting-software-india, https://www.climes.io/ccts-reporting-india)
- Newtral / Noa: BRSR drafting with BRSR Core evidence (https://newtral.io/frameworks/brsr)
- StepChange: named in an India carbon software comparison (https://www.climes.io/carbon-accounting-software-india)
- Sentra.world: carbon accounting for industrial manufacturing, BRSR (https://sentra.world/)
- GroundESG (Sparrow): https://infinity.sparrowrms.in/sustainability/groundesg/
- Bharat Carbon: CCTS target-setting and carbon accounting (https://www.bharatcarbon.earth/)
- Zoei ESG: https://zoeiesg.com/carbon-accounting-software-in-india/
- Ecodrisil ESG Xpress: BRSR (listed at https://www.passionateinmarketing.com/leading-brsr-reporting-platforms-for-indian-companies-in-2025/)
- CBAM specialists for Indian exporters: GreenSutra (https://greensutra.in/services/cbam-solutions/), CbamTrack (https://cbamtrack.com/), Encarbonsys (https://encarbonsys.com/cbam-blog/cbam-india-exporters-guide-2026.html), Seasaw (https://seasaw.ai/resources/cbam-india-exporters/), o2log (https://o2log.com/services/cbam-reporting.html)
- CCTS/MRV tools: Climate Decode TerraNova (https://climate-decode.com/markets/india-ccts), CarbonNeeti (https://carbonneeti.com/blog/ccts-india-2025-2026-complete-guide), Green Curve (https://greencurve.solutions/ccts)
- Global platforms benchmarked in the repo README: Watershed, Persefoni, Greenly (https://watershed.com/platform/measure, https://www.persefoni.com/business/carbon-footprint-measurement-analytics, https://greenly.earth/en-us/products/carbon-footprint); also SAP Green Ledger in the CBAM context (https://www.savictech.com/insights/eu-cbam-india-exporters-sap-sustainability-2026/)
- Consultancies competing for the advisory side: PwC India (https://www.pwc.in/consulting/esg-consulting.html), Grant Thornton Bharat (https://www.grantthornton.in/services/esg-risk-consulting/esg-consulting-services/), KPMG India, SES (https://www.sesgovernance.com/brsr-guide), Astrum (https://www.astrum.in/what-we-do/esg-consulting-services/), Consultivo (https://consultivo.in/social-sustainability/esg-environmental-social-governance/)
- Name collision, not a functional competitor, but it competes for the brand SERP: Astra ESG Solutions by Grand View Research, an ESG scores/data/consulting business with LinkedIn and PR Newswire presence (https://astra.grandviewresearch.com/, https://in.linkedin.com/company/astra-esg-solutions)
